w\in W
and some
t\in T(E)
. Note that
t
gives a well-defined class in
T/(1-w)T
, and thus
\simc(t)
is independent of the choice of
t
. By the construction of
\tau
, we must have
w=\phi(s_{\tau})
. The cuspidality of
\sigma(\tau)
implies
w\in\left\{s_{1}s_{2},s_{2}s_{1},w_{0}\right\}
. Then
\psi
is TRSELP (tame regular semisimple elliptic
L
-parameter) in the sense of
[
DR09
, §4.1]
.

For any TRSELP
\psi
, DeBacker–Reeder constructed the
L
-packet of depth-zero supercuspidal representations associated with it. These representations are distributed among the pure inner forms of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
. The pure inner forms are parameterized by Galois cohomology
H^{1}(K,\mathrm{GSp}_{4})
. Since
H^{1}(K,\mathrm{Sp}_{4})=1
(because
\mathrm{Sp}_{4}
is simply-connected) and
H^{1}(K,\mathbf{G}_{m})=1
, we have
H^{1}(K,\mathrm{GSp}_{4})=1
. Thus, all these representations are of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
.

Now we explain the construction in
[
DR09
, §4]
in a special case. The
L
-packet of
\psi
, denoted by
\Pi(\psi)
, is parameterized by
\mathrm{Irr}(C_{\psi})
the (finite) set of irreducible representations of
C_{\psi}=\pi_{0}(Z_{G}(\imaginary\psi))
.
One can check that
C_{\psi}
is trivial when
s_{\tau}=s_{1}s_{2}
or
s_{2}s_{1}
and is isomorphic to
\mathbf{Z}/2\mathbf{Z}
when
s_{\tau}=w_{0}
. In both cases, we simply take the trivial representation of
C_{\psi}
. Then the corresponding element in
\Pi(\psi)
is given by

\displaystyle\ind^{G(K)}_{G({\mathcal{O}}_{K})K^{\times}}\widetilde{R}_{\mathbb{T}}^{\theta}

where
(\mathbb{T},\theta)=(T_{s_{\tau}},\theta_{s_{\tau},{\mathbf{a}}^{(0)}})
,
\widetilde{R}_{\mathbb{T}}^{\theta}|_{G({\mathcal{O}}_{K})}\simeq\epsilon_{G}\epsilon_{\mathbb{T}}{R}_{\mathbb{T}}^{\theta}
, and
\widetilde{R}_{\mathbb{T}}^{\theta}|_{K^{\times}}
sends
p
to
\simc(t)
.

Proof of Theorem
2.4.1
in the cuspidal case.

We know that
\sigma(\tau)\subset\pi|_{G({\mathcal{O}}_{K})}
implies that
\pi\simeq\ind_{G({\mathcal{O}}_{K})K^{\times}}^{G(K)}(\widetilde{\sigma}(\tau))
for some
\widetilde{\sigma}(\tau)
extending
\sigma(\tau)
. Let
\omega_{\pi}
be the central character of
\pi
. By the above construction,
\pi
is contained in the
L
-packet of
\psi
such that
\psi|_{I_{K}}\simeq\tau
and
\simc(\psi)(\Frob_{K})=\omega_{\pi}(p)
. The multiplicity one assertion follows from

\displaystyle\Hom_{G({\mathcal{O}}_{K})}(\sigma(\tau),\pi|_{G({\mathcal{O}}_{K})})\simeq\Hom_{G({\mathcal{O}}_{K})K^{\times}}(\widetilde{\sigma}(\tau),\pi|_{G({\mathcal{O}}_{K})K^{\times}})\simeq\Hom_{G(K)}(\pi,\pi)

where the first isomorphism exists because
\pi
admits a central character, and the second isomorphism follows from the Frobenius reciprocity for compact inductions.
∎

2.4.6.
Serre weights of a tame inertial
L
-parameter

We define the conjectural set of Serre weights associated to a tame inertial
L
-parameter following
[
GHS18
, Definition 9.2.5]
.

Definition 2.4.7
.

Let
\overline{\rho}
be a tame inertial
L
-parameter over
\mathbf{F}
. We define
W^{?}(\overline{\rho})
to be the set
{\mathcal{R}}(\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}([\overline{\rho}])))
.

Definition 2.4.8
.

Let
\overline{\rho}\simeq\overline{\tau}(s,\mu)
be a 6-generic tame inertial
L
-parameter over
\mathbf{F}
. We define a function

\displaystyle F_{\overline{\rho}}:\underline{W}

\displaystyle\rightarrow W^{?}(\overline{\rho})

\displaystyle w

\displaystyle\mapsto{\mathcal{R}}(F_{\sigma([\overline{\rho}])}(w)).

We define
W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{\rho})
to be the image of
F_{\overline{\rho}}
and call its elements as
obvious weights
of
\overline{\rho}
.

Note that
W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{\rho})
does not depend on the choice of the lowest alcove presentation of
\overline{\rho}
and coincides with
W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{\rho})
defined in
[
LLHLMa
, Definition 2.6.3]
. In
loc. cit.
,
F_{\overline{\rho}}(w)
is called as the obvious weight of
\overline{\rho}
corresponding to
w
.

2.4.9.
Transfer to
\mathrm{GL}_{4}

Recall that we have a map
{\mathcal{T}}
defined in §
2.1
which maps
\underline{W}
to
\underline{W}_{4}
and
X^{*}(\underline{T})
to
X^{*}(\underline{T}_{4})
.
Using this, we define the
transfer
of Deligne–Lusztig representations and Serre weights of
G_{0}(\mathbf{F}_{p})
to
(\mathrm{GL}_{4})_{0}(\mathbf{F}_{p})
.

Proposition 2.4.10
.

The map
{\mathcal{T}}
induces a well-defined assignment from Deligne–Lusztig representations (resp. Serre weights) of
G_{0}(\mathbf{F}_{p})
to the Deligne–Lusztig representations (resp. Serre weights) of
(\mathrm{GL}_{4})_{0}(\mathbf{F}_{p})
given by

\displaystyle R_{s}(\mu)

\displaystyle\mapsto{\mathcal{T}}(R_{s}(\mu)):=R_{{\mathcal{T}}(s)}({\mathcal{T}}(\mu))

\displaystyle F(\lambda)

\displaystyle\mapsto{\mathcal{T}}(F(\lambda)):=F({\mathcal{T}}(\lambda)).

Suppose that
\mu-\eta\in\underline{C}_{0}
is
6
-deep. Then
F(\lambda)\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{R_{s}(\mu)})
implies
{\mathcal{T}}(F(\lambda))\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{{\mathcal{T}}(R_{s}(\mu))})
. The converse is true if furthermore
\mu-\eta=(a_{j},b_{j};c_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
and
\absolutevalue{a_j - p/2}>3/2
for each
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
.

Proof.

The first claim for Deligne–Lusztig representations follows from the fact that the map
{\mathcal{T}}
respects the Weyl group action on the character lattice and
p
-dot actions. For Serre weights, it follows from that
{\mathcal{T}}
maps
X^{*}_{1}(\underline{T})
and
X^{0}(\underline{T})
into
X^{*}_{1}(\underline{T}_{4})
and
X^{0}(\underline{T}_{4})
respectively.

Suppose that
F(\lambda)\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{R_{s}(\mu)})
. By Proposition
2.2.6
, there exists
w\in\underline{W}
and
\widetilde{w}\in\underline{\widetilde{W}}_{1}
such that
\widehat{w}\uparrow\widetilde{w}
and

\displaystyle F(\lambda)\simeq F\left(\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\widetilde{w}\cdot(\mu-\eta+s\pi(\widehat{w}^{-1}(0)))\right)\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{R_{s}(\mu)}).

Let
{\mathcal{T}}(\widetilde{w}_{h})=\widetilde{w}_{h}^{\prime}
. By applying
{\mathcal{T}}
to the above equation, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}(F(\lambda))\simeq F\left(\widetilde{w}_{h}^{\prime{-1}}{\mathcal{T}}(\widetilde{w})\cdot({\mathcal{T}}(\mu)-\eta^{\prime}+{\mathcal{T}}(s\pi(\widehat{w}^{-1}(0))))\right).

Since two ordering
\leq
and
\uparrow
coincide on
\underline{\widetilde{W}}^{+}
, we have
{\mathcal{T}}(\widehat{w})\uparrow{\mathcal{T}}(\widetilde{w})
by Lemma
2.1.6
. Then the above equality implies
{\mathcal{T}}(F(\lambda))\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{{\mathcal{T}}(R_{s}(\mu))})
by Proposition
2.2.6
.

For the converse, suppose
{\mathcal{T}}(F(\lambda))\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{{\mathcal{T}}(R_{s}(\mu))})
. Then there exists
w^{\prime}\in\underline{W}_{4}
and
\widetilde{w}^{\prime}\in\underline{\widetilde{W}}_{1}
such that
\widehat{w}^{\prime}\uparrow\widetilde{w}^{\prime}
and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}(F(\lambda))\simeq F\left(\widetilde{w}_{h}^{\prime{-1}}\widetilde{w}^{\prime}\cdot({\mathcal{T}}(\mu)-\eta^{\prime}+{\mathcal{T}}(s)\pi(\widehat{w}^{\prime{-1}}(0)))\right).

This shows that
\widetilde{w}_{h}^{\prime{-1}}\widetilde{w}^{\prime}\cdot({\mathcal{T}}(\mu)-\eta^{\prime}+{\mathcal{T}}(s)\pi(\widehat{w}^{\prime{-1}}(0)))
is fixed by
\Theta
(defined in §
2.1
). Suppose that
\widetilde{w}^{\prime}
is fixed by
\Theta
. Then
\widehat{w}^{\prime{-1}}(0)
is fixed by
\Theta
, and simple computation shows that
\widehat{w}^{\prime}
is fixed by
\Theta
as well. Thus
\widetilde{w}^{\prime}={\mathcal{T}}(\widetilde{w})
and
\widehat{w}^{\prime}={\mathcal{T}}(\widehat{w})
for some
\widetilde{w}\in\underline{\widetilde{W}}_{1}
and
w\in\underline{W}
. As in the previous paragraph, this shows that
F(\lambda)\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{R_{s}(\mu)})
.

We finish the proof by showing that
\widetilde{w}^{\prime}
is fixed by
\Theta
. Let
\widetilde{w}_{0}\in\underline{\widetilde{W}}
be an element such that
\lambda\in\widetilde{w}_{0}\cdot\underline{C}_{0}
. Then we can write
\widetilde{w}^{\prime}={\mathcal{T}}(\widetilde{w}_{0})\delta^{\prime}
for some
\delta^{\prime}\in\underline{\Omega}_{4}
. Suppose that
\delta^{\prime}
is not fixed by
\Theta
. Since
\Omega_{4}/{\mathcal{T}}(\Omega)
is cyclic group of order 2, we can assume that
\delta^{\prime}_{j}
is the generator of
\Omega_{4}
sending
(a,b,c,d)
to
(b,c,d,a-p)
for at least one
j\in{\mathcal{J}}
. Let us write
\mu-\eta=(a_{j},b_{j};c_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
and
{\mathcal{T}}(s)\pi(\widehat{w}^{\prime{-1}})=(x_{j},y_{j},z_{j},w_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
. Then

\displaystyle\left(\delta^{\prime}\cdot({\mathcal{T}}(\mu-\eta)+{\mathcal{T}}(s)\pi(\widehat{w}^{\prime{-1}}))\right)_{j}

\displaystyle=\delta^{\prime}_{j}\cdot(a_{j}+b_{j}+c_{j}+x_{j},a_{j}+c_{j}+y_{j},b_{j}+c_{j}+z_{j},c_{j}+w_{j})

\displaystyle=(a_{j}+c_{j}+y_{j},b_{j}+c_{j}+z_{j},c_{j}+w_{j},a_{j}+b_{j}+c_{j}+x_{j}-p).

Since
\delta^{\prime}\cdot({\mathcal{T}}(\mu-\eta)+{\mathcal{T}}(s)\pi(\widehat{w}^{\prime{-1}}))
is fixed by
\Theta
, we have
2a_{j}-p=z_{j}+w_{j}-y_{j}-x_{j}
. However,
\absolutevalue{z_j+w_j-y_j-x_j}\leq 3
by
[
LLHL19
, Remark 4.1.4]
. This leads to a contradiction and
\delta^{\prime}
is fixed by
\Theta
.
∎

Example 2.4.11
.

The condition
\absolutevalue{a_j-p/2}>3/2
in the above Lemma may seem dubious but it is necessary. For example, let
K=\mathbf{Q}_{p}
and
(s,\mu-\eta)\in W\times X^{*}(T)
be a lowest alcove presentation of
R_{s}(\mu)
where
s=e
,
\mu-\eta=(a,b;c)
. We take
a=(p-1)/2
. Let
w^{\prime}\in W_{4}
be the element sending
(x,y,z,w)
to
(y,z,w,x)
. Then
\widehat{w}^{\prime}=w^{\prime}t_{-\nu}
where
\nu=(1,0,0,0)
. Let

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{w}_{h}^{\prime{-1}}\widehat{w}\cdot({\mathcal{T}}(\mu)-\eta^{\prime}+\nu)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{w}_{h}^{\prime{-1}}\cdot{\mathcal{T}}(a,a-b;b+c-a)

so that
F(\lambda^{\prime})\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{{\mathcal{T}}(R_{e}(\mu))})
. However, one can easily check that

\displaystyle F(\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\cdot(a,a-b;b+c-a))\notin\mathrm{JH}(\overline{R_{e}(\mu)}).

Corollary 2.4.12
.

Let
\overline{\rho}\simeq\tau(s,\mu)
be a 6-generic tame inertial
L
-parameter. Recall the set
W^{?}(\std(\overline{\rho}))
defined in
[
LLHLMa
, Definition 2.6.1]
Suppose that
\mu-\eta=(a_{j},b_{j};c_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
and
\absolutevalue{a_j-p/2}>3/2
for each
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
. Then

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}(W^{?}(\overline{\rho}))=W^{?}(\std(\overline{\rho}))\cap\{{\mathcal{T}}(F(\lambda))\mid\lambda\in X_{1}^{*}(\underline{T})\}.

The following Corollary shows the compatibility between the inertial local Langlands correspondence and the transfer of Deligne–Lusztig representations.

Corollary 2.4.13
.

Let
\pi
be a smooth irreducible
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-representation of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(K)
and
\psi=\mathrm{rec}_{\mathrm{GT},p}(\pi)
. Let
\Pi
be a smooth irreducible
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{4}(K)
corresponding to
\std(\psi)
under the local Langlands correspondence of
[
HT01
]
(conjugated by
\iota
). Let
\tau
be a 1-generic tame inertial
L
-parameter. If
\sigma(\tau)\subset\pi|_{\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})}
, then
{\mathcal{T}}(\sigma(\tau))\subset\Pi|_{\mathrm{GL}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
2.4.1
, we have
\psi|_{I_{K}}\simeq\tau
. Then the claim follows from the construction of
{\mathcal{T}}(\sigma(\tau))
and
[
Sho18
, Theorem 3.7 (2)]
. (Note that although the local Langlands correspondence used in
loc. cit.
is normalized, it only differs by unramified twist.)
∎

Remark 2.4.14
.

The converse of the above Corollary is not true, as the
L
-packet of
\psi
can have two elements (e.g. if
\psi|_{I_{K}}\simeq\tau(s,\mu)
and
s_{\tau}=w_{0}
).

2.5.
Combinatorics between types and weights

Let
G
be either
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
or
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
. We first introduce some notations and definitions.

Notation 2.5.1
.

Let
(s,\mu)
be a lowest alcove presentation of a Deligne–Lusztig representation
R
(resp. a tame inerital
L
-parameter
\tau
). We let
\widetilde{w}(R)
(resp.
\widetilde{w}(\tau)
) denote
t_{\mu+\eta}s\in\underline{\widetilde{W}}
.
If
\overline{\rho}
is another tame inertial
L
-parameter with a lowest alcove presentation
(s^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
, we write

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau):=\widetilde{w}(\tau)^{-1}\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})\in\underline{\widetilde{W}}.

Definition 2.5.2
.

We call an element of
X^{*}(\underline{Z})
as an
algebraic central character
and an element of
X^{*}(\underline{Z})/(p-\pi)X^{*}(\underline{Z})
as a
central character
.

Note that
X^{*}(\underline{Z})/(p-\pi)X^{*}(\underline{Z})\simeq\Hom(Z({\mathcal{O}}_{p}/p),\mathbf{F}^{\times})
which justifies the term central character.

Recall that we have an isomorphism

(2.5.3)

\displaystyle\underline{\Omega}\simeq\underline{\widetilde{W}}/\underline{W}_{a}\simeq X^{*}(\underline{Z}).

If
\sigma
is a Serre weight with a lowest alcove presentation
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\omega)
, then the central character of
\sigma
can be described as the image of
t_{\omega-\eta}\widetilde{w}_{1}
under (
2.5.3
) composed with
X^{*}(\underline{Z})\twoheadrightarrow X^{*}(\underline{Z})/(p-\pi)X^{*}(\underline{Z})
. Similarly, if
R
is a Deligne–Lusztig representation with a lowest alcove presentation
(s,\mu)
, then the central character of
R
is given by the lift of the image of
\widetilde{w}(R)
under (
2.5.3
) composed with
X^{*}(\underline{Z})\twoheadrightarrow X^{*}(\underline{Z})/(p-\pi)X^{*}(\underline{Z})
.

For a dominant character
\lambda\in X^{*}(\underline{T})
, let
W(\lambda)_{/{\mathcal{O}}}
be the unique up to isomorphism irreducible algebraic
\underline{G}_{/{\mathcal{O}}}
-representation of highest weight
\lambda
. Let
V(\lambda)
be the restriction of
W(\lambda)_{/{\mathcal{O}}}
to
G_{0}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
.
We define a
type
(of
G_{0}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
) to be a pair
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
where
\lambda\in X_{*}(\underline{T}^{\vee})
is a dominant cocharacter and
\tau
is a 1-generic tame inertial
L
-parameter. To a type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
, we associate a locally algebraic representations of
G_{0}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle\sigma(\lambda,\tau):=\sigma(\tau)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}}V(\phi^{-1}(\lambda)).

Let
W(\lambda)_{/\mathbf{F}}
be the dual Weyl module of highest weight
\lambda
for the algebraic group
\underline{G}_{/\mathbf{F}}
and
W(\lambda)
be the restriction of
W(\lambda)_{/\mathbf{F}}
to
G_{0}(\mathbf{F}_{p})
. Note that
V(\lambda)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbf{F}\simeq W(\lambda)
.

Definition 2.5.4
.

(1)

Let
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\omega)
be a lowest alcove presentation of a Serre weight
\sigma
. We say
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\omega)
is
compatible with
\zeta\in X^{*}(\underline{Z})
if the image of
t_{\omega-\eta}\widetilde{w}_{1}
in
X^{*}(\underline{Z})
under (
2.5.3
) is equal to
\zeta
. We also say that
\sigma

has algebraic central character
\zeta
(with respect to
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\omega)
)
.

(2)

Let
(s,\mu)
be a lowest alcove presentation of a Deligne–Lusztig representation
R
(resp. a tame inertial
L
-parameter
\tau
over
E
). Let
\lambda\in X^{*}(\underline{T})
. We say
(s,\mu)
is
\lambda
-compatible with
\zeta\in X^{*}(\underline{Z})
if the image of
t_{\lambda}t_{\mu+\eta}s
in
X^{*}(\underline{Z})
under (
2.5.3
) is equal to
\zeta
. When
\lambda=0
, we also say that
R
(resp.
\tau
)
has algebraic central character
\zeta
(with respect to
(s,\mu)
)
.

(3)

Let
(s,\mu)
be a lowest alcove presentation of a tame inertial
L
-parameter
\overline{\tau}
over
\mathbf{F}
. Let
\lambda\in X^{*}(\underline{T})
. We say
(s,\mu)
is
\lambda
-compatible with
\zeta\in X^{*}(\underline{Z})
if the image of
t_{\lambda}t_{\mu}s
in
X^{*}(\underline{Z})
under (
2.5.3
) is equal to
\zeta
. (Note that this differs by
t_{\eta}
from item (2); see
[
LLHLMa
, Remark 2.4.2]
.) When
\lambda=0
, we also say that
\tau

has algebraic central character
\zeta
(with respect to
(s,\mu)
)
.

(4)

We say that a lowest alcove presentation of tame inertial type
\tau
over
E
(or
\overline{\tau}
over
\mathbf{F}
) is
(
\lambda
-)compatible with
a lowest alcove presentation of a Serre weight (or Deligne–Lusztig representation) if the former is (
\lambda
-)compatible with
\zeta\in X^{*}(\underline{Z})
and the latter is compatible with
\zeta
.

Remark 2.5.5
.

A choice of algebraic central character lifting the central character of Serre weights or Deligne–Lusztig representations corresponds to a choice of lowest alcove presentations (see
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 2.2.4 and 2.3.2]
). Later, we will study objects whose constructions depend on choices of lowest alcove presentations and their connections. The (
\lambda
-)compatibility is a notion to make such choices consistent.

Let
\lambda\in X^{*}(\underline{T})
be a dominant character. We have the set of
admissible pairs
defined in
[
LLHLMa
, §2.1]

\displaystyle\mathrm{AP}(\lambda+\eta):=\left\{(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})\in(\underline{\widetilde{W}}_{1}^{+}\times\underline{\widetilde{W}}^{+})/X^{0}(\underline{T})\mid\widetilde{w}_{1}\uparrow t_{\lambda}\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\widetilde{w}_{2}\right\}.

Proposition 2.5.6

(Proposition 2.3.7 in
[
LLHLMa
]
)
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{*}(\underline{T})
be a dominant character. Let
m
be an integer such that
m\geq\max\{h_{\lambda}+3,6\}
if
G=\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
and
m\geq\max\{h_{\lambda}+n-1,2(n-1)\}
if
G=\mathrm{GL}_{n}
. For
m
-generic Deligne–Lusztig representation
R
, we have the following bijection

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{AP}(\lambda+\eta)&\simeq\mathrm{JH}(\overline{R}\otimes_{\mathbf{F}}W(\lambda))\\
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})&\mapsto F_{(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}(R)\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0))}.\end{split}

Moreover, every Jordan–Hölder factor are
(m-h_{\lambda}-3)
-deep and the lowest alcove presentations
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}(R)\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0))
of these Serre weights are
\lambda
-compatible with the lowest alcove presentation of
R
.

Remark 2.5.7
.

Suppose that we have
\lambda=0
in Proposition
2.5.6
. By
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 2.1.6]
, the condition
\widetilde{w}_{1}\uparrow\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\widetilde{w}_{2}
is equivalent to
\widetilde{w}_{2}\uparrow\widetilde{w}_{h}\widetilde{w}_{1}
. Let us write

\displaystyle\nu=\pi^{-1}(\widetilde{w}_{1})\cdot(\mu-\eta+s\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0))

for
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2})\in\mathrm{AP}(\eta)
so that
F_{(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}(R)\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0))}=F(\nu)
.
Then

\displaystyle\pi^{-1}(\widetilde{w}_{2})\cdot(\mu-\eta+s\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0))\uparrow\widetilde{w}_{h}\cdot\nu

as in Proposition
2.2.6
.

Proposition 2.5.8

(Proposition 2.6.2 in
[
LLHLMa
]
)
.

Let
m
be an integer such that
m\geq 6
if
G=\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
and
m\geq 2(n-1)
if
G=\mathrm{GL}_{n}
. Let
\overline{\rho}
be a tame inertial
L
-parameter over
\mathbf{F}
with a
m
-generic lowest alcove presentation. Then there is a bijection

\displaystyle\{(\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}_{2})\in(\underline{\widetilde{W}}^{+}_{1}\times\underline{\widetilde{W}}^{+})/X^{0}(\underline{T})\mid\widetilde{w}_{2}\uparrow\widetilde{w}\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}W^{?}(\overline{\rho})

\displaystyle(\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto F_{(\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0))}.

Moreover, every Jordan–Hölder factor are
(m-3)
-deep and the lowest alcove presentations
(\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0))
of these Serre weights are compatible with the lowest alcove presentation of
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})
.

Definition 2.5.9
.

Let
\sigma
and
\kappa
be Serre weights. We write
\sigma\uparrow\kappa
if there exist
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in X^{*}_{1}(\underline{T})
such that
\sigma\simeq F(\lambda)
,
\kappa\simeq F(\lambda^{\prime})
, and
\lambda\uparrow\lambda^{\prime}
.

Lemma 2.5.10
.

Let
\overline{\rho}
be a tame inertial
L
-parameter over
\mathbf{F}
that is 6-generic if
G=\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
and
2(n-1)
-generic if
G=\mathrm{GL}_{n}
. Let
\sigma,\kappa\in W^{?}(\overline{\rho})
be Serre weights. Then
\sigma\uparrow\kappa
if and only if
\sigma\simeq F_{(\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0))}
and
\kappa\simeq F_{(\widetilde{w}^{\prime},\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0))}
for
\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}^{\prime}\in\underline{\widetilde{W}}^{+}_{1}
and
\widetilde{w}_{2}\in\underline{\widetilde{W}}^{+}
such that
\widetilde{w}_{2}\uparrow\widetilde{w}\uparrow\widetilde{w}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\sigma\simeq F_{(\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0))}
and
\kappa\simeq F_{(\widetilde{w}^{\prime},\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})\widetilde{w}_{2}^{\prime-1}(0))}
for
\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}^{\prime}\in\underline{\widetilde{W}}^{+}_{1}
and
\widetilde{w}_{2},\widetilde{w}_{2}^{\prime}\in\underline{\widetilde{W}}^{+}
such that
\widetilde{w}_{2}\uparrow\widetilde{w}
and
\widetilde{w}_{2}^{\prime}\uparrow\widetilde{w}^{\prime}
. By changing
\widetilde{w}_{2}
up to
X^{0}(\underline{T})
(and
\widetilde{w}
accordingly), we can guarantee that
\pi^{-1}(\widetilde{w}_{2})\cdot(\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0)-\eta)
and
\pi^{-1}(\widetilde{w}_{2}^{\prime})\cdot(\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})\widetilde{w}_{2}^{\prime-1}(0)-\eta)
are in the same
\underline{W}_{a}
-orbit (under the
p
-dot action). Let
\widetilde{w}_{2}=wt_{\nu}
and
\widetilde{w}_{2}^{\prime}=w^{\prime}t_{\nu^{\prime}}
. Then
(p\pi^{-1}-1)\nu\equiv(p\pi^{-1}-1)\nu^{\prime}\bmod\underline{\Lambda}_{R}
. By applying
(1+p\pi^{-1}+\dots(p\pi^{-1})^{r-1})
to this equation where
r
is an integer such that
\pi^{r}=\mathrm{id}
, we deduce that
\nu\equiv\nu^{\prime}\bmod\underline{\Lambda}_{R}
. Then
p\pi^{-1}(\nu)-\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})\nu-\eta
and
p\pi^{-1}(\nu)-\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})\nu^{\prime}-\eta
are in the same
\underline{W}_{a}
-orbit. Since they are in the same
p
-alcove
\underline{C}_{0}+\pi^{-1}(p\nu)
, they have to be equal. Thus, we conclude that
\nu=\nu^{\prime}
, which implies that
\widetilde{w}_{2}=\widetilde{w}_{2}^{\prime}
. Then
\widetilde{w}\uparrow\widetilde{w}^{\prime}
follows immediately from
\sigma\uparrow\kappa
.
∎

For the remaining of this subsection, we let
G=\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
. The following lemmas will be useful in proving main results in §
6
.

Lemma 2.5.11

(Corollary 2.6.5 in
[
LLHLMa
]
)
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{*}(\underline{T})
be a dominant character. Let
\overline{\rho}
and
\tau
be tame inertial
L
-parameters over
\mathbf{F}
and
E
, with
\lambda
-compatible 6-generic and
\max\{6,h_{\lambda}+3\}
-generic lowest alcove presentation respectively, such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)=t_{w^{-1}(\lambda+\eta)}
for some
w\in\underline{W}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))\cap W^{?}(\overline{\rho})=\left\{(F_{\overline{\rho}}(w))\right\}.

Lemma 2.5.12
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{*}_{1}(\underline{T})
be a
12
-deep dominant character. Let
\overline{\rho}
be a tame inertial
L
-parameter over
\mathbf{F}
with a 12-generic lowest alcove presentation
(s_{\overline{\rho}},\mu_{\overline{\rho}})
. For each
s\in\underline{W}
, we consider a tame inertial type
\tau(s,\widetilde{w}_{h}\cdot\lambda+\eta)
.
Suppose that for each
s\in\underline{W}
,
\tau(s,\widetilde{w}_{h}\cdot\lambda+\eta)
admits a lowest alcove presentation such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau(s,\widetilde{w}_{h}\cdot\lambda+\eta))\in{\mathrm{Adm}}(\eta)
. Then
F(\lambda)\in W^{?}(\overline{\rho})
.

Proof.

This can be proven as
[
LLHL19
, Lemma 4.1.10]
.
∎

Remark 2.5.13
.

Let
(s,\mu+\eta)
be the lowest alcove presentation of
\tau(s,\widetilde{w}_{h}\cdot\lambda+\eta)
as in the previous Lemma. We remark that the condition
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau(s,\widetilde{w}_{h}\cdot\lambda+\eta))\in{\mathrm{Adm}}(\eta)
implies that the images of
st_{\mu+\eta}
and
s_{\overline{\rho}}t_{\mu_{\overline{\rho}}}
under (
2.5.3
) are equal, or equivalently,
\mu_{\overline{\rho}}-\mu\in\underline{\Lambda}_{R}
(cf. the condition
(P3)
in
[
LLHL19
, §4.1]
). This condition uniquely determines the lowest alcove presentation
(s,\mu+\eta)
of
\tau(s,\widetilde{w}_{h}\cdot\lambda+\eta)
(if it exists).

Lemma 2.5.14
.

Let
\overline{\rho}:I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow\prescript{L}{}{\underline{G}}(\mathbf{F})
be a 6-generic tame inertial
L
-parameter. Let
\sigma\in W^{?}(\overline{\rho})
be a Serre weight with a lowest alcove presentation
(\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}_{2})
for some
(\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}_{2})\in\underline{\widetilde{W}}_{1}^{+}\times\underline{\widetilde{W}}^{+}
such that
\widetilde{w}_{2}\uparrow\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\widetilde{w}
. Suppose that
\tau
is a tame type with a
6
-generic lowest alcove representation such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)=\widetilde{w}^{-1}w_{0}\widetilde{w}_{2}
. Then,

(1)

\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
; and

(2)

if
\kappa\in W^{?}(\overline{\rho})\cap\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
and
\sigma\uparrow\kappa
, then
\sigma=\kappa
.

Proof.

Since
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho})\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0)=\widetilde{w}(\tau)\widetilde{w}^{-1}(0)
, we have
\sigma=F_{(\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}(\tau)\widetilde{w}^{-1}(0))}
and
F_{(\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}(\tau)\widetilde{w}^{-1}(0))}\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
by Proposition
2.5.6
. Suppose that
\kappa\in W^{?}(\overline{\rho})\cap\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
and
\sigma\uparrow\kappa
. Let
(\widetilde{s},\omega)
be a lowest alcove presentation of
\kappa
compatible with
\overline{\rho}
. By Proposition
2.5.6
, there exists
\widetilde{s}_{1}\in\widetilde{W}^{+}
such that
\widetilde{s}\uparrow\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\widetilde{s}_{1}
and
\omega=\widetilde{w}(\tau)\widetilde{s}_{1}^{-1}(0)
. By Lemma
2.5.10
,
\sigma\uparrow\kappa
implies that
\widetilde{s}_{1}=\widetilde{w}
and
\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\widetilde{w}\uparrow\widetilde{s}
. By
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 2.1.6]
,
\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\widetilde{w}\uparrow\widetilde{s}
is equivalent to
\widetilde{w}_{h}\widetilde{s}\uparrow\widetilde{w}
and
\widetilde{s}\uparrow\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\widetilde{s}_{1}
is equivalent to
\widetilde{s}_{1}=\widetilde{w}\uparrow\widetilde{w}_{h}\widetilde{s}
. Thus
\widetilde{s}=\widetilde{w}_{h}^{-1}\widetilde{w}
and
\sigma=\kappa
.
∎

3.
The theory of local models

In this section, we generalize the theory of local models in
[
LLHLMa
]
to the group
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
. Note that when we write
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
in this section, we mean the dual group
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}^{\vee}
. In particular, the set of coroots
\Phi^{\vee}
of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
is identified with a set of roots of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}^{\vee}
by the duality isomorphism
\phi
. If
\alpha^{\vee}\in\Phi^{\vee}
, we let
U_{\alpha^{\vee}}\subset\mathrm{GSp}_{4}^{\vee}
denote the root subgroup associated to the root
\phi(\alpha^{\vee})
of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}^{\vee}
. In other words,
U_{\alpha}^{\vee}\subset\mathrm{GSp}_{4}^{\vee}
is a subgroup such that
U_{\alpha^{\vee}}\simeq\mathbf{G}_{a}
and
tut^{-1}=\phi(\alpha^{\vee})(t)u
for any
t\in T^{\vee}
and
u\in U_{\alpha^{\vee}}
.

We let
G=\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
for the remainder of this paper.

Let
X=\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbf{Z}}
be an affine line with coordinate function
v
. We denote by
X_{0}=\spec\mathbf{Z}
the zero section of
X
and
X^{0}=\spec\mathbf{Z}[v,v^{-1}]
. We often write
t:\spec R\rightarrow X
to denote
\mathbf{Z}[v]
-algebra
R
such that
v
is mapped to
t\in R
.

3.1.
Global affine Grassmannians

Let
{\mathcal{G}}
be the Neron blowup of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4/X}
in
B_{/X}
along
X_{0}
defined in
[
MRR
, Definition 3.1]
. By Theorem 3.2 of
loc. cit.
, it is a smooth affine group scheme over
X
with connected fibers. For
t:\spec R\rightarrow X
with
t
regular in
R
, the set of
R
-points is given by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}(R)=\{g\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R)\mid\ g\bmod t\in B(R/t)\}.

There is a morphism of
X
-group schemes
{\mathcal{G}}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4/X}
. If
g\in{\mathcal{G}}(R)
, we denote by
\overline{g}
its image in
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R)
. We also have a similitude character
\simc:{\mathcal{G}}\rightarrow\mathbf{G}_{m}
sending
g
to
\simc(\overline{g})
.

The base change
{\mathcal{G}}\times_{X}X^{0}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{GSp}_{4/X^{0}}
and the base change along
\varpi:\spec{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow X
is isomorphic to standard Iwahori group scheme
{\mathcal{I}}
whose
R
-points for any
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra
R
are given by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}(R)=\left\{g\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R\llbracket v\rrbracket)\mid g\bmod v\in B(R)\right\}.

In particular,
{\mathcal{G}}\times_{X,\varpi}\spec{\mathcal{O}}
coincides with the group scheme constructed in
[
PZ13
, Corollary 4.2]
for
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
and
{\mathcal{I}}
(see also
[
MRR
, Example 3.3]
).

We also define a functor
L^{+}{\mathcal{M}}
whose
R
-points, for
t:\spec R\rightarrow X
, are given by

\displaystyle L^{+}{\mathcal{M}}(R):=\{g\in\Lie\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R\llbracket v-t\rrbracket)\mid\text{$g$ is upper triangular modulo $v$}\}.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{4}
be the Bruhat–Tits group for
\mathrm{GL}_{4}
defined as in
[
LLHLMa
, §3.1]
. Note that the map
\std:\mathrm{GSp}_{4}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{4}
induces a morphism
X
-group schemes
{\mathcal{G}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{G}}_{4}
(see
[
MRR
, §2.4]
) which we denote by
\std
as well. It is easy to see that
\std:{\mathcal{G}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{G}}_{4}
is a closed immersion.

We write
L{\mathcal{G}}
for the loop group and
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}
for the positive loop group of
{\mathcal{G}}
. For
t:\spec R\rightarrow X
, their
R
-points are given by

\displaystyle L{\mathcal{G}}(R)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{G}}(R(\!(v-t)\!))

\displaystyle L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}(R)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{G}}(R\llbracket v-t\rrbracket)

where we consider
R\llbracket v-t\rrbracket
and
R(\!(v-t)\!)
as
\mathbf{Z}[v]
-algebra by sending
v
to
v
. It is known that
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}
is representable by a (not finite type) group scheme and
L{\mathcal{G}}
is representable by an ind-group scheme.

Remark 3.1.1
.

When
R
is Noetherian,
v
is regular in both
R\llbracket v-t\rrbracket
and
R(\!(v-t)\!)
. Thus we have the following description:

\displaystyle L{\mathcal{G}}(R)

\displaystyle=\left\{g\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R(\!(v-t)\!))\mid g\bmod v\in B(R(\!(v-t)\!)/v)\right\}

\displaystyle L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}(R)

\displaystyle=\left\{g\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R\llbracket v-t\rrbracket)\mid g\bmod v\in B(R\llbracket v-t\rrbracket/v)\right\}.

We define
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}
to be the fpqc quotient sheaf
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}{\backslash}L{\mathcal{G}}
. By
[
PZ13
, Proposition 6.5]
,
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}
is representable by an ind-projective ind-scheme.
By the properties of
{\mathcal{G}}
, the generic fiber
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}\times_{X}X^{0}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathrm{GSp}_{4}}\times_{\mathbf{Z}}X^{0}
, a constant familiy of affine Grassmannian for the group
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
over
X^{0}
, and its special fiber
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}\times_{X}X_{0}
is the affine flag variety
\mathrm{Fl}:={\mathcal{I}}{\backslash}\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
.

Let
d\in\mathbf{Z}
and
h\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 0}
. We define subfunctors
L{\mathcal{G}}^{\simc=d},L{\mathcal{G}}^{\simc=d,\leq h}\subset L{\mathcal{G}}
by

\displaystyle L{\mathcal{G}}^{\simc=d}(R)

\displaystyle=\left\{g\in L{\mathcal{G}}(R)\mid\simc(\overline{g})\in(v-t)^{d}(R\llbracket v-t\rrbracket)^{\times}\right\}

\displaystyle L{\mathcal{G}}^{\simc=d,\leq h}(R)

\displaystyle=\left\{g\in L{\mathcal{G}}^{\simc=d}(R)\mid\overline{g}\in\frac{1}{(v-t)^{h}}M_{4}(R\llbracket v-t\rrbracket)\right\}.

Both of them are stable under left multiplication by
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}
and induce fpqc quotient subsheafs

\displaystyle\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d,\leq h}:=L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}{\backslash}L{\mathcal{G}}^{\simc=d,\leq h}\subset\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d}:=L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}{\backslash}L{\mathcal{G}}^{\simc=d}\subset\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}.

The sheaf
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d,\leq h}
is representable by a projective scheme over
X
and
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d}=\varinjlim_{h}\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d,\leq h}
.

Our next goal is describing affine open charts of the projective scheme
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d,\leq h}
. We define the negative loop group to be a subfunctor
L^{--}{\mathcal{G}}\subset L{\mathcal{G}}
such that for
t:\spec R\rightarrow X
,

\displaystyle L^{--}{\mathcal{G}}(R)=\left\{g\in{\mathcal{G}}(R(\!(v-t)\!))\middle|\begin{array}[]{c}\overline{g}\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}\left(R\left[\frac{1}{v-t}\right]\right)\\
\overline{g}\bmod{\frac{1}{v-t}}\in\overline{U}(R)\\
\overline{g}\bmod\frac{v}{v-t}\in B\left(R\left[\frac{1}{v-t}\right]/\left(\frac{v}{v-t}\right)\right)\end{array}\right\}.

Lemma 3.1.2
.

The multiplication map

\displaystyle L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}\times_{X}L^{--}{\mathcal{G}}\rightarrow L{\mathcal{G}}

is formally étale in the sense of
[
LLHLMa
, Definition 3.2.4]
, and so is the map
L^{--}{\mathcal{G}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}
.

Proof.

Since this map is a restriction of a monomorphism
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{4}\times_{X}L^{--}{\mathcal{G}}_{4}\rightarrow L{\mathcal{G}}_{4}
(
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 3.2.2]
), it is a monomorphism. Then we can show that it is formally étale following the argument in
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 3.2.6]
using a version of
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 3.2.3]
for our setup and the formal smoothness of
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}
and
L^{--}{\mathcal{G}}
. Note that formal smoothness easily follows from the smoothness of
{\mathcal{G}}
.
∎

Let
\widetilde{z}=wt_{\nu}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee}
. We define
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})
to be the subfunctor of
L{\mathcal{G}}
given by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})(R)=\left\{g\in L{\mathcal{G}}(R)\middle|\begin{array}[]{cl}\overline{g}(v-t)^{-\nu}\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R[\frac{1}{v-t}]),\\
\overline{g}(v-t)^{-\nu}w^{-1}\bmod\frac{1}{v-t}\in\overline{U}(R),\\
\overline{g}(v-t)^{-\nu}\bmod\frac{v}{v-t}\in B(R[\frac{1}{v-t}]/(\frac{v}{v-t}))\end{array}\right\}.

Lemma 3.1.3
.

The subfunctor
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})\subset L{\mathcal{G}}
is stable under left multiplication by
L^{--}{\mathcal{G}}
and is left
L^{--}{\mathcal{G}}
-torsor. The natural map
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}
is monomorphism.

Proof.

The first claim follows from the definition of
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})
and the second claim follows from Lemma
3.1.2
.
∎

For
d=\simc(\nu)
and
h\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we define
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}
to be the base change
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})\times_{L{\mathcal{G}}}L{\mathcal{G}}^{\simc=d,\leq h}
. The following Proposition shows that
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}
is represented by a finite type affine scheme over
X
.

Proposition 3.1.4
.

For a Noetherian
\mathbf{Z}[v]
-algebra
R
,
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}(R)
is the set of matrices
A\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R[(v-t)^{\pm 1}])
satisfying:

•

For
1\leq i,j\leq 4
,

\displaystyle A_{ij}=v^{\delta_{i>j}}\left(\sum_{k=-h}^{\nu^{\prime}_{j}-\delta_{i>j}-\delta_{i<w^{\prime}(j)}}c_{ij,k}(v-t)^{k}\right)

and
c_{w^{\prime}(j)j,\nu^{\prime}_{j}-\delta_{w^{\prime}(j)>j}}=1
where
(\nu^{\prime}_{1},\nu^{\prime}_{2},\nu^{\prime}_{3},\nu^{\prime}_{4})=\std(\nu)
and
w^{\prime}=\std(w)
,

•

\simc(A)=\simc(w)(v-t)^{d}
.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}={\mathcal{U}}_{4}(\std(\widetilde{z}))^{\det,\leq h}\times_{L{\mathcal{G}}_{4}}L{\mathcal{G}}
and Proposition 3.2.8 in
[
LLHLMa
]
(where
{\mathcal{U}}_{4}(\std(\widetilde{z}))^{\det,\leq h}
denotes the affine chart defined in §3.2 of
loc. cit.
).
∎

Proposition 3.1.5
.

The map
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d,\leq h}
is an open immersion.

Proof.

We claim that
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d,\leq h}
is formally étale. Since a formally étale monomorphism between finite type schemes is an open immersion (
[
LLHLMa
, Remark 3.2.5]
and
[
Sta18
,
Tag 025G
]
), this completes the proof.

Let
A
be an Artinian local ring with residue field
k
.
Suppose we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\spec k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\spec A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d,\leq h}.}

We write
\overline{t}\in k
and
t\in A
for the image of the coordinate
v
of
X
. Since
k
and
A
are Noetherian, we can interpret the image of
\spec k
(and
\spec A
) in
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})
as a matrix
g_{k}\in\mathrm{GSp}(k(\!(v-\overline{t})\!))
satisfying certain conditions.
We claim that there exists
g_{A}\in{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})(A)
lifting
g_{k}
. Since
g_{k}(v-\overline{t})^{-\nu}\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(k[{\frac{1}{v-\overline{t}}}])
, we can find
g_{A}\in L{\mathcal{G}}(A)
lifting
g_{k}
and
g_{A}(v-t)^{-\nu}\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(A[{\frac{1}{v-t}}])
by the smoothness of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
. The set of such
g_{A}
is
\Lie\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathfrak{m}}_{A}[\frac{1}{v-t}])(v-t)^{\nu}
-coset.
To show that there exists
g^{\prime}_{A}\in{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})(A)
lifting
g_{k}
, we need to find
g^{\prime}_{A}
satisfying two conditions:

\displaystyle g^{\prime}_{A}(v-t)^{-\nu}w^{-1}\bmod\frac{1}{v-t}\in\overline{U}(A),\ \ g^{\prime}_{A}(v-t)^{-\nu}\bmod\frac{v}{v-t}\in B(A[\frac{1}{v-t}]/(\frac{v}{v-t})).

Thus, we need to find
N\in\Lie\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathfrak{m}}_{A}[\frac{1}{v-t}])
such that

\displaystyle g_{A}(v-t)^{-\nu}w^{-1}+Nw^{-1}\bmod\frac{1}{v-t}\in\overline{U}(A),\ \ g_{A}(v-t)^{-\nu}+N\bmod\frac{v}{v-t}\in B(A[\frac{1}{v-t}]/(\frac{v}{v-t})).

The existence of such
N
follows from the surjectivity of the quotient map

\displaystyle\Lie\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathfrak{m}}_{A}[\frac{1}{v-t}])\rightarrow\Lie\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathfrak{m}}_{A})\times\Lie\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathfrak{m}}_{A}[\frac{1}{v-t}]/(\frac{v}{v-t})).

Once we know that
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})(A)\neq\emptyset
, we can use Lemma
3.1.2
to find
\spec A\rightarrow{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})
which lifts
g_{k}
and when composed with
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}
provides the
A
-point of
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d,\leq h}
given in the above diagram. Then it has to factor through
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}
. Thus
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d,\leq h}
is formally étale.
∎

3.2.
Geometry of universal local models

We introduce universal local models and discuss their basic properties.

3.2.1.
Schubert varieties

Given a dominant cocharacter
\lambda\in X_{*}(T^{\vee})
, we denote by
s_{\lambda}
a section
X\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}
induced by the element
(v-t)^{\lambda}\in L{\mathcal{G}}(R)

for any
\mathbf{Z}[v]
-algebra
R
. A
global Schubert variety

{\mathcal{S}}_{X}(\lambda)
is defined as the minimal irreducible closed subscheme of
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}
containing the section
s_{\lambda}
and stable under the right multiplication of
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}
(cf.
[
Zhu14
, Definition 3.1]
). The map
S_{X}(\lambda)\rightarrow X
is proper. We also write
{\mathcal{S}}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)={\mathcal{S}}_{X}(\lambda)\times_{X}X^{0}
. As
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}\times_{X}X^{0}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathrm{GSp}_{4}}\times_{\mathbf{Z}}X^{0}
,
{\mathcal{S}}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)
is the constant family of the Schubert variety in
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathrm{GSp}_{4}}
for
\lambda
over
X^{0}
.

Let
{\mathrm{Conv}}(\lambda)
be the convex hull of the subset
W\lambda\subset X_{*}(T^{\vee})
.
A
open Schubert cell

{\mathcal{S}}^{\circ}(X)
is defined as an open subscheme

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}^{\circ}_{X}(\lambda)={\mathcal{S}}_{X}(\lambda)\backslash\cup_{\lambda^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{Conv}}(\lambda),\lambda^{\prime}\notin W\lambda}{\mathcal{S}}_{X}(\lambda^{\prime})\subset{\mathcal{S}}_{X}(\lambda).

Again, the base change
{\mathcal{S}}^{\circ}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)={\mathcal{S}}^{\circ}_{X}(\lambda)\times_{X}X^{0}
is the constant family of open Schubert cells of
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathrm{GSp}_{4}}
for
\lambda
over
X^{0}
.

We have a map
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}
given by the orbit map
g\mapsto s_{\lambda}g
. Note that it factors through a subscheme
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d,\leq h}
with
d=\simc(\lambda)
and
h
sufficiently large. The stabilizer subgroup scheme
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\lambda}\subset L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}
of
s_{\lambda}
is given by

\displaystyle L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\lambda}(R)=L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}(R)\cap\mathrm{Ad}((v-t)^{-\lambda})(L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}(R))

for
t:\spec R\rightarrow X
. Thus we have a monomorphism

\displaystyle L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\lambda}{\backslash}L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}^{\simc=d,\leq h}

whose scheme-theoretic image is
{\mathcal{S}}_{\lambda}(X)
. Over
X^{0}
, we have an isomorphism
(L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\lambda}{\backslash}L^{+}{\mathcal{G}})\times_{X}X^{0}\simeq{\mathcal{S}}^{\circ}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)
. Note that there is a map from
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\lambda}\times_{X}X^{0}
to
P_{\lambda}\times X^{0}
sending
g\mapsto g\bmod(v-t)
where
P_{\lambda}\subset\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
is a parabolic subgroup associated with
\lambda
and containing
B
. Thus, we have a natural map
(L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\lambda}{\backslash}L^{+}{\mathcal{G}})\times_{X}X^{0}\rightarrow P_{\lambda}{\backslash}\mathrm{GSp}_{4}\times_{\mathbf{Z}}X^{0}
given by
g\mapsto g\bmod(v-t)
. When it is composed with the previous isomorphism, we get a map

\displaystyle\pi_{\lambda}:{\mathcal{S}}^{\circ}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)\xrightarrow{}(P_{\lambda}\backslash\mathrm{GSp}_{4})\times_{\mathbf{Z}}X^{0}.

3.2.2.
Universal local models

For convenience, we let
\std:\mathbb{A}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{4}
be the morphism sending
(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})
to
(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}-a_{2},a_{3}-a_{1})
. Note that this matches with the description of
\std:X_{*}(T^{\vee})\rightarrow X_{*}(T^{\vee}_{4})
.

We define a subfunctor
L{\mathcal{G}}^{\nabla}
of
L{\mathcal{G}}\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3}
given by

\displaystyle L{\mathcal{G}}^{\nabla}(R)=\left\{(g,{\mathbf{a}})\mid g\in L{\mathcal{G}}(R),{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{A}^{3},v\frac{{\overline{g}}}{v}\overline{g}^{-1}+\overline{g}\mathrm{Diag}(\std({\mathbf{a}}))\overline{g}^{-1}\in\frac{1}{v-t}L^{+}{\mathcal{M}}(R)\right\}.

for
t:\spec R\rightarrow X
. Since
L{\mathcal{G}}^{\nabla}
is stable under left multiplication by
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}
, it defines a closed sub-ind-scheme
\mathrm{Gr}^{\nabla}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}:=L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}{\backslash}L{\mathcal{G}}^{\nabla}\subset\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3}
which is ind-proper over
X\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3}
.

Definition 3.2.3
.

Let
\lambda\in X_{*}(T^{\vee})
be a dominant cocharacter. We define the
naive universal local model

{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{nv}}_{X}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)
as
\mathrm{Gr}^{\nabla}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}\cap({\mathcal{S}}_{X}(\lambda)\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3})
.

We write
{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{nv}}_{X^{0}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)={\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{nv}}_{X}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)\times_{X}X^{0}
. It is a proper scheme over
X^{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{3}
.

Proposition 3.2.4
.

Let
\lambda\in X_{*}(T^{\vee})
be a dominant cocharacter. The map
\pi_{\lambda}
induces an isomorphism

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{nv}}_{X^{0}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)\cap({\mathcal{S}}^{\circ}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3})\right)\left[\frac{1}{h_{\lambda}!}\right]\simeq(P_{\lambda}{\backslash}\mathrm{GSp}_{4})\times_{\mathbf{Z}}X^{0}\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3}\left[\frac{1}{h_{\lambda}!}\right].

Proof.

Let
\overline{N}_{\lambda}
be the unipotent radical of opposite parabolic to
P_{\lambda}
. Recall the decomposition
\overline{N}_{\lambda}\simeq\prod U_{\alpha^{\vee}}
where the product runs over
\alpha^{\vee}\in\Phi^{\vee}
satisfying
\langle\phi(\alpha^{\vee}),\lambda\rangle<0
. For any commutative ring
R
and
N\in\overline{N}_{\lambda}(R)
, we can write
N
as a product of
N_{\alpha^{\vee}}\in U_{\alpha^{\vee}}(R)
over the same set of
\alpha^{\vee}
.

Note that
\{\overline{N}_{\lambda}w\}_{w\in W}
forms an affine open cover of
P_{\lambda}{\backslash}\mathrm{GL}_{n}
. By pulling back along
\pi_{\lambda}
, we get an affine open cover of
{\mathcal{S}}^{\circ}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)
. More precisely, we have
\pi_{\lambda}^{-1}(\overline{N}_{\lambda}w)=\widetilde{N}_{\lambda}w
where
\widetilde{N}_{\lambda}
is the affine scheme over
X^{0}
whose
R
-points for for
t:\spec R\rightarrow X
are given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}_{\lambda}(R)=\left\{(v-t)^{\lambda}N\mid\text{$N\in\overline{N}_{\lambda}(R[v-t])$, $\deg N_{\alpha^{\vee}}\leq\langle\phi(\alpha^{\vee}),-\lambda\rangle-1$}\right\}.

Let us write
N_{\alpha^{\vee}}=\sum_{j=0}^{\langle\phi(\alpha^{\vee}),-\lambda\rangle-1}X_{\alpha^{\vee},j}(v-t)^{j}
with
X_{\alpha^{\vee},j}\in R
. From the proof of
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 3.3.4]
, we can deduce that taking the intersection
\mathrm{Gr}^{\nabla}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}\cap(\widetilde{N}_{\lambda}w\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3})\left[\frac{1}{h_{\lambda}!}\right]
imposes conditions that
X_{\alpha^{\vee},j}
for
\alpha^{\vee}
and
j>0
is determined by
X_{\alpha^{\prime\vee},0}
for
\alpha^{\prime}\leq\alpha<0
. Since the map

\displaystyle\pi_{\lambda}:\mathrm{Gr}^{\nabla}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}\cap(\widetilde{N}_{\lambda}w\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3})\left[\frac{1}{h_{\lambda}!}\right]\rightarrow\overline{N}_{\lambda}w\times_{\mathbf{Z}}X^{0}\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3}\left[\frac{1}{h_{\lambda}!}\right]

takes
(v-t)^{\lambda}Nw
to
(N\bmod(v-t))w
, this proves that
\pi_{\lambda}
is an isomorphism.
∎

Remark 3.2.5
.

The above Proposition implies that
{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{nv}}_{X^{0}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)\cap({\mathcal{S}}^{\circ}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3})\left[\frac{1}{h_{\lambda}!}\right]
is indeed proper over
X^{0}\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3}\left[\frac{1}{h_{\lambda}!}\right]
(by the properness of partial flag varieties). Thus the map

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{nv}}_{X^{0}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)\cap({\mathcal{S}}^{\circ}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3})\left[\frac{1}{h_{\lambda}!}\right]\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{nv}}_{X^{0}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)\left[\frac{1}{h_{\lambda}!}\right]

is a proper open immersion. Since proper morphism is universally closed, this map is an inclusion of a connected component. Using this inductively, we obtain an isomorphism (cf.
[
LLHLMa
, Corollary 3.3.5]
)

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{nv}}_{X^{0}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)\left[\frac{1}{h_{\lambda}!}\right]\right)_{{\mathrm{red}}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\coprod_{\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in X_{*}^{+}(T^{\vee})}(P_{\lambda^{\prime}}{\backslash}\mathrm{GSp}_{4})\times_{\mathbf{Z}}X^{0}\times\mathbf{Z}\mathbb{A}^{3}\left[\frac{1}{h_{\lambda}!}\right].

Definition 3.2.6
.

Let
\lambda\in X_{*}(T^{\vee})
be a dominant cocharacter. The
universal local model

{\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)
is the closure of the connected component
{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{0}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)\cap({\mathcal{S}}^{\circ}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3})
of
{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{0}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)
inside
{\mathcal{M}}_{X}^{{\mathrm{nv}}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)
. In particular, it is
v
-flat.

Proposition 3.2.7
.

Let
\lambda\in X_{*}(T^{\vee})
be a dominant cocharacter. Then
{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{0}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)\left[\frac{1}{(2h_{\lambda})!}\right]
is smooth over
X^{0}\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3}\left[\frac{1}{(2h_{\lambda})!}\right]
.

Proof.

Since the proof of
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 3.2.6]
generalizes to our situation quite straight forwardly, we only sketch the idea of the proof. We follow the notation in
loc. cit.
and write
Y={\mathcal{M}}_{X^{0}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)\left[\frac{1}{(2h_{\lambda})!}\right]
,
S=X^{0}\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3}\left[\frac{1}{(2h_{\lambda})!}\right]
, and
pr
for the natural projection map
Y\rightarrow S
.

There is a
T^{\vee}
-action on
Y
induced by the right multiplication by
T^{\vee}
on
{\mathcal{G}}
. Note that this preserves the non-smooth locus in
Y
. Since
\mathrm{pr}
is proper and the non-smooth locus in
Y
is closed, the image of the non-smooth locus in
S
is closed as well. If the non-smooth locus is non-empty, it has a non-zero geometric fiber which is a proper variety over a field with
T^{\vee}
-action. Thus, such fiber contains a
T^{\vee}
-fixed point. Since it is contained in a geometric fiber of
{\mathcal{S}}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)
, it is known to be contained in the support of a section
s_{\mu}:X^{0}\rightarrow{\mathcal{S}}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)
for some
\mu\in{\mathrm{Conv}}(\lambda)
(see Lemma 3.3.7 in
loc. cit.
).

Thus, it suffices to prove the smoothness at a closed point
x\in\spec k\rightarrow Y
lying in the support of
s_{\mu}
. Let
s=\mathrm{pr}(x)
. We claim that
\dim T_{x}Y/S\leq\dim_{x}Y_{s}
.
If we have this bound, the completion
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Y,x}
is generated over
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{S,s}
by
\dim_{x}Y_{s}
many variables. Since
S
is regular,
\dim\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Y,x}\leq\dim\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{S,s}+\dim_{x}Y_{s}
where the equality holds if and only if
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{Y,x}
is a power series ring over
\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{S,s}
with
\dim_{x}Y_{s}
many variables. By Remark
3.2.5
and the choice of
x
, we have

\displaystyle\dim_{x}Y_{s}=\dim P_{\mu}{\backslash}\mathrm{GSp}_{4},\ \ \dim_{x}Y=\dim P_{\mu}{\backslash}\mathrm{GSp}_{4}+4

Thus, the equality holds and
\mathrm{pr}
is smooth at
x
by
[
Sta18
,
Tag 07VH
]
.

Finally, we prove the claimed inequality. Following the argument in
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 3.3.6]
and using Lemma
3.1.4
, we can show that
T_{x}Y_{s}
is a subspace of the space of matrices of the form
(1+\epsilon X)(v-t)^{\mu}
where
X\in\Lie\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(k(\!(v-t)\!))
whose entries are polynomials with degree bounds, and
X
satisfies certain equation imposed by
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X^{0}}^{\nabla}
. More precisely, the computation in
loc. cit.
shows that all diagonal entries of
X
are zero, and for each root
\alpha
, the
\alpha
-th entry of
X
is zero if
\langle\mu,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle\geq 0
and is determined by the coefficient of the lowest degree term if
\langle\mu,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle<0
. Thus the space of such
X
has dimension at most
\dim P_{\mu}{\backslash}\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
. This completes the proof.
∎

We finish this subsection with two lemmas regarding the normalization of the universal local model. For an integral scheme
Y
, we let
Y^{\mathrm{nm}}\rightarrow Y
denote the normalization of
Y
.

Let
l>0
be an integer. It will be useful to consider the base change

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{l}:={\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)\times_{X,v\mapsto v^{l}}X

and its normalization
({\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{l})^{\mathrm{nm}}
. When
l=e
and if we can take
\varpi=(-p)^{1/e}
as the uniformizer in
{\mathcal{O}}
, this has the advantage that the map
\spec{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}
sending
v
to
-p
is the composite of the maps
\spec{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}
sending
v\mapsto\varpi
and
X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}\rightarrow X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}
sending
v\mapsto v^{e}
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)\times_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{3},v\mapsto-p}\spec{\mathcal{O}}={\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{e}\times_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{3},v\mapsto\varpi}\spec{\mathcal{O}}.

Lemma 3.2.8
.

There is an open subscheme
U\subset\mathbb{A}^{3}
only depending on
\lambda
and
l
, such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{l}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{3}}U\rightarrow X\times_{\mathbf{Z}}U
and
({\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{l})^{\mathrm{nm}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{3}}U\rightarrow X\times_{\mathbf{Z}}U
are flat.

Proof.

This follows from
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 3.5.5, Remark 3.5.6]
.
∎

Lemma 3.2.9
.

There is an open subscheme
U\subset\mathbb{A}^{3}
only depending on
\lambda
and
l
, such that if
R
is complete DVR and
f:\spec R\rightarrow X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}
is a morphism sending
v
to a uniformizer of
R
and factors through
X\times U
, the base change

\displaystyle({\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{l})^{\mathrm{nm}}_{R}:=({\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{l})^{\mathrm{nm}}\times_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}}\spec R

is flat over
\spec R
, and
({\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{l})^{\mathrm{nm}}_{R}
is normal.

Proof.

This follows from
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 3.5.2]
. To satisfy Setup 3.5.1 of
loc. cit.
, take
S=\mathbb{A}^{3}[\frac{1}{(2h_{\lambda})!}]
,
M=({\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{l})^{\mathrm{nm}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{3}}S
, and use Proposition
3.2.7
.
∎

3.3.
Local models in mixed characteristic

We specialize to objects over
{\mathcal{O}}
by taking base change
\spec{\mathcal{O}}\rightarrow X
sending
v
to
-p
. For a fpqc sheaf
Y\rightarrow X
, we write
Y_{\mathcal{O}}=Y\times_{X}\spec{\mathcal{O}}
. For example, we have
L{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}=L{\mathcal{G}}\times_{X}\spec{\mathcal{O}}
and
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}},L^{+}{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{O}}
similarly.

We have the global affine Grassmannian
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}=L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}{\backslash}L{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}
. Its generic fiber
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},E}
is equal to the usual affine Grassmannian associated to
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
over
E
. For
\lambda\in X_{*}(T^{\vee})
a dominant cocharacter, we write
S_{E}^{\circ}(\lambda)\subset\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathrm{GSp}_{4},E}
for the open affine Schubert cell and
S_{E}(\lambda)
for its reduced closure. The Zariski clousre of
S_{E}(\lambda)
in
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}
is the Pappas–Zhu local model
M(\leq\lambda)
associated to the group
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
, the conjugacy class of
\lambda
, and the Iwahori subgroup
{\mathcal{I}}
(
[
PZ13
]
). It is known that
M(\leq\lambda)
is projective over
\spec{\mathcal{O}}
(§7.1 in
loc. cit.
).

Let
{\mathbf{a}}\in{\mathcal{O}}^{3}
. We define
L{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}^{\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}}\subset L{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}
to be the subfunctor given by

\displaystyle L{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}^{\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}}(R):=\left\{g\in L{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}(R)\mid v\frac{d{\overline{g}}}{dv}\overline{g}^{-1}+\overline{g}\mathrm{Diag}(\std({\mathbf{a}}))\overline{g}^{-1}\in\frac{1}{v+p}L^{+}{\mathcal{M}}_{4}(R)\right\}

for an
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra
R
. It is stable under left
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}
multiplication and induces a closed sub-ind-scheme
\mathrm{Gr}^{\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}\subset\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}
.

Definition 3.3.1
.

We define the
naive local model
as
M^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})=M(\leq\lambda)\cap\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}^{\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}}
. The
(mixed characteristic) local model

M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
is defined to be the Zariski closure of
S^{\circ}_{E}(\lambda)\cap\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}^{\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}}
in
M(\leq\lambda)
. By its construction,
M(\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})
is projective and flat over
\spec{\mathcal{O}}
.

We define
U(\widetilde{z}):={\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})\times_{X}\spec{\mathcal{O}}
and
U(\widetilde{z},\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}}):=U(\widetilde{z})\cap M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
where the intersection is taken inside
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}
. Note that the latter is equal to
({\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}\times_{X}\spec{\mathcal{O}})\cap M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
for large enough
h\geq 0
. Thus, it is an open affine subscheme of
M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
by Proposition
3.1.5
.

Our main interest in the geometry of
M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
is whether a completed local ring
\mathcal{O}_{M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}),x}^{\wedge}
is a domain for some
x\in M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
. To understand this property more geometrically, we introduce the following definition.

Definition 3.3.2
.

Let
Y
be a scheme. A point
y\in Y
is called
unibranch
if the normalization of the local ring
({\mathcal{O}}_{Y,y})_{\mathrm{red}}
is local.

Remark 3.3.3
.

Suppose that
Y
is Noetherian and excellent. The followings are equivalent:

(1)

y\in Y
is unibranch;

(2)

the fiber above
y
of the normalization map
Y^{\mathrm{nm}}\rightarrow Y
is a single point (
[
Sta18
,
Tag 0C3B
]
);

(3)

the completed local ring
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y,y}^{\wedge}
is a domain (
[
Sta18
,
Tag 0C2E
]
).

Let
\widetilde{z}=wt_{\nu}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee}
. There is a constant section
\widetilde{z}:\spec\mathbf{Z}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}\times_{X}X_{0}
for
h
large enough, given by
wv^{\nu}\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{Z}(\!(v)\!))
. We denote its composition with
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}\times_{X}X_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}\times_{X}X_{0}

again by
\widetilde{z}
.

Let
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{A}^{3}({\mathcal{O}})
. We write the induced
\mathbf{F}
point by
{\mathbf{a}}
as well. We write
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
for the
\mathbf{F}
-point
\spec\mathbf{F}\rightarrow(\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}\times_{X}X_{0})\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3}
given by
(\widetilde{z},{\mathbf{a}})
.

Recall that
e
denotes the ramification index of the extension
{\mathcal{O}}/\mathbf{Z}_{p}
.

Theorem 3.3.4
.

There exists a non-empty open subscheme
U\subset\mathbb{A}^{3}
depending only on
\lambda
and
e
, such that if
{\mathbf{a}}\in U({\mathcal{O}})
, then
M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
is unibranch at any point
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
contained in the special fiber. In addition,
{\mathcal{O}}(U(\widetilde{z},\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}))^{\wedge_{p}}
is a domain.

To prove this Theorem, we introduce the local model in equal characteristic and prove its unibranch property.

Definition 3.3.5
.

Let
{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{A}^{3}(\mathbf{F})
. We define
{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}):={\mathcal{M}}_{X}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)\times_{\mathbb{A}^{3}}{\mathbf{a}}
.
We define the
equal characteristic local model

{\mathcal{M}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
by the Zariski closure of

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{nv}}_{X^{0}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla)\cap({\mathcal{S}}^{\circ}_{X^{0}}(\lambda)\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3})\right)\times_{\mathbb{A}^{3}}{\mathbf{a}}

in
{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
.

Remark 3.3.6
.

Although mixed or equal characteristic local models are generally
not
equal to base changes of universal local models, their generic fibers can be obtained by taking base changes of the generic fibers of universal local models. Suppose that
h_{\lambda}<p
.
By taking fiber product at
E
-point
(-p,{\mathbf{a}})\in X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}
(resp. at
\mathbf{F}
-point
{\mathbf{a}}
), we also have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{0}}(\lambda,\nabla)\times_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}}\spec E

\displaystyle=M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})\times_{\mathcal{O}}\spec E

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{0}}(\lambda,\nabla)\times_{\mathbb{A}^{3}}\spec\mathbf{F}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{M}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})\times_{X}X^{0}.

Moreover,
M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
(resp.
{\mathcal{M}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
) is characterized as
p
-flat (resp.
v
-flat) closure of
{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{0}}(\lambda,\nabla)\times_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}}\spec E
inside
{\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)\times_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{0}}(\lambda,\nabla)\times_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}}\spec E
inside
{\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)\times_{\mathbb{A}^{3}}\spec\mathbf{F}
).

For
l\in\mathbf{Z}_{>0}
and an
X
-scheme
Y
, we write
Y_{l}
for the base change
Y\times_{X,v\mapsto v^{l}}X
.

Proposition 3.3.7

(cf. Proposition 3.4.4 in
[
LLHLMa
]
)
.

Let
l>0
be an integer. Then
{\mathcal{M}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})_{l}
is unibranch at any
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
contained in its special fiber. Moreover, the preimage of
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})
in
({\mathcal{M}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})_{l})^{\mathrm{nm}}\times_{X}X_{0}
is connected.

Proof.

This mainly follows from
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 3.4.7, 3.4.8]
. Indeed, we can take a one-parameter subgroup in Lemma 3.4.7 in
loc. cit.
valued in
T^{\vee}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}
(instead of
T^{\vee}_{4}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}
) whose induced action on
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})^{\simc,\leq h}
(instead of
{\mathcal{U}}_{4}(\widetilde{z})^{\det,\leq h}
) satisfies the conditions stated in Lemma 3.4.7 in
loc. cit.
. Using this, the proof of
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 3.4.4]
can be applied in our case too.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.3.4
.

The novel idea in
[
LLHLMa
]
is comparing the mixed characteristic and equal characteristic local models inside the universal local model. Let
U
be an open subscheme
U\subset\mathbb{A}^{3}
satisfying Lemma
3.2.8
and
3.2.9
for
l=e
and
(2h_{\lambda})!e!
is invertible. Note that this implies
U({\mathcal{O}})=\emptyset
unless
p>(2h_{\lambda})!e!
. Thus we can assume that
{\mathcal{O}}/\mathbf{Z}_{p}
is tame and take
\varpi=(-p)^{1/e}
(after enlarging the residue field if necessary). Let
{\mathbf{a}}\in U({\mathcal{O}})
. The following diagram explains how to make such comparison.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})^{\mathrm{nm}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{e})^{\mathrm{nm}}\times_{X\times U}\spec{\mathcal{O}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\spec{\mathcal{O}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{e})^{\mathrm{nm}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{e}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{e})^{\mathrm{nm}}\times_{X\times U}\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbf{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})_{e}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbf{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathcal{M}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})_{e})^{\mathrm{nm}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(v\mapsto\varpi,{\mathbf{a}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(v\mapsto v,{\mathbf{a}})}

By Lemma
3.2.8
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{e}
is flat over
X\times U
. Thus, all rectangles are cartesian by Remark
3.3.6
. Moreover, two base changes of normalization map
({\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{e})^{\mathrm{nm}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{e}
are finite and birational. Finiteness is obvious, and birationality is preserved by base change because the dense open subscheme
{\mathcal{M}}_{X^{0}}(\lambda,\nabla)_{e}\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{e}
, which is already normal by Proposition
3.2.7
, is still dense after each base change. This induces two surjective dashed arrows by
[
Sta18
,
Tag 035Q
]
, and the top dashed arrow is an isomorphism by Lemma
3.2.9
.

Note that
M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
and
{\mathcal{M}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
share the same special fiber. Suppose that
M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
is
not
unibranch at
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
. Then there are at least two points in the preimage of
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
in
M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})^{\mathrm{nm}}
. Therefore, the preimage of
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
in
({\mathcal{M}}(\lambda,\nabla)_{e})^{\mathrm{nm}}
contains at least two points. In turn, this implies that the preimage of
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
in
({\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda,\nabla)_{e})^{\mathrm{nm}}\times_{X\times U}\mathbb{A}_{\mathbf{F}}^{1}
contains at least two points. By the surjectivity of the bottom dashed map, this contradicts Proposition
3.3.7
. This proves that
M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
is unibranch at
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
.

Note that
{\mathcal{O}}(U(\widetilde{z},\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}))[1/\varpi]
is regular domain by Proposition
3.2.7
. Also, the preimage of
U(\widetilde{z},\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
in the special fiber of
M(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})^{\mathrm{nm}}
is connected by Proposition
3.3.7
and the above diagram. Then
{\mathcal{O}}(U(\widetilde{z},\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}))^{\wedge_{p}}
is a domain by
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 3.7.2]
.
∎

3.4.
Products of local models

We now generalize Theorem
3.3.4
to products of local models. Let
{\mathcal{J}}
be a finite set. Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}\in X_{*}(T^{\vee})^{\mathcal{J}}
be a dominant cocharacter and
\widetilde{z}=(\widetilde{z}_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}
. We define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{X,{\mathcal{J}}}(\lambda,\nabla)=\prod_{{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}}{\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda_{j},\nabla)\subset(\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbb{A}^{3})^{\mathcal{J}}.

Let
{\mathbf{a}}=({\mathbf{a}}_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}\in(\mathbb{A}^{3})^{\mathcal{J}}({\mathcal{O}})
.
We also define
M_{\mathcal{J}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}):=\prod_{{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}}M(\lambda_{j},\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{j}})
and
U_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{z},\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})):=\prod_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}U(\widetilde{z}_{j},\lambda_{j},\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{j}})
.
The following is the main result of this section.

Theorem 3.4.1
.

There exists a non-empty open subscheme
U\subset(\mathbb{A}^{3})^{\mathcal{J}}
depending only on
\lambda
and
e
, such that if
{\mathbf{a}}\in U({\mathcal{O}})
, then
M_{\mathcal{J}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
is unibranch at any point
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
contained in its special fiber. In addition,
{\mathcal{O}}(U_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{z},\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}))^{\wedge_{p}}
is a domain.

Proof.

Let
M_{j}:={\mathcal{M}}_{X}(\lambda_{j},\nabla)\rightarrow X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}
and
\widetilde{z}_{j}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee}
. The special fiber
M_{j}\times_{X}X_{0}
intersect with a section
\widetilde{z}_{j}
only if
\widetilde{z}_{j}=wt_{\nu}
for some
w\in W
and
\nu\in{\mathrm{Conv}}(\lambda_{j})
. Note that there are finitely many
\widetilde{z}_{j}
satisfying these conditions.

Let
\eta
be the generic point of
\mathbb{A}^{3}
. We define
Fix_{j}
to be the set of
\widetilde{z}_{j}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee}
which intersects with
M_{j}\times_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{3}}(X_{0}\times\eta)
. For any
\widetilde{z}_{j}
such that
\widetilde{z}_{j}=wt_{\nu}
for some
w\in W
and
\nu\in{\mathrm{Conv}}(\lambda_{j})
, consider a map

\displaystyle f_{\widetilde{z}_{j}}:(M_{j}\times_{X}X_{0})\cap\widetilde{z}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{3}.

(Here, the intersection is taken inside
{\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},X}\times_{X}X_{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{3}}
.)
The image of
f_{\widetilde{z}_{j}}
is constructible, and it contains
\eta
if and only if
\widetilde{z}_{j}\in Fix_{j}
. Note that any constructible set containing the generic point of irreducible scheme contains an open neighborhood of the generic point. Thus, there exists an open neighborhood
V^{\prime}_{j}
of
\eta
which is contained in the image of
f_{\widetilde{z}_{j}}
for all
\widetilde{z}_{j}\in Fix_{j}
and the complement of the image of
f_{\widetilde{z}_{j}}
for all
\widetilde{z}_{j}\notin Fix_{j}
.

Let
V_{j}
be the intersection of
V^{\prime}_{j}
with the open subscheme of
\mathbb{A}^{3}
satisfying the conclusion of Lemma
3.2.8
for
\lambda_{j}
and
e
.
Then
M_{j}|_{V_{j}}\rightarrow X\times V_{j}
and
\widetilde{z}_{j}\in Fix_{j}
satisfy the assumptions of
[
LLHLMa
, Corollary 3.6.2]
. As a result, there exists an integer
e^{\prime}
and an open subscheme
U^{\prime}_{j}\subset V_{j}
satisfying the following: for any
\widetilde{z}_{j}\in Fix_{j}
and
{\mathbf{a}}_{j}\in U_{j}({\mathcal{O}})
, there exists a finite DVR extension
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathcal{O}}
of degree
\leq e^{\prime}
and an
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
-point of
M_{j}
lifting
(\widetilde{z}_{j},{\mathbf{a}}_{j})\in M(\lambda_{j},\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{j}})(\mathbf{F})
.

Let
U_{j}\subset U_{j}^{\prime}
be the open subscheme in which Theorem
3.3.4
holds for
\lambda_{j}
and
ee^{\prime}!
. Then we define
U=\prod_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}U_{j}
. Let
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}/{\mathcal{O}}
be the extension obtained by adjoining
e^{\prime}!
-th root of uniformizer and
e^{\prime}!
-th root of unity. Note that
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
contains any extension of
{\mathcal{O}}
of degree
\leq e^{\prime}
.
For
\widetilde{z}\in\prod_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}Fix_{j}
and
{\mathbf{a}}\in U({\mathcal{O}})
,
M_{\mathcal{J}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
is unibranch at
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
if and only if the completed local ring of
M_{\mathcal{J}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})
at
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
is a domain, which is a subring of the completed local ring of
M_{\mathcal{J}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})\times_{\mathcal{O}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
at
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
. Similarly,
{\mathcal{O}}(U_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{z},\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}}))^{\wedge_{p}}
is a subring of
{\mathcal{O}}(U_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{z},\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}))^{\wedge_{p}}
. Thus, it suffices to prove the claim after replacing
{\mathcal{O}}
by
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
. Note that both the completed local ring of
M_{\mathcal{J}}(\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})\times_{\mathcal{O}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
at
\widetilde{z}_{\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}}
and
{\mathcal{O}}(U_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{z},\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}))^{\wedge_{p}}
are completed tensor products of the completed local ring of
M(\lambda_{j},\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{j}})\times_{\mathcal{O}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
at
\widetilde{z}_{j,\mathbf{F},{\mathbf{a}}_{j}}
and
{\mathcal{O}}(U_{\mathcal{J}}(\widetilde{z}_{j},\lambda_{j},\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{j}}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}))^{\wedge_{p}}
, which are known to be domain by Theorem
3.3.4
. By the choice of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
, each
M_{j}\times_{{\mathcal{O}}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
has
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
-point lifting
(\widetilde{z}_{j},{\mathbf{a}}_{j})
in its special fiber. Then the two claims follow from
[
KW09b
, Proposition 2.2]
, and
[
BLGGT14
, Lemma A.1.1]
respectively (as explained in the last two paragraphs of the proof of
[
LLHLMa
, Theorem 3.7.1]
).
∎

3.5.
Special fiber of naive local models in mixed characteristic

In the remainder of this section, we study the special fiber of the naive local models in mixed characteristic. Recall that the naive local models in mixed characteristic are defined by imposing the monodromy condition on the Pappas–Zhu local models inside
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}=L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}{\backslash}L{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}
. The special fiber
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\spec\mathbf{F}
is the affine flag variety
\mathrm{Fl}:={\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}{\backslash}L(\mathrm{GSp}_{4})_{\mathbf{F}}
. Given
{\mathbf{a}}\in{\mathcal{O}}^{3}
, we define

\mathrm{Fl}^{\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}}:=\mathrm{Fl}\times_{\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}}\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}^{\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}}
.

It is known by
[
PZ13
, Theorem 9.3]
that the special fiber
M(\leq\lambda)_{\mathbf{F}}
is the reduced union of the affine Schubert cells
S^{\circ}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{z})
for
\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)
. Thus, the underlying reduced closed subscheme of
M^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})_{\mathbf{F}}
is equal to the reduced union of
S^{\circ}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{z})\cap\mathrm{Fl}^{\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}}
for
\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)
.

For
\alpha\in\Phi
, we define
H_{\alpha}^{(0,1)}=\{x\in X^{*}(T)\times_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{R}\mid 0<\langle x,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle<1\}
.
The following is the main result of this subsection.

Theorem 3.5.1
.

Let
h>0
be an integer,
\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{W}
, and
{\mathbf{a}}\in{\mathcal{O}}^{3}
. Suppose that
\widetilde{w}
is
h
-small and
{\mathbf{a}}\bmod\varpi\in\mathbf{F}^{3}
is
h
-generic (Definition
2.1.8
). Then
S^{\circ}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{w}^{*})\cap\mathrm{Fl}^{\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}}
is an affine space of dimension
4-\#\left\{\alpha\in\Phi^{+}\mid\widetilde{w}(A_{0})\subset H_{\alpha}^{(0,1)}\right\}
.

As a Corollary, we get a classification of top(
=4
)-dimensional irreducible components of
M^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})_{\mathbf{F}}
.
If
d\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
X
is a reduced scheme, we let
\mathrm{Irr}_{d}X
denote the set of
d
-dimensional irreducible closed subschemes of
X
.

Definition 3.5.2
.

We say that
\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{W}
is
regular
if
\widetilde{w}^{*}(A_{0})\nsubseteq H^{(0,1)}_{\alpha}
for any
\alpha\in\Phi^{+}
.
Let
\lambda\in X_{*}(T^{\vee})
. We define
{\mathrm{Adm}}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\vee}(\lambda)\subset{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)
to be the subset of
\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)
such that
\widetilde{z}^{*}
is regular. We similarly define
{\mathrm{Adm}}_{\mathrm{reg}}(\nu)
for
\nu\in X^{*}(T)
. Then
(-)^{*}
induces a bijection between
{\mathrm{Adm}}_{\mathrm{reg}}(\nu)
and
{\mathrm{Adm}}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\vee}(\phi(\nu))
.

Corollary 3.5.3
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{*}(T^{\vee})
be a dominant cocharacter. Suppose that
{\mathbf{a}}\bmod\varpi\in\mathbf{F}^{n}
is
h_{\lambda}
-generic. There is a bijection

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Adm}}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\vee}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Irr}_{4}M^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{\mathbf{a}})_{\mathbf{F}}

\displaystyle\widetilde{z}

\displaystyle\mapsto\overline{(S^{\circ}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{z})\cap\mathrm{Fl}^{\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}})}.

Proof.

This follows from
[
PZ13
, Theorem 9.3]
and Theorem
3.5.1
. To apply Theorem
3.5.1
, note that
\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}_{\mathrm{reg}}(\lambda)
is
h_{\lambda}
-small.
∎

To prove the Theorem
3.5.1
, we describe
S^{\circ}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{z})
using explicit coordinates. A version of Bruhat decomposition says that we have a double coset decomposition

\displaystyle(L\mathrm{GSp}_{4})_{\mathbf{F}}=\cup_{\widetilde{z}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee}}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}\widetilde{z}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}

and the open Schubert cell
S^{\circ}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{z})
can be identified with
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}{\backslash}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}\widetilde{z}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}
.

Let
L^{--}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbf{F}}\subset L{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbf{F}}
be the subfunctor given by

\displaystyle L^{--}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbf{F}}(R)=\left\{g\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}\left(R\left[\frac{1}{v}\right]\right)\mid g\bmod\frac{1}{v}\in\overline{B}(R)\right\}

for any
\mathbf{F}
-algebra
R
. We define
N_{\widetilde{z}}:=\widetilde{z}^{-1}L^{--}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbf{F}}\widetilde{z}\cap{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}
.

Recall that the duality isomorphism
\phi
identifies a coroot
\alpha^{\vee}
of
G
and a root
\phi(\alpha^{\vee})
of
G^{\vee}
, and we write
U_{\alpha^{\vee}}
for the root subgroup of
G^{\vee}
associated to
\phi(\alpha^{\vee})
. Given
(\alpha^{\vee},m)\in\Phi^{\vee}\times\mathbf{Z}
, we let
U_{\alpha^{\vee},m}\subset LU_{\alpha^{\vee}}
denote the subfunctor such that for any
\mathbf{F}
-algebra
R
,
U_{\alpha^{\vee},m}(R)\subset U_{\alpha^{\vee}}(R(\!(v)\!))
is identified with
v^{m}R\subset R(\!(v)\!)
under the isomorphism
U_{\alpha^{\vee}}\simeq\mathbf{G}_{a}
.

In what follows, we fix
x\in A_{0}
. Note that
\lceil\langle x,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle\rceil=\delta_{\alpha^{\vee}>0}
and
\lfloor\langle x,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle\rfloor=-\delta_{\alpha^{\vee}<0}
.

Proposition 3.5.4
.

Let
\widetilde{z}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee}
.

(1)

We have the following decomposition

\displaystyle N_{\widetilde{z}}\simeq\prod_{(\alpha^{\vee},m)\in\Phi^{\vee}_{\widetilde{z}}}U_{-\alpha^{\vee},m}

where the product runs over the set
\Phi^{\vee}_{\widetilde{z}}=\left\{(\alpha^{\vee},m)\in\Phi^{\vee}\times\mathbf{Z}\mid\langle x,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle<m<\langle\widetilde{z}^{*}(x),\alpha^{\vee}\rangle\right\}
.

(2)

There is an isomorphism

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}\times_{\mathbf{F}}\left\{\widetilde{z}\right\}\times_{\mathbf{F}}N_{\widetilde{z}}\simeq{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}\widetilde{z}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}

given by multiplication. This induces an isomorphism
\widetilde{z}N_{\widetilde{z}}\simeq S^{\circ}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{z})
.

Proof.

(1)

We claim that
U_{-\alpha^{\vee},m}\subset N_{\widetilde{z}}
if and only if
(\alpha^{\vee},m)\in\Phi^{\vee}_{\widetilde{z}}
.
It is easy to check that
U_{-\alpha^{\vee},m}\subset{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}
if and only if
\delta_{\alpha^{\vee}>0}\leq m
. Let
\widetilde{z}^{*}=st_{\nu}\in\widetilde{W}
. Direct computation shows that
\widetilde{z}U_{-\alpha^{\vee},m}\widetilde{z}^{-1}=U_{-s^{-1}(\alpha^{\vee}),m-\langle\nu,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle}
. Thus
U_{-\alpha,m}\subset\widetilde{z}^{-1}L^{--}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbf{F}}\widetilde{z}
if and only if
m-\langle\nu,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle<\langle x,s^{-1}(\alpha^{\vee})\rangle
, or equivalently
m<\langle s(x)+\nu,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle
. This proves the claim.

Observe that
U_{-\alpha^{\vee},m}\subset N_{\widetilde{z}}
only if
\langle\widetilde{z}^{*}(x),\alpha^{\vee}\rangle>0
. Let
w\in W
be the unique element such that
w\widetilde{z}^{*}\in\widetilde{W}^{+}
. If
\langle\widetilde{z}^{*}(x),\alpha^{\vee}\rangle=\langle w\widetilde{z}^{*}(x),w(\alpha^{\vee})\rangle>0
, then
w(\alpha^{\vee})>0
. In other words,
\phi(w)N_{\widetilde{z}}\phi(w)^{-1}\subset L\overline{U}
. Then the claimed decomposition follows from the decomposition of
\overline{U}
.

(2)

The injectivity of the multiplication map is obvious. The surjectivity follows from
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}=((\widetilde{z}^{-1}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}\widetilde{z})\cap{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}})N_{\widetilde{z}}
.

∎

Let
d_{\alpha^{\vee},\widetilde{z}}=\lfloor\langle\widetilde{z}^{*}(x),\alpha^{\vee}\rangle\rfloor-\lceil\langle x,\alpha^{\vee}\rangle\rceil
.

Corollary 3.5.5
.

Let
R
be a Noetherian
\mathbf{F}
-algebra. If
M\in N_{\widetilde{z}}(R)
, we can write
M=\prod_{\alpha^{\vee}\in\Phi^{\vee}}v^{\delta_{\alpha^{\vee}>0}}M_{\alpha^{\vee}}
where
M_{\alpha^{\vee}}\in U_{-\alpha^{\vee}}(R[v])\simeq R[v]
is a polynomial of degree at most
d_{\alpha^{\vee},\widetilde{z}}
.

Proof of Theorem
3.5.1
.

Let
\widetilde{z}=\widetilde{w}^{*}
and
w\in W
be the unique element such that
w\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{W}^{+}
.
We follow the proof of
[
LLHLMa
, Theorem 4.2.4]
using the Corollary
3.5.5
instead of Corollary 4.2.12 of
loc. cit.
. Indeed,
loc. cit.
shows that the monodromy condition on
\widetilde{z}M
for some
M\in N_{\widetilde{z}}(R)
implies that coefficients in the polynomial
M_{\alpha^{\vee}}
are determined by its top degree coefficient and coefficients of
M_{\alpha^{\prime\vee}}
such that
w(\alpha^{\prime\vee})<w(\alpha^{\vee})
. Inductively, this shows that
S^{\circ}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{z})\cap\mathrm{Fl}^{\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}}
is an affine space with coordinates given by the top degree coefficients of
M_{\alpha^{\vee}}
.
Thus, the dimension of
S^{\circ}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{z})\cap\mathrm{Fl}^{\nabla_{\mathbf{a}}}
is equal to

\displaystyle\#\left\{\alpha^{\vee}\in\Phi^{\vee}\mid U_{-\alpha^{\vee},m}\subset N_{\widetilde{z}}\text{ for some $m\geq 0$}\right\}.

This is equal to the number of
\alpha^{\vee}\in\Phi^{\vee}
such that
\langle\widetilde{z}^{*}(x),\alpha^{\vee}\rangle>0
and
\widetilde{z}^{*}(x)
and
x
does not lie in the same
\alpha
-strip. The first condition says that
w(\alpha^{\vee})\in\Phi^{\vee,+}
, which has size 4. For such
\alpha
, the second condition holds unless
\alpha^{\vee}\in\Phi^{\vee,+}
and
