\displaystyle\underline{\alpha}_{S}:S^{\prime}\mapsto\left\{\alpha^{\prime}\in Isom({\mathfrak{M}}_{S^{\prime}},{\mathfrak{M}}_{S^{\prime}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{N}}_{S^{\prime}})\mid((\alpha^{\prime})^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{N}}_{S^{\prime}})^{-1}\circ\alpha^{\prime}=-1_{{\mathfrak{M}}_{S^{\prime}}}\right\}

for any
S
-scheme
S^{\prime}
. The sheaf
\underline{Isom}({\mathfrak{M}}_{S},{\mathfrak{M}}_{S}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{N}}_{S})
is representable by affine scheme by
[
CEGS22
, Proposition 3.1.3]
. There is a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\alpha}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Isom}({\mathfrak{M}}_{S},{\mathfrak{M}}_{S}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{N}}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Isom}({\mathfrak{M}}_{S},{\mathfrak{M}}_{S}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{N}}_{S})\times_{S}\underline{Isom}({\mathfrak{M}}_{S}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{N}}_{S},{\mathfrak{M}}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{Aut}({\mathfrak{M}}_{S})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-1_{{\mathfrak{M}}_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

where the right verticle map is a constant section given by
-1_{{\mathfrak{M}}_{S}}\in\underline{Aut}({\mathfrak{M}}_{S})
, the left bottom horizontal map sends
\alpha
to
(\alpha,(\alpha^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{N}})^{-1})
, and
c
is the composition. This shows that
\underline{\alpha}_{S}
is representable by scheme. The claim that
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}
is a
p
-adic formal algebraic stack follows from
[
Eme
, Lemma 5.19]
.
∎

We recall several notions regarding Breuil–Kisin modules. We refer
[
LLHLMa
, §5]
and the references therein for more detailed discussions.

Let
\beta=(\beta^{(j^{\prime})})_{j^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}}
be an eigenbasis of
{\mathfrak{M}}\in Y_{n}^{[0,h],\tau}
(in the sense of
[
LLHLMa
, Definition 5.1.6]
). Then the dual basis
\beta^{\vee}=((\beta^{(j^{\prime})})^{\vee})_{j^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}}
is an eigenbasis of
{\mathfrak{M}}^{\vee}
.

Definition 4.2.6
.

Let
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}(R)
. An
eigenbasis
of
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)
is a pair
(\beta,\gamma)
of eigenbasis
\beta=(\beta^{(j^{\prime})})_{j^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}}
of
{\mathfrak{M}}
and
\gamma=(\gamma^{(j^{\prime})})_{j^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}}
of
{\mathfrak{N}}
satisfying

\displaystyle\alpha(\beta^{(j^{\prime})})=((\beta^{(j^{\prime})})^{\vee}\otimes\gamma^{(j^{\prime})})J.

Let
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}(R)
be a symplectic Breuil–Kisin module with eigenbasis
(\beta,\gamma)
. For
j^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}
, we write
C^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}\in M_{4}(R\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket)
for the matrix representation of
\phi_{\mathfrak{M}}^{(j^{\prime})}
with respect to
\varphi^{*}(\beta^{(j^{\prime}-1)})
and
\beta^{(j^{\prime})}
. The height condition implies
E(u^{\prime})^{h}(C^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta})^{-1}\in M_{4}(R\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket)
.

Recall that
(s,\mu)
is a 1-generic lowest alcove presentation of
\tau
. For
j^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}
, we write
j^{\prime}=j_{0}+fk
for unique
0\leq j\leq f-1
and
0\leq k\leq r-1
. Define

(4.2.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bm{\alpha}^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}}&=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}s_{\tau}^{-k}(s_{f-1}^{-1}s_{f-2}^{-1}\dots s_{f-j}^{-1})(\mu_{f-j}+\eta_{f-j})&\text{if $j^{\prime}\neq 0$}\\
\mu_{0}+\eta_{0}&\text{if $j^{\prime}=0$}\end{array}\right.\\
({\mathbf{a}}^{\prime})^{(j^{\prime})}&=\sum_{i=0}^{f^{\prime}-1}\bm{\alpha}^{\prime}_{-j^{\prime}+i}p^{i}\\
s^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{or}},j^{\prime}}&=s_{\tau}^{k+1}(s_{f-1}^{-1}s_{f-2}^{-1}\dots s_{j+1}^{-1}).\end{split}

Then we “remove the descent datum” and get the matrix

\displaystyle A_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}^{(j^{\prime})}:=\mathrm{Ad}(\phi(s^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{or}},j^{\prime}})^{-1}\phi(-({\mathbf{a}}^{\prime})^{(j^{\prime})})(u^{\prime}))(C_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}^{(j^{\prime})})\in M_{4}(R\llbracket v\rrbracket)

that is upper triangular modulo
v
for each
j^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}
. By height condition, we have
E(v)^{h}(A_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}^{(j^{\prime})})^{-1}\in M_{4}(R\llbracket v\rrbracket)
which is upper triangular modulo
v
. Both
C_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}^{(j^{\prime})}
and
A_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}^{(j^{\prime})}
only depend on
j^{\prime}\bmod f
. Since
\alpha(\beta^{(j^{\prime})})=((\beta^{(j^{\prime})})^{\vee}\otimes\gamma^{(j^{\prime})})J
, we have the following symplecticity property of matrices
C_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}^{(j^{\prime})}
and
A_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}^{(j^{\prime})}
.

Lemma 4.2.8
.

Let
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}(R)
with an eigenbasis
(\beta,\gamma)
.

(1)

The partial Frobenius matrix
C^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}
satisfies

\displaystyle(C^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta})^{\top}JC^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}=C^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{N}},\gamma}E(v)^{h}J

In particular, we have
C^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket[\frac{1}{E(v)}])
.

(2)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle(A^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta})^{\top}JA^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}=A^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{N}},\gamma}E(v)^{h}J.

In particular, for
p
-adically complete
R
, we have
A^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}\in L{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}(R)
.

Proof.

We can show that
(\beta^{\vee}\otimes\gamma)J
is an eigenbasis of
{\mathfrak{M}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{N}}
and

\displaystyle C^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{N}},(\beta^{\vee}\otimes\gamma)J}=C^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{N}},\gamma}E(v)^{h}J^{-1}(C^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta})^{-\top}J

and similarly for
A^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{N}},(\beta^{\vee}\otimes\gamma)J}
. Then the claim follows from the definition of symplectic Breuil–Kisin module and its eigenbasis.
∎

We record how
A^{(j^{\prime})}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}
changes under the change of basis.

Proposition 4.2.9
.

Let
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}(R)
with an eigenbasis
(\beta_{1},\gamma_{1})
. Suppose that
(\beta_{2},\gamma_{2})
is another eigenbasis such that, for each
j^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}
,
\beta_{2}^{(j^{\prime})}=\beta_{1}^{(j^{\prime})}D^{(j^{\prime})}
and
\gamma^{(j^{\prime})}_{2}=\gamma_{1}^{(j^{\prime})}\simc(D^{(j^{\prime})})
for some
D^{(j^{\prime})}\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket)
. Define

\displaystyle I^{(j^{\prime})}:=\mathrm{Ad}(\phi(s^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{or}},j^{\prime}})^{-1}\phi(-({\mathbf{a}}^{\prime})^{(j^{\prime})})(u^{\prime}))(D^{(j^{\prime})}).

Then
I^{(j^{\prime})}\in{\mathcal{I}}(R)
and it satisfies

\displaystyle A_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta_{1}}^{(j^{\prime})}=I^{(j^{\prime})}A_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta_{2}}^{(j^{\prime})}(\mathrm{Ad}(\phi(s_{j}^{-1})\phi(\mu_{j}+\eta_{j})(v))(\varphi(I^{(j^{\prime}-1)})^{-1}))

where
j\in{\mathcal{J}}
,
j=j^{\prime}\bmod f
.

Conversely, for any
I^{(j^{\prime})}\in{\mathcal{I}}(R)
only depending on
j^{\prime}\bmod f
, then the matrix

\displaystyle D^{(j^{\prime})}:=\mathrm{Ad}(\phi(({\mathbf{a}}^{\prime})^{(j^{\prime})})(u^{\prime})\phi(s^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{or}},j^{\prime}}))(I^{(j^{\prime})})\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R\llbracket u^{\prime}\rrbracket),

and
(\beta_{2},\gamma_{2})
given by
\beta_{2,j^{\prime}}=\beta_{1,j^{\prime}}D^{(j^{\prime})}
and
\gamma_{2,j^{\prime}}=\gamma_{1,j}\simc(D^{(j^{\prime})})
is an eigenbasis.

Proof.

This essentially follows from
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 5.1.8]
. Note that the conditions
\beta_{2}^{(j^{\prime})}=\beta_{1}^{(j^{\prime})}D^{(j^{\prime})}
and
\gamma^{(j^{\prime})}_{2}=\gamma_{1}^{(j^{\prime})}\simc(D^{(j^{\prime})})
imply that
(\beta_{2},\gamma_{2})
is an eigenbasis.
∎

Definition 4.2.10
.

Let
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}(\mathbf{F})
. We define the
shape
of
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)
to be the unique element
\widetilde{z}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}
such that
A^{(j)}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}\in{\mathcal{I}}(\mathbf{F})\widetilde{z}_{j}{\mathcal{I}}(\mathbf{F})
for some eigenbasis
(\beta,\gamma)
and for each
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
. By Proposition
4.2.9
, this is independent of the choice of eigenbasis.

For integers
a\leq b
, we define
L^{[a,b]}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}
to be the subfunctor of
L{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}
given by

\displaystyle L^{[a,b]}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}(R)=\left\{g\in L{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}(R)\mid E(v)^{-a}\overline{g},E(v)^{b}\overline{g}^{-1}\in M_{4}(R\llbracket v+p\rrbracket)\right\}.

for
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra
R
. It is preserved by left and right multiplication by
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}
. We write

\displaystyle\mathrm{Gr}^{[a,b]}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}=L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}{\backslash}L^{[a,b]}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}

for the induced sub-ind-scheme of
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}
. For
(s,\mu)\in W^{\mathcal{J}}\times X^{*}(T)^{\mathcal{J}}
, we define
(s,\mu)
-twisted
\varphi
-conjugation action of
(L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}})^{\mathcal{J}}
on
(L^{[a,b]}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}})^{\mathcal{J}}
by

\displaystyle(I^{(j)})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}\cdot(A^{(j)})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}:=\left(I^{(j)}A^{(j)}((\mathrm{Ad}(\phi(s_{j}^{-1})\phi(\mu_{j}+\eta_{j})(v))(\varphi(I^{(j-1)})^{-1}))\right)_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}.

Similarly, we define
(s,\mu)
-twisted conjugation action by the above formula, but with the
\varphi
dropped.
We denote by
[(L^{[a,b]}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}})^{\mathcal{J}}/_{\varphi,(s,\mu)}(L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}})^{\mathcal{J}}]
the fpqc quotient stack using
(s,\mu)
-twisted
\varphi
-conjugation action.

Let
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau,\beta}
be the fpqc-stackification of a category fibered in groupoids over
\spf{\mathcal{O}}
sending
R
to a groupoid of tuples
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
where
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{[0,h],\tau}(R)
and
(\beta,\gamma)
is an eigenbasis of
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)
. By Proposition
4.2.9
,
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau,\beta}
is
L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathcal{J}}
-torsor over
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}
.

Proposition 4.2.11
.

The morphism
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau,\beta}\rightarrow(L^{[0,h]}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}})^{\mathcal{J}}
sending
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
to
(A^{(j)}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta})_{\mathcal{J}}
induces an isomorphism of
p
-adic formal algebraic stacks
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}\simeq[(L^{[0,h]}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}})^{\mathcal{J}}/_{\varphi,(s,\mu)}(L^{+}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}})^{\mathcal{J}}]^{\wedge_{p}}
. Here,
{\wedge_{p}}
denotes the
p
-adic completion.

Proof.

This can be proven as
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 5.2.1]
using Proposition
4.2.9
.
∎

Recall that
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mathbf{F}}
is equal to the affine flag variety
\mathrm{Fl}={\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}{\backslash}L(\mathrm{GSp}_{4})_{\mathbf{F}}
. For integers
a\leq b
, let
L^{[a,b]}(\mathrm{GSp}_{4})_{\mathbf{F}}
denote the functor defined on
\mathbf{F}
-algebras
R
by

\displaystyle L^{[a,b]}(\mathrm{GSp}_{4})_{\mathbf{F}}:R\rightarrow\left\{A\in L\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R)\mid Av^{-a},A^{-1}v^{b}\in M_{4}(R\llbracket v\rrbracket)\right\}.

The fpqc quotient
[{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}{\backslash}L^{[a,b]}(\mathrm{GSp}_{4})_{\mathbf{F}}]
is isomorphic to a Noetherian closed subscheme
\mathrm{Fl}^{[a,b]}\subset\mathrm{Fl}
. Note that
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mathbf{F}}^{[a,b]}\subset\mathrm{Fl}^{[a,b]}
where the inclusion is strict.

We define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{1,\mathbf{F}}:R\mapsto\left\{A\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R\llbracket v\rrbracket)\mid A\bmod v\in U(R)\right\}

and
\widetilde{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mathbf{F}}^{[a,b]}:=[{\mathcal{I}}_{1,\mathbf{F}}{\backslash}L^{[a,b]}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbf{F}}]
. It is a
T^{\vee}_{\mathbf{F}}
-torsor over
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mathbf{F}}^{[a,b]}
. Similarly, we have
\widetilde{\mathrm{Fl}}^{[a,b]}:={\mathcal{I}}_{1,\mathbf{F}}{\backslash}L^{[a,b]}(\mathrm{GSp}_{4})_{\mathbf{F}}
which is a
T^{\vee}_{\mathbf{F}}
-torsor over
\mathrm{Fl}^{[a,b]}
.

Proposition 4.2.12
.

Suppose that the lowest alcove presentation
(s,\mu)
of
\tau
is
(h+1)
-generic. There is an isomorphism between algebraic stacks
\pi_{(s,\mu)}:Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{[0,h],\tau}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}[(\widetilde{\mathrm{Gr}}^{[0,h]}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mathbf{F}})^{\mathcal{J}}/_{(s,\mu)}T_{\mathbf{F}}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}]
.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
4.2.11
and
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 5.2.2]
. Note that
(I^{(j)})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
in
loc. cit.
is an element in
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R\llbracket v\rrbracket)^{\mathcal{J}}
if and only if
(X_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
in
loc. cit.
is an element in
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R\llbracket v\rrbracket)^{\mathcal{J}}
.
∎

Recall that, for each
\widetilde{z}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}
, there is a subfunctor
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})=\prod_{{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}}{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z}_{j})\subset L{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathcal{J}}
. For any integers
a\leq b
, we define

\displaystyle U^{[a,b]}(\widetilde{z}):={\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})_{\mathcal{O}}\cap L^{[a,b]}{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathcal{J}}.

The natural projection map
U^{[a,b]}(\widetilde{z})\rightarrow(\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{O}}}^{[a,b]})^{\mathcal{J}}
is an open immersion by Proposition
3.1.5
. Since
\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mathbf{F}}^{\mathcal{J}}=\mathrm{Fl}^{\mathcal{J}}
is ind-proper and its
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}
-fixed points are exactly
\widetilde{z}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}
,
{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})_{\mathbf{F}}
form an open cover of
\mathrm{Gr}^{\mathcal{J}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mathbf{F}}
.
Also we have
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}
-torsors

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{U}}}(\widetilde{z})

\displaystyle:=T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})\rightarrow{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}^{[a,b]}(\widetilde{z})

\displaystyle:=T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}U(\widetilde{z})^{[a,b]}\rightarrow U(\widetilde{z})^{[a,b]}.

The images of
{U}(\widetilde{z})^{[a,b]}_{\mathbf{F}}
(resp.
\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z})^{[a,b]}_{\mathbf{F}}
) form an open cover of
({\mathrm{Gr}}^{[a,b]}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mathbf{F}})^{\mathcal{J}}
(resp.
(\widetilde{\mathrm{Gr}}^{[a,b]}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mathbf{F}})^{\mathcal{J}}
).

Recall that
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}
is a
p
-adic formal algebraic stack, which implies
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}=\varinjlim_{i}Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}\times_{\spf{\mathcal{O}}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{i}
. The algebraic stack
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}\times_{\spf{\mathcal{O}}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{i}
has the same underlying topological spaces for all
i\in\mathbf{Z}_{>0}
. There is a bijection between open substack of given algebraic stack and open subsets of its underlying topological spaces (see
[
Sta18
,
Tag 06FJ
]
). Thus there is bijection between open substacks of
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{i}}^{[0,h],\tau}:=Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}\times_{\spf{\mathcal{O}}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{i}
and open substacks of
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{[0,h],\tau}
.

Definition 4.2.13

(cf. Definition 5.2.4 in
[
LLHLMa
]
)
.

Let
\widetilde{z}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}
.

(1)

We define
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{[0,h],\tau}(\widetilde{z})
to be the open substack of
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{[0,h],\tau}
corresponding to

\displaystyle[\widetilde{U}^{[0,h]}(\widetilde{z})_{\mathbf{F}}/_{(s,\mu)}T_{\mathbf{F}}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}]\subset[(\widetilde{\mathrm{Gr}}^{[0,h]}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mathbf{F}})^{\mathcal{J}}/_{(s,\mu)}T_{\mathbf{F}}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}]

under the isomorphism
\pi_{(s,\mu)}
. We write
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{i}}^{[0,h],\tau}(\widetilde{z})
to be the open substack of
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{i}}^{[0,h],\tau}
induced by
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{[0,h],\tau}(\widetilde{z})
.

(2)

We define the
p
-adic formal open substack
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}(\widetilde{z}):=\varinjlim_{i}Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{i}}^{[0,h],\tau}(\widetilde{z})
of
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}
.

(3)

Let
R
be a
p
-adically complete
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra. We say that
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}(R)

admits
\widetilde{z}
-gauge
if
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}(\widetilde{z})(R)
.

(4)

For
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}(\widetilde{z})(R)
, we define a
\widetilde{z}
-gauge basis
of
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)
as an eigenbasis
(\beta,\gamma)
such that
A_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}^{(j)}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{U}}}(\widetilde{z}_{j})(R)
for all
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
.

Proposition 4.2.14
.

Let
(s,\mu)
be a
(h+1)
-generic lowest alcove presentation of
\tau
. Let
R
be a
p
-adically complete
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra and
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}(\widetilde{z})(R)
. If
{\mathfrak{M}}
admits an eigenbasis, then
{\mathfrak{M}}
admits a
\widetilde{z}
-gauge basis, and the set of
\widetilde{z}
-gauge bases is a
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}(R)
-torsor.

Proof.

Note that if
(\beta,\gamma)
is a
\widetilde{z}
-gauge basis for
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)
, then
\beta
has to be a
\widetilde{z}
-gauge basis for
{\mathfrak{M}}
in the sense of
[
LLHLMa
, Definition 5.2.6]
. By Proposition 5.2.7 of
loc. cit.
,
{\mathfrak{M}}
admits a
\widetilde{z}
-gauge basis
\beta
,
and the set of such basis is
T_{4}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}
-torsor. Let
\gamma
be any eigenbasis of
{\mathfrak{N}}
.
Since
(\beta^{\vee}\otimes\gamma)J
and
\alpha(\beta)
are
\widetilde{z}
-gauge basis of
{\mathfrak{M}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{N}}
, there exists
t\in T_{4}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}(R)
such that
\alpha(\beta_{j})=(\beta_{j}^{\vee}\otimes\gamma_{j})Jt_{j}
for
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
. Then the alternating condition implies that
t_{j}Jt_{j}^{-1}=J
. Let
\beta^{\prime}=\beta t^{\prime}
for some
t^{\prime}\in T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{4}(R)
and
\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma c
for some
c\in(R\llbracket v\rrbracket^{\times})^{\mathcal{J}}
. Then
(\beta^{\prime})^{\vee}=\beta^{\vee}(t^{\prime})^{-1}
and

\displaystyle\alpha(\beta^{\prime}_{j})=((\beta^{\prime}_{j})^{\vee}\otimes\gamma^{\prime}_{j})t^{\prime}_{j}Jt^{\prime}_{j}t_{j}c_{j}^{-1}\text{ for each ${j\in{\mathcal{J}}}$}.

The condition on
t
implies that there exists
t^{\prime}_{j}
and
c_{j}
such that
t^{\prime}_{j}Jt^{\prime}_{j}=c_{j}Jt^{-1}_{j}
for each
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
, and the solution gives a
\widetilde{z}
-gauge basis
(\beta^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})
of
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)
. Since the set of solutions is a
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}
-torsor, the set of
\widetilde{z}
-gauge basis is a
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}
-torsor.
∎

Let
\lambda\in X_{*}(T^{\vee})^{\mathcal{J}}
be a dominant cocharacter such that
\std(\lambda_{j})\in[0,h]^{4}
for all
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
. There is a
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat closed
p
-adic formal substack
Y_{4}^{\leq\std(\lambda),\tau}\subset Y_{4}^{[0,h],\tau}
(e.g.
[
LLHLMa
, §5.3]
). For simplicity, we write
Y_{4}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
instead of
Y_{4}^{\leq\std(\lambda),\tau}
. We have its symplectic variant
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
which is defined as a
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat part of
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}\times_{Y^{[0,h],\tau}_{4}}Y_{4}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
. For any finite extension
E^{\prime}/E
with the ring of integer
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
,
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{[0,h],\tau}({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})
belongs to
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})
if and only if the elementary divisors of
A_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}^{(j)}\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(E^{\prime}(\!(v+p)\!))
(for any eigenbasis
(\beta,\gamma)
) is bounded by
E(v)^{\lambda_{j}}
for each
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
. In particular,
(A_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}^{(j)})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
gives a
E^{\prime}
-point in
S_{E}(\lambda)\subset\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},E}^{\mathcal{J}}
. We have its open substack

\displaystyle Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau}_{\mathrm{Sym}}(\widetilde{z})=Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau}_{\mathrm{Sym}}\times_{Y^{[0,h],\tau}_{\mathrm{Sym}}}Y^{[0,h],\tau}_{\mathrm{Sym}}(\widetilde{z}).

Remark 4.2.15
.

For any finite extension
\mathbf{F}^{\prime}/\mathbf{F}
,
Y_{4}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\mathbf{F}^{\prime})
is the full subgroupoid of
Y_{4}^{[0,h],\tau}(\mathbf{F}^{\prime})
consisting of Breuil–Kisin modules whose shapes lie in the set
{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\std(\lambda))
by
[
CL18
, Proposition 5.4]
. By Lemma
2.1.6
,
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\mathbf{F}^{\prime})
is the full subgroupoid of
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{[0,h],\tau}(\mathbf{F}^{\prime})
consisting of Breuil–Kisin modules whose shapes lie in the set
{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)\simeq{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\std(\lambda))^{\Theta}
.

On the local model side, recall the Pappas–Zhu local model
M_{\mathcal{J}}(\leq\lambda)\subset\mathrm{Gr}^{[0,h],{\mathcal{J}}}_{{\mathcal{J}},{\mathcal{O}}}
. We have its open neighborhood at
\widetilde{z}
defined by
U(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda):=M_{\mathcal{J}}(\leq\lambda)\cap{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})_{\mathcal{O}}
. We also write
\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda):=T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}\times U(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)
.

Theorem 4.2.16
.

Let
(s,\mu)
be a
(h+1)
-generic lowest alcove presentation of
\tau
and let
\widetilde{z}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}
. We have a local model diagram of
p
-adic formal algebraic stacks over
{\mathcal{O}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)^{\wedge_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})=\left[\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)/_{(s,\mu)}T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}\right]^{\wedge_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)^{\wedge_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}}

where diagonal arrows are
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
-torsors. The superscript
\wedge_{p}
means taking
p
-adic completion.

Proof.

We need to show that
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})=\left[\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)/_{(s,\mu)}T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}\right]^{\wedge_{p}}
. Let
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau,\beta}(\widetilde{z})
be the fpqc-stackification of a category fibered in groupoids over
\spf{\mathcal{O}}
sending
R
to a groupoid of tuples
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
where
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})(R)
and
(\beta,\gamma)
is a
\widetilde{z}
-gauge basis of
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)
. For a ring of integer
{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}
in a finite extension
E^{\prime}/E
and
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau,\beta}(\widetilde{z})({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})
,
(A_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}^{(j)})_{\mathcal{J}}\in\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)({\mathcal{O}}^{\prime})
by the characterization of
Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}
and the definition of gauge bases. Since
Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau,\beta}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}(\widetilde{z})
and
\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)^{\wedge_{p}}
are
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat, this induces a map
Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau,\beta}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}(\widetilde{z})\rightarrow\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)^{\wedge_{p}}
sending
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
to
(A_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}^{(j)})_{\mathcal{J}}
, which induces an isomorphism
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}\left[\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)/_{(s,\mu)}T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}\right]^{\wedge_{p}}
by Proposition
4.2.9
and
4.2.14
.
∎

Corollary 4.2.17
.

Under the hypothesis in Theorem
4.2.16
,
Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau}_{\mathrm{Sym}}(\widetilde{z})\neq\emptyset
if and only if
\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)
.

Proof.

The stack
Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau}_{\mathrm{Sym}}(\widetilde{z})
is nonempty if and only if
U(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)\neq\emptyset
. By
[
PZ13
, Theorem 9.3]
,
M_{\mathcal{J}}(\leq\lambda)_{\mathbf{F}}=\cup_{\widetilde{s}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)}S^{\circ}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{s})
. Then
U(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)\neq\emptyset
if and only if
S^{\circ}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{s})\cap{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{z})_{\mathbf{F}}
for some
\widetilde{s}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)
. The last condition is equivalent to
\widetilde{z}\in S_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{s})
for some
\widetilde{s}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)
by
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 4.7.1]
(which easily generalizes to our setup). Then the claim follows from the standard description of torus fixed points of affine Schubert varieties.
∎

4.3.
Symplectic étale
\varphi
-modules

In this subsection, we fix a dominant cocharacter
\lambda\in X_{*}(T)^{\mathcal{J}}
such that
\std(\lambda_{j})\in[0,h]^{4}
and a
1
-generic inertial tame type
\tau
.

Let
n>0
be an integer. The ring
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{E}},K}:=W(k)(\!(v)\!)^{\wedge_{p}}
is equipped with Frobenius endomorphism
\varphi
extending usual Frobenius on
W(k)
and sending
v
to
v^{p}
. For
p
-adically complete Noetherian
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra
R
,
\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},n}(R)
is defined as the groupoid of rank
n
étale
\varphi
-modules with
R
-coefficients. It is known that
\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},n}
is an ind-algebraic fppf stack over
\spf{\mathcal{O}}

[
EG23
, Corollary 3.1.5]
.

Objects of
\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},n}(R)
are given by rank
n
projective modules
{\mathcal{M}}
over
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{E}},K}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}R
equipped with an isomorphism
\phi_{\mathcal{M}}:\varphi^{*}({\mathcal{M}})\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathcal{M}}
. For each
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
, we have an induced morphism
\phi_{\mathcal{M}}^{(j)}:{\mathcal{M}}^{(j-1)}\rightarrow{\mathcal{M}}^{(j)}
. We also define
{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}\in\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},n}(R)
the dual étale
\varphi
-module of
{\mathfrak{M}}
whose underlying module is
\Hom_{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{E}},K}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}R}({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{E}},K}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}R)
and
\phi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}}:\varphi^{*}({\mathcal{M}}^{\vee})\rightarrow{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}
given by a formula

\displaystyle\phi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}}(f)(m)=\varphi(f(\phi_{{\mathcal{M}}}^{-1}(m))

for all
f\in\varphi^{*}({\mathcal{M}}^{\vee})=\Hom_{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{E}},K}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}R}(\varphi^{*}({\mathcal{M}}),{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{E}},K}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}R)
and
m\in{\mathcal{M}}
.

We define
\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}
as the moduli stack of
symplectic
étale
\varphi
-modules whose objects are given by triples
({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}},\alpha)\in\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}(R)
where
{\mathcal{M}}\in\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},4}(R)
,
{\mathcal{N}}\in\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},1}(R)
, and
\alpha:{\mathcal{M}}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\rightarrow}\limits^{\sim}}{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{E}},K}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}R}{\mathcal{N}}
satisfying alternating condition
(\alpha^{\vee}\otimes{\mathcal{N}})^{-1}\circ\alpha=-1
.

Proposition 4.3.1
.

The map
\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}\rightarrow\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},4}\times_{\mathcal{O}}\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},1}
sending
({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}},\alpha)
to
({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}})
is representable by algebraic spaces. In particular,
\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}
is an ind-algebraic fppf stack over
\spf{\mathcal{O}}
.

Proof.

This can be proven as Proposition
4.2.5
using
[
EG23
, Corollary 3.1.5]
instead of
[
CEGS22
, Proposition 3.1.3]
.
∎

Definition 4.3.2
.

Let
R
be a
p
-adically complete
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra and
({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}},\alpha)\in\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}(R)
. A
basis
of
({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}},\alpha)
is a pair
(\beta,\gamma)=((\beta^{(j)})_{{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}},(\gamma^{(j)})_{{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}})
where for each
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
,
\beta^{(j)}
(resp.
\gamma^{(j)}
) is a basis for a rank 4 (resp. 1) free
R\llbracket v\rrbracket[1/v]^{\wedge_{p}}
-module
{\mathcal{M}}^{(j)}
(resp.
{\mathcal{N}}^{(j)}
) such that

\displaystyle\alpha(\beta^{(j)})=((\beta^{(j)})^{\vee}\otimes\gamma^{(j)})J.

Lemma 4.3.3
.

Let
R
be a
p
-adically complete
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra and
({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}},\alpha)\in\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}(R)
.

(1)

If
(\beta,\gamma)
is a basis of
({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}},\alpha)
, then the matrix representation of
\phi_{\mathcal{M}}^{(j)}
(resp.
\phi_{\mathcal{N}}^{(j)}
) with respect to the basis
\beta^{(j-1)}
and
\beta^{(j)}
(resp.
\gamma^{(j-1)}
and
\gamma^{(j)}
) is given by a matrix
A^{(j)}\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R(\!(v)\!)^{\wedge_{p}})
(resp.
\simc(A)\in(R(\!(v)\!)^{\wedge_{p}})^{\times}
).

(2)

If
(\beta_{1},\gamma_{1})
and
(\beta_{2},\gamma_{2})
are basis of
({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}},\alpha)
such that
\beta_{2}^{(j)}=\beta_{1}^{(j)}A_{j}
and
\gamma_{2}^{(j)}=\gamma_{1}^{(j)}c_{j}
for some
A_{j}\in\mathrm{GL}_{4}(R(\!(v)\!)^{\wedge_{p}})
and
c_{j}\in(R(\!(v)\!)^{\wedge_{p}})^{\times}
, then
A_{j}\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R(\!(v)\!)^{\wedge_{p}})
and
c_{j}=\simc(A_{j})
.

Proof.

Item (1) can be shown as Lemma
4.2.8
. Item (2) follows from a direct computation using the condition
\alpha(\beta^{(j)})=((\beta^{(j)})^{\vee}\otimes\gamma^{(j)})J
.
∎

For a tame inertial type
\tau^{\prime}
valued in
T_{n}^{\vee}(E)
, there is a morphism
\varepsilon_{\tau^{\prime}}:Y^{[0,h],\tau^{\prime}}_{n}\rightarrow\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},n}
representable by algebraic spaces, proper, and of finite presentation (
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 5.4.1]
).

Given
{\mathfrak{M}}\in Y^{[0,h],\tau^{\prime}}_{n}(R)
and an integer
m\geq 0
, we define
{\mathfrak{M}}(m)\in Y^{[m,h+m],\tau^{\prime}}_{n}(R)
to be the Breuil–Kisin module whose underlying module is
{\mathfrak{M}}
and the Frobenius endomorphism is given by
\phi_{{\mathfrak{M}}(m)}=E(v)^{m}\phi_{{\mathfrak{M}}}
.

We define a morphism of ind-algebraic fppf stacks over
\spf{\mathcal{O}}

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\tau}:Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}

\displaystyle({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)

\displaystyle\mapsto(\varepsilon_{\std(\tau)}({\mathfrak{M}}),\varepsilon_{\simc(\tau)}({\mathfrak{N}}(h)),\varepsilon_{\std(\tau)}(\alpha)).

Remark 4.3.4
.

Note that the dual of Breuil–Kisin module and étale
\varphi
-module are not compatible. This is because of
E(v)^{h}
in the formula defining the Frobenius of dual Breuil–Kisin modules. This is why we use
\varepsilon_{\simc(\tau)}({\mathfrak{N}}(h))
instead of
\varepsilon_{\simc(\tau)}({\mathfrak{N}})
in the definition of
\varepsilon_{\tau}
, so that we still have
\varepsilon_{\tau}({\mathfrak{M}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathfrak{N}})=\varepsilon_{\std(\tau)}({\mathfrak{M}})^{\vee}\otimes\varepsilon_{\simc(\tau)}({\mathfrak{N}}(h))
. In particular, this explains that
\varepsilon_{\std(\tau)}(\alpha)
is well-defined.

Proposition 4.3.5
.

Suppose that
\tau
is
(h+1)
-generic. The map
\varepsilon_{\tau}:Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}\rightarrow\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}
is a closed immersion.

Proof.

By
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 5.4.3]
,
\varepsilon_{\tau}:Y^{[0,h],\tau}_{4}\rightarrow\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},4}
is a monomorphism. Then
\varepsilon_{\tau}
is fully faithful by
[
Sta18
,
Tag 04ZZ
]
. As a result, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{[0,h],\tau}_{4}\times_{\spf{\mathcal{O}}}Y^{0,\simc(\tau)}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},4}\times_{\spf{\mathcal{O}}}\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{\std(\tau)}\times\varepsilon_{\simc(\tau)}}

is cartesian, and the claim follows from
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 5.4.3]
.
∎

Lemma 4.3.6
.

Let
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)\in Y^{[0,h],\tau}_{\mathrm{Sym}}(R)
and
({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}},\varepsilon_{\tau}(\alpha))=\varepsilon_{\tau}(({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha))
. If
(\beta,\gamma)
is an eigenbasis of
({\mathfrak{M}},{\mathfrak{N}},\alpha)
, there exists a basis
({\mathfrak{b}},{\mathfrak{c}})
of
({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}},\varepsilon_{\tau}(\alpha))
determined by
(\beta,\gamma)
such that
\phi_{{\mathcal{M}}}^{(j)}
with respect to
{\mathfrak{b}}
is given by
A^{(j)}_{{\mathfrak{M}},\beta}s_{j}^{-1}v^{\mu_{j}+\eta_{j}}
.

Proof.

The existence of
{\mathfrak{b}}
follows from
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 5.4.2]
. Then there is a unique
{\mathfrak{c}}
such that
({\mathfrak{b}},{\mathfrak{c}})
is a basis of
({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}},\varepsilon_{\tau}(\alpha))
.
∎

Let
a\leq b
be integers. There is a natural map

\displaystyle\iota^{\prime}_{\widetilde{z}}:\prod_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}(L^{[a,b]}\mathrm{GSp}_{4})_{\mathbf{F}}\widetilde{z}_{j}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}

\displaystyle(A^{(j)}\widetilde{z}_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}

\displaystyle\mapsto({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}},\alpha)

where
{\mathcal{M}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{N}}
) is a free rank
4
(resp. 1) étale
\varphi
-module such that
\phi_{\mathcal{M}}^{(j)}
(resp.
\phi_{\mathcal{N}}^{(j)}
) with respect to the standard basis is given by
A^{(j)}\widetilde{z}_{j}
(resp.
\simc(A^{(j)}\widetilde{z}_{j})
) and
\alpha
is given by the matrix
J
with respect to the standard basis of
{\mathcal{M}}
and its dual basis.
We also define a closed subscheme

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{Fl}}^{[a,b]}_{{\mathcal{J}},\widetilde{z}}:=\prod_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}({\mathcal{I}}_{1,\mathbf{F}}{\backslash}(L^{[a,b]}\mathrm{GSp}_{4})_{\mathbf{F}}\widetilde{z}_{j})\subset\widetilde{\mathrm{Fl}}^{\mathcal{J}}.

We denote by
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathbf{F}}\mathrm{\text{-}conj}
a
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathbf{F}}
-action on
\widetilde{\mathrm{Fl}}^{[a,b]}_{{\mathcal{J}},\widetilde{z}}
given by

\displaystyle(D_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}\cdot({\mathcal{I}}_{1,\mathbf{F}}A^{(j)}\widetilde{z}_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}=(D_{j}{\mathcal{I}}_{1,\mathbf{F}}A^{(j)}\widetilde{z}_{j}D_{j-1}^{-1})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}

for
(D_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}\in T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathbf{F}}
and
({\mathcal{I}}_{1,\mathbf{F}}A^{(j)}\widetilde{z}_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}\in\widetilde{\mathrm{Fl}}^{[a,b]}_{{\mathcal{J}},\widetilde{z}}
.

Proposition 4.3.7
.

Suppose that
\widetilde{z}=\sigma^{-1}t_{\nu+\eta}
where
\nu
is
(b-a+1)
-deep in
\underline{C}_{0}
. Then the morphism
\iota^{\prime}_{\widetilde{z}}
induces a monomorphism

\displaystyle\iota_{\widetilde{z}}:[\widetilde{\mathrm{Fl}}^{[a,b]}_{{\mathcal{J}},\widetilde{z}}/T_{\mathbf{F}}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}\mathrm{\text{-}conj}]\hookrightarrow\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.3.3
and
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 5.4.4]
, the morphism
\iota^{\prime}_{\widetilde{z}}
factors through a monomorphism

\displaystyle[\prod_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}(L^{[a,b]}\mathrm{GSp}_{4})_{\mathbf{F}}\widetilde{z}_{j}/_{\varphi}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{F}}]\hookrightarrow\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}},

and the source is isomorphic to
[\widetilde{\mathrm{Fl}}^{[a,b]}_{{\mathcal{J}},\widetilde{z}}/T_{\mathbf{F}}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}\mathrm{\text{-}conj}]
by Lemma 5.2.2 in
loc. cit.
.
∎

By combining Proposition
4.2.12
, Lemma
4.3.6
, and Proposition
4.3.7
, we obtain the following result.

Proposition 4.3.8
.

Let
a\leq b
,
h\geq 0
be integers and
\widetilde{z}=\sigma^{-1}t_{\nu+\eta}\in\widetilde{W}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}
such that
\nu
is
(b-a+1)
-deep in
\underline{C}_{0}
. Suppose that
(\mathrm{Gr}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mathbf{F}}^{[0,h],{\mathcal{J}}})\widetilde{w}^{*}(\tau)\subset\mathrm{Fl}^{[a,b]}_{{\mathcal{J}},\widetilde{z}}
. Then we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{M}_{\mathcal{J}}(\leq\lambda)_{\mathbf{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{Gr}}_{{\mathcal{G}},\mathbf{F}}^{\left[0,h\right],{\mathcal{J}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{Fl}}^{\left[a,b\right]}_{{\mathcal{J}},\widetilde{z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\widetilde{\mathrm{Fl}}^{\left[a,b\right]}_{{\mathcal{J}},\widetilde{z}}/T_{\mathbf{F}}^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}\text{-}\mathrm{conj}\right]\ }
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\left[0,h\right],\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{\widetilde{w}^{*}(\tau)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{(s,\mu)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\widetilde{z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{\tau}}

where all hooked arrows are closed immersions.

4.4.
Local models for potentially crystalline stacks

We have a canonical morphism
\varepsilon_{\infty}:{\mathcal{X}}_{n}\rightarrow\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},n}
constructed in
[
EG23
, Proposition 3.7.2]
which corresponds to restricting
G_{K}
-representations to
G_{K_{\infty}}
-representations when evaluated at complete Noetherian finite local
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras. We have an induced map
\varepsilon_{\infty}:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathrm{Sym}}\rightarrow\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}
.

Proposition 4.4.1
.

Suppose
\tau
is
(h+2)
-generic. Then the map
\varepsilon_{\infty}:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}\rightarrow\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}
is a monomorphism.

Proof.

This follows from
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 7.2.11]
using the argument given in the proof of Proposition
4.3.5

∎

We define
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
(resp.
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{reg}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
) to be the union of
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\lambda^{\prime},\tau}
for all dominant (resp. regular dominant) cocharacters
\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda
. Similarly, we have
{\mathcal{X}}_{4}^{\leq\lambda^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}}
.

Recall the
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat closed substack
Y^{[0,h],\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}_{4}
(resp.
Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}_{4}
) of
Y^{[0,h],\tau}_{4}
(resp.
Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau}_{4}
) defined in
[
LLHLMa
, §7.2]
. It’s key property is that if
\tau
is
(h+2)
-generic, then
{\mathcal{X}}^{[0,h],\tau}_{4}\rightarrow\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},4}
factors through
Y^{[0,h],\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}_{4}
and induces isomorphisms
{\mathcal{X}}^{[0,h],\tau}_{4}\simeq Y^{[0,h],\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}_{4}
and
{\mathcal{X}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}_{4}\simeq Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}_{4}
. We define
Y^{[0,h],\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}_{\mathrm{Sym}}
(resp.
Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}_{\mathrm{Sym}}
) to be the
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat part of
Y^{[0,h],\tau}_{\mathrm{Sym}}\times_{Y^{[0,h],\tau}_{4}}Y^{[0,h],\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}_{4}
(resp.
Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau}_{\mathrm{Sym}}\times_{Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau}_{4}}Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}_{4}
). We have the following result.

Proposition 4.4.2
.

Suppose that
\tau
is
(h+2)
-generic. The map
\varepsilon_{\infty}
factors through
Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}
and induces isomorphisms
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}\simeq Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}\simeq Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}
.

Proof.

Since
\varepsilon_{\infty}
and
\varepsilon_{\tau}
are fully faithful, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau}

\displaystyle\simeq\left(({\mathcal{X}}_{4}^{[0,h],\tau}\times{\mathcal{X}}_{1}^{[0,2h],\simc(\tau)})\times_{\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},4}\times\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},1}}\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}\right)_{{\mathcal{O}}\mathrm{\text{-}flat}}

\displaystyle Y_{\mathrm{Sym}}^{[0,h],\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\simeq\left((Y_{4}^{[0,h],\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}\times Y_{1}^{[0,2h],\simc(\tau)})\times_{\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},4}\times\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},1}}\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}\right)_{{\mathcal{O}}\mathrm{\text{-}flat}}

where the subscript
{{\mathcal{O}}\mathrm{\text{-}flat}}
means taking
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat part. Then the claim follows from the corresponding result for
\mathrm{GL}_{4}\times\mathrm{GL}_{1}
in
[
LLHLMa
, Prop 7.2.3]
.
∎

We assume that
\tau
is
(h+2)
-generic until the end of this subsection. By using the previous Proposition, we consider
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{[0,h],\tau}
as a closed substack of
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{[0,h],\tau}
.

Before stating the main result of this subsection, we introduce some notations. Recall that
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
is the union of
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})
for
\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)
. We define

\displaystyle Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}(\widetilde{z})\subset Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau,\nabla_{\infty}},\ \ {\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\lambda,\tau},\ \ {\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{reg}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{reg}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}

to be the open substacks obtained by taking intersection with
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})
inside
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
. We also define
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
-torsors

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{\tau,\infty})\rightarrow Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}_{\mathrm{Sym}}(\widetilde{z}),\ \ \widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})\rightarrow{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z}),\ \ \widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{reg}}}^{\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})\rightarrow{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{reg}}}^{\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})

by taking pullback along the
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
-torsor
\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)^{\wedge_{p}}\rightarrow Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau}_{\mathrm{Sym}}(\widetilde{z})
.

We define
M_{{\mathcal{J}},{\mathrm{reg}}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})
to be the union of
M_{\mathcal{J}}(\lambda^{\prime},\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})
for all regular dominant cocharacters
\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda
. Note that
M_{{\mathcal{J}},{\mathrm{reg}}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})
is a closed subvariety of
M_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})
which is in turn contained in
M_{\mathcal{J}}(\leq\lambda)
. We define open subschemes

\displaystyle U^{\mathrm{nv}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})\subset M_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})\ \ \text{and}\ \ U_{{\mathrm{reg}}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})\subset M_{{\mathcal{J}},{\mathrm{reg}}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})

by taking intersection with the open subscheme
U(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)\subset M_{\mathcal{J}}(\leq\lambda)
. We also define
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
-torsors

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})\rightarrow U^{\mathrm{nv}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})\ \ \text{and}\ \ U_{{\mathrm{reg}}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})\rightarrow\widetilde{U}_{{\mathrm{reg}}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}})

by taking pullback along the trivial
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
-torsor
\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)\rightarrow U(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)
.

To the lowest alcove presentation
(s,\mu)
of
\tau
, we associate tuples of integers
{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}=({\mathbf{a}}_{\tau,j^{\prime}})_{j^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}}\in({\mathcal{O}}^{3})^{{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime}}
given by
{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau,j^{\prime}}:=(s^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{or}},j^{\prime}})^{-1}({\mathbf{a}}^{\prime})^{(j^{\prime})}/(1-p^{f^{\prime}})
. We remark that if
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
and
j\equiv j^{\prime}\bmod f
, then
{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau,j^{\prime}}\equiv s_{j}^{-1}(\mu_{j}+\eta_{j})\bmod\varpi
(see
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 7.3.1]
).

Theorem 4.4.3
.

(1)

We have the following commutative diagram of
p
-adic formal algebraic stacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{reg}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})}

\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}(\mathbf{F})

For the proof of Theorem
4.4.3
, we need the following Lemma. Its proof is given in §
5.4
.

Lemma 4.4.4
.

Suppose that
\tau
is
(h+16)
-generic. Then
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})\neq\emptyset
if and only if
\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)
.

Proof of Theorem
4.4.3
.

Since the proof is completely analogous to that of
[
LLHLMa
, Theorem 7.3.2]
, we briefly sketch the proof.

(1)

Let
\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)^{\wedge_{p}}=\spf R
. By Proposition 7.1.6 in
loc. cit.
, the composite
\spf R\rightarrow Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau}_{\mathrm{Sym}}\rightarrow Y_{4}^{\leq\lambda^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}}
provides an ideal
I_{\nabla_{\infty}}\leq R
such that
\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{\infty})=\spf R/I_{\nabla_{\infty}}
. By Proposition 7.1.10 of
loc. cit.
, there is an inclusion

\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{\tau,\infty})\times_{\mathcal{O}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}/p\subset\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})
of subschemes in
\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)
.
As an application of Proposition 3.3.9 in
loc. cit.
, we can lift the map

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{\tau,\infty})\times_{\mathcal{O}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}/p\rightarrow\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})\rightarrow{U}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})

to
\spec R/I_{\nabla_{\infty}}\rightarrow{U}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})
at the cost of a genericity assumption on
\mu
. (The Proposition 3.3.9 in
loc. cit.
, which uses Elkik’s approximation theorem, easily generalizes to our setup; its proof only uses the fact that open charts of universal local models are affine and smooth after inverting
v
which follows from Proposition
3.2.7
in our case.) We can also lift the map

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{\tau,\infty})\times_{\mathcal{O}}\spec{\mathcal{O}}/p\rightarrow\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})\rightarrow T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}

to
\spec R/I_{\nabla_{\infty}}\rightarrow T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
because of smoothness of
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
. By combining with the previous map, we obtain a map
\spec R/I_{\nabla_{\infty}}\rightarrow\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})
which induces the shorter dotted arrow because
R/I_{\nabla_{\infty}}
is
p
-adically complete. It is moreover a closed immersion because it is closed immersion modulo
p
. Since
\widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{reg}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})
(resp.
\widetilde{U}_{\mathrm{reg}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})^{\wedge_{p}}
) is
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat and equals to the union of top(
=1+7\#{\mathcal{J}}
)-dimensional irreducible components of
\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{\tau,\infty})
(resp. the
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat locus of
\widetilde{U}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})
), the longer dotted arrow is induced by the shorter dotted arrow.

(2)

Since both
\widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{reg}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})
and
\widetilde{U}_{\mathrm{reg}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})^{\wedge_{p}}
are reduced
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat
p
-adic formal schemes equidimensional of dimension
1+7\#{\mathcal{J}}
, the longer dotted arrow is an isomorphism if both
\widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{reg}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})
and
\widetilde{U}_{\mathrm{reg}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})^{\wedge_{p}}
have the same number of irreducible component. We apply Theorem
3.4.1
for all regular dominant
\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda
. Then there exists a polynomial
P\in\mathbf{Z}[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
only depending on
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}}
and the ramification index
e
of
{\mathcal{O}}/\mathbf{Z}_{p}
such that if
P({\mathbf{a}}_{\tau,j})\neq 0\bmod p
for all
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
,
U(\widetilde{z},\lambda^{\prime},\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})
is unibranch at
\widetilde{z}
and
U(\widetilde{z},\lambda^{\prime},\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})^{\wedge_{p}}
is irreducible for all regular dominant
\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda
. By Corollary
4.2.17
. this implies that the number of irreducible component of
\widetilde{U}_{\mathrm{reg}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})^{\wedge_{p}}
is at most

\displaystyle\#\{\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda\mid\text{$\lambda^{\prime}$ regular dominant, $\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda^{\prime})$}\}.

On the other hand, Lemma
4.4.4
implies that the number of irreducible component of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{reg}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})
is at least

\displaystyle\#\{\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda\mid\text{$\lambda^{\prime}$ regular dominant, $\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda^{\prime})$}\}.

Thus the longer dotted arrow is an isomorphism.

Suppose that
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}(\mathbf{F})
. For regular dominant
\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda
, we let
n_{\lambda^{\prime}}
be the number of minimal primes in any versal ring to
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\lambda^{\prime},\tau}
at
\overline{\rho}
. If
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\mathbf{F})
is tame, it’s image in
Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\mathbf{F})
is a triple
({\mathfrak{M}}_{\overline{\rho}},{\mathfrak{N}}_{\overline{\rho}},\alpha_{\overline{\rho}})
where
{\mathfrak{M}}_{\overline{\rho}}
is a semisimple Breuil–Kisin module of some shape
\widetilde{z}
by
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 5.5.7]
. Then
{\mathfrak{M}}_{\overline{\rho}}\in Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}(\widetilde{z})(\mathbf{F})
lifts to an element in
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}(\mathbf{F})\widetilde{z}\subset\widetilde{U}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda)(\mathbf{F})
. Thus, we can take a versal ring to
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
at
\overline{\rho}
which is also a versal ring to
\widetilde{U}_{\mathrm{reg}}(\widetilde{z},\leq\lambda^{\prime},\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})
at
\widetilde{z}
. By the previous paragraph, the number of minimum primes of this versal ring is exactly

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda}n_{\lambda^{\prime}}=\#\{\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda\mid\text{$\lambda^{\prime}$ regular dominant, $\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda^{\prime})$}\}.

By induction on
\lambda
, we can show that
n_{\lambda}=1
if and only if
\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)
and
n_{\lambda}=0
otherwise.

∎

4.5.
Potentially crystalline stacks modulo
p

Let
\lambda\in X_{*}(T^{\vee})^{\mathcal{J}}
be a regular dominant cocharacter such that
\std(\lambda)\subset([0,h]^{4})^{\mathcal{J}}
. Let
\tau
be a tame inertial type with
(h+2)
-generic lowest alcove presentation
(s,\mu)
which is
\lambda
-compatible with
\zeta\in X^{*}(Z)^{{\mathcal{J}}}
. Recall that both
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda,\tau}
are equidimensional algebraic stacks over
\mathbf{F}
of dimension
4f
. Thus
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda,\tau}
is topologically a union of irreducible components of
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}
, which are labelled by Serre weights.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
. We define algebraic stacks
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\sigma}
and
\widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
by the following cartesian diagram

(4.5.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{M}_{\mathcal{J}}(\leq\lambda)_{\mathbf{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\left(s,\mu\right)}}

If
\mu
is
(2h-2)
-deep in
\underline{C}_{0}
, then the closed immersion
\widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{M}_{\mathcal{J}}(\leq\lambda)_{\mathbf{F}}
factors through
\widetilde{M}_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})_{\mathbf{F}}
by
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 7.1.10]
.
Recall that by Theorem
3.6.2
,
7f
-dimensional irreducible components of
\widetilde{M}_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})_{\mathbf{F}}
are exactly
\widetilde{C}_{\sigma}^{\zeta}\widetilde{w}(\tau)^{*}
for all
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(W(\phi^{-1}(\lambda)-\eta)\otimes\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
.

The following Theorem describes the underlying reduced substack of
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
.

Theorem 4.5.2
.

Let
\lambda,\tau
be as above. We assume that
\mu
is
(h+16)
-deep in
\underline{C}_{0}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left({\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{reg}}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}\right)_{\mathrm{red}}=\left({\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\lambda,\tau}\right)_{\mathrm{red}}=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(W(\phi^{-1}(\lambda)-\eta)\otimes\overline{\sigma}(\tau))}{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}.

We need the following Lemma, whose proof is given in §
5.4
.

Lemma 4.5.3
.

Suppose that
\mu
is
(h+16)
-deep in
\underline{C}_{0}
. Then for each
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(W(\phi^{-1}(\lambda)-\eta)\otimes\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
, we have
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda,\tau}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
.

Proof of Theorem
4.5.2
.

Since
\mu
is
(h+16)
-deep in
\underline{C}_{0}
, the Lemma
4.5.3
shows that
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda,\tau}
(resp.
\widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda,\tau}
) has at least
\#\mathrm{JH}(W(\phi^{-1}(\lambda)-\eta)\otimes\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
many irreducible components of dimension
4f
(resp.
7f
). On the other hand, the number of
7f
-dimensional irreducible components of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}
is at most that of
\widetilde{M}_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})_{\mathbf{F}}
, which is
\#\mathrm{JH}(W(\phi^{-1}(\lambda)-\eta)\otimes\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
by Theorem
3.6.2
. This proves our claim.
∎

Theorem 4.5.4
.

Let
(s,\mu)
be a
(h+3)
-generic lowest alcove presentation of
\tau
which is
(\lambda-\eta)
-compatible with
\zeta\in X^{*}(\underline{Z})
. If
\sigma
is a Serre weight such that
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\lambda,\tau}
, then
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(W(\phi^{-1}(\lambda)-\eta)\otimes\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
and we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{C}_{\sigma}^{\zeta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{M}_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})_{\mathbf{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{M}_{\mathcal{J}}(\leq\lambda)_{\mathbf{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{Fl}}_{{\mathcal{J}},\widetilde{w}^{*}\left(\tau\right)}^{[0,h]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{X}}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\leq\lambda,\tau}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\widetilde{\mathrm{Fl}}_{{\mathcal{J}},\widetilde{w}^{*}\left(\tau\right)}^{[0,h]}/T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathbf{F}}\text{-}\mathrm{conj}\right]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K,\mathbf{F}}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathbf{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathbf{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{\widetilde{w}^{*}\left(\tau\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathbf{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathbf{F}}}

where all rectangles are cartesian, all vertical arrows labelled by
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathbf{F}}
are
T^{\vee,{\mathcal{J}}}_{\mathbf{F}}
-torsors, and all hooked arrows are closed immersion. The bottom diagonal map is the composition of canonical morphisms
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{\infty}}\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K,\mathbf{F}}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}
. Furthermore, if
(s,\mu)
is
(h+16)
-generic, then the above diagram holds for all
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(W(\phi^{-1}(\lambda)-\eta)\otimes\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.3.8
and (
4.5.1
), we get the diagram except the top diagonal arrow. The image of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\sigma}
in
\widetilde{M}_{\mathcal{J}}^{\mathrm{nv}}(\leq\lambda,\nabla_{{\mathbf{a}}_{\tau}})_{\mathbf{F}}
is a top dimensional irreducible component. By Theorem
3.6.2
, it is equal to
\widetilde{C}_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\zeta}\widetilde{w}^{*}(\tau)^{-1}
for some
\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathrm{JH}(W(\phi^{-1}(\lambda)-\eta)\otimes\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
. We claim that
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma
. Let
(\widetilde{w},\omega)
be a lowest alcove presentation of
\sigma^{\prime}
compatible with
\zeta
. We write
\kappa=\pi^{-1}(\widetilde{w})\cdot(\omega-\eta)
so that
\sigma^{\prime}=F(\kappa)
. Note that
\kappa
is 3-deep in its alcove by Proposition
2.5.6
. Also, we write
\widetilde{w}=t_{\nu_{w}}w
. We can and do choose a triple
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2},\widetilde{s})
such that
\widetilde{s}=t_{\nu_{s}}s
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{1}=\widetilde{w},\ \widetilde{w}_{2}=\widetilde{w}_{h}\widetilde{w}_{1},\ \widetilde{s}\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0)=\omega,\ w_{j}s_{j}^{-1}w_{j-1}^{-1}=1.

Then
C_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\zeta}=S^{\nabla_{0}}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2},\widetilde{s})
which contains a dense open subscheme
U_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\zeta}=S^{\circ}_{\mathbf{F}}(\widetilde{w}_{1},\widetilde{w}_{2},\widetilde{s})^{\nabla_{0}}
. Let
\widetilde{U}_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\zeta}\subset\widetilde{C}_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\zeta}
be the preimage of
U_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\zeta}
. By
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 7.4.6]
, the image of
\widetilde{U}_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{\zeta}
in
\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}
consists of
({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}},\alpha)
such that
{\mathbb{V}}^{*}_{K}({\mathcal{M}})
has the form

(4.5.5)

\displaystyle{\begin{pmatrix}\chi_{1}&*&*&*\\
0&\chi_{2}&*&*\\
0&0&\chi_{3}&*\\
0&0&0&\chi_{4}\end{pmatrix}}

where
\chi_{i}=\prod_{{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}}\omega_{K,\sigma_{j}}^{(\kappa_{j}+\eta_{j})_{i}}|_{I_{K_{\infty}}}
. On the other hand,
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\sigma}
has an open dense subscheme
\widetilde{{\mathcal{U}}}_{\sigma}
whose
\mathbf{F}
-points are maximally nonsplit of niveau 1 and of weight
\sigma
. Let
\overline{\rho}
be an
\mathbf{F}
-point in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{U}}}_{\sigma}\cap\widetilde{U}_{\sigma}^{\prime\zeta}\widetilde{w}^{*}(\tau)^{-1}
. Since
\overline{\rho}
is 3-generic, the
G_{K_{\infty}}
-stable standard flag of
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{K_{\infty}}}
given by (
4.5.5
) is also
G_{K}
-stable (
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 7.2.10(4)]
). Then the condition on
\chi_{i}
and
\overline{\rho}
being maximally nonsplit of niveau 1 and of weight
\sigma
imply that
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma
. The last assertion follows from Theorem
4.5.2
.
∎

Proposition 4.5.6
.

Let
\overline{\rho}
be a 22-generic tame
L
-parameter over
\mathbf{F}
. Let
W_{g}(\overline{\rho})
be a set of 3-deep Serre weights
\sigma
such that
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{F})
.

(1)

We have
W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{\rho})\subset W_{g}(\overline{\rho})\subset W^{?}(\overline{\rho})
.

(2)

Let
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\omega)
be a lowest alcove presentation of
\sigma\in W^{?}(\overline{\rho})
compatible with
\zeta
. For each
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
, let
P_{\widetilde{w}_{1,j}}
be the polynomial in Theorem
3.7.1
. If
P_{\widetilde{w}_{1,j}}(\omega_{j})\neq 0\bmod p
for all
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
, then
\sigma\in W_{g}(\overline{\rho})

Proof.

For any
\sigma\in W^{?}(\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})
, we can use Lemma
2.5.14
and
[
LLHLMa
, Remark 2.1.8]
to find a tame inertial
L
-parameter
\tau
with a lowest alcove representation such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}},\tau)\in{\mathrm{Adm}}(\eta)
and
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
. Since
\overline{\rho}
is 22-generic, such
\tau
is 19-generic. By applying Theorem
4.5.4
to such
\tau
,
\lambda=\eta
, and
\sigma
, we get
W_{g}(\overline{\rho})=W_{g}^{\zeta}(\overline{\rho})
. Then the claim follows from Theorem
3.7.2
.
∎

5.
Global setup

5.1.
Some local Galois deformation rings

Let
\widehat{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
be the category of completed Noetherian local
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras with residue field
\mathbf{F}
.

Let
F
be either a number field or a local field.
Let
\overline{\rho}:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
be a continuous representation. We let
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\square}
be the framed deformation ring representing the functor
D^{\square}_{\overline{\rho}}
taking
A\in\widehat{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
to the set of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(A)
-valued lifts
\rho
of
\overline{\rho}
. If
\psi:G_{F}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
is a character lifting
\simc(\overline{\rho})
, we write
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\square,\psi}
for the fixed similitude deformation ring representing
D^{\square,\psi}_{\overline{\rho}}
taking
A\in\widehat{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
to the set of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(A)
-valued lifts
\rho
with
\simc(\rho)=\psi\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}A
.

5.1.1.
Local deformations:
l=p

Suppose that
F=K
. Given a type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
, we denote by
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
the unique
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat quotient of
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\square}
whose
A
-points, for any
{\mathcal{O}}
-flat
A\in\widehat{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
, are lattices in potentially crystalline representations with Hodge–Tate weight
\lambda+\eta
and tame inertial type
\tau
. We have its version with fixed similitude character
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,\psi}
. Note that
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,\psi}
is non-zero only if
\psi
is potentially crystalline of type
(\simc(\lambda+\eta),\simc(\tau))
. We record the following Lemma relating a potentially crystalline deformation ring to its fixed similitude variants.

Lemma 5.1.2
.

Recall that
p>2
. Twisting by the universal unramified twist

\displaystyle\mathrm{ur}_{1+x}:G_{K}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}\llbracket X\rrbracket

sending
\Frob_{K}
to
1+x
induces an isomorphism
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}\simeq R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,\psi}\llbracket X\rrbracket
.

Proof.

This can be proven as
[
EG14
, Lemma 4.3.1]
.
∎

We also write
R_{\std(\overline{\rho})}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
to denote the potentially crystalline (
\mathrm{GL}_{4}
-)deformation ring of
\std(\overline{\rho})
and type
(\std(\lambda+\eta),\std(\tau))
.

We prove that certain potentially crystalline deformation ring of tame
\overline{\rho}:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
is nonzero and a domain. We have the following necessary condition for
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}\neq 0
under a mild assumption on
\overline{\rho}
and
\tau
.

Proposition 5.1.3
.

Let
\overline{\rho}:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
be a continuous representation and
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
be a type.

(1)

If
\tau
is not
(h_{\lambda+\eta}+1)
-generic and
\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}}
is
\max\{2h_{\lambda+\eta},22\}
-generic, then
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}=0
.

(2)

If
\tau
has a lowest alcove presentation
(s,\mu)
with
\mu

(h_{\lambda+\eta}+1)
-deep in
\underline{C}_{0}
and
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}\neq 0
, then
\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}}
has a
\phi^{-1}(\lambda)
-compatible lowest alcove presentation such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)\in{\mathrm{Adm}}(\phi^{-1}(\lambda)+\eta)
.

Proof.

By
[
Enn19
, Lemma 5]
, if
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}\neq 0
, then
R_{\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}\neq 0
. Thus, we may assume that
\overline{\rho}=\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}}
.

(1)

Note that
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}=0
if
R_{\std(\overline{\rho})}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}=0
. Then the latter follows from a mild strengthening of
[
Enn19
, Proposition 7]
(as explained in the proof of
[
LLHLMa
, Corollary 8.5.2]
).

(2)

If
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}\neq 0
, then
R_{\std(\overline{\rho})}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}\neq 0
. Then the claim follows from the corresponding result for
\mathrm{GL}_{4}
(
[
LLHLMa
, Corollary 5.5.8]
) and Lemma
2.1.6
. ∎

There is a Coxeter length function
l
on
\underline{W}_{a}
which can be extended to
\underline{\widetilde{W}}\simeq\underline{W}_{a}\rtimes\underline{\Omega}
by setting
l(\widetilde{w}\delta)=l(\widetilde{w})
for
\widetilde{w}\in\underline{W}_{a}
and
\delta\in\underline{\Omega}
. It is expected that if
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}\neq 0
, the complexity of
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
increases as the length of
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)
decreases. In the special case that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)
has the
maximal
length, i.e.
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)\in\underline{W}(\phi^{-1}(\lambda)+\eta)
, we can compute
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
explicitly under a genericity assumption.

Theorem 5.1.4
.

Let
\overline{\rho}:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
be a tame representation. Let
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
be a type and
(s,\mu)
be a
(2h_{\lambda+\eta}+1)
-generic lowest alcove presentation of
\tau
. If there is a lowest alcove presentation
(s_{\overline{\rho}},\mu_{\overline{\rho}})
of
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{K}}
such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)\in\underline{W}(\lambda+\eta)
, then
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
is formally smooth over
{\mathcal{O}}
with
4f+11
variables. Moreover, any
\rho:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}})
of type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
lifting
\overline{\rho}
is potentially diagonalizable.

An analogue of Theorem
5.1.4
for the group
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
and
\lambda=0
is proven in
[
LLHL19
, Theorem 3.4.1]
. We first prove the following generalization of
loc. cit.
for any dominant
\lambda
. Then Theorem
5.1.4
follows almost immediately.

Theorem 5.1.5
.

Let
\overline{\rho}:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbf{F})
be a semisimple representation. Let
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
be a type (for
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
) and
(s,\mu)\in\underline{W}_{n}\times X^{*}(\underline{T}_{n})
be a
(2h_{\lambda+\eta}+1)
-generic lowest alcove presentation of
\tau
. If there is a lowest alcove presentation
(s_{\overline{\rho}},\mu_{\overline{\rho}})
of
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{K}}
such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)\in\underline{W_{n}}(\lambda+\eta^{\prime})
, then
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau}
is formally smooth over
{\mathcal{O}}
with
\frac{n(n-1)f}{2}+n^{2}
variables. Moreover, any
\rho:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathcal{O}})
of type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
lifting
\overline{\rho}
is potentially diagonalizable.

Proof.

We first claim that
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{n}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{F})
. By
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 5.5.7]
, there exists
\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}\in Y_{n}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau}(\mathbf{F})
, semisimple of shape
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)^{*}=w(\lambda+\eta^{\prime})
for some
w\in\underline{W}_{n}
, such that
\overline{\rho}|_{G_{K_{\infty}}}\simeq T^{*}_{dd}(\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}})
. Then the claim follows from Proposition 7.2.3 in
loc. cit.
.

Let
\overline{\beta}
be a gauge basis for
\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}
. Then we have

\displaystyle A_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}},\overline{\beta}}^{(j)}=D_{j}(v+p)^{w_{j}(\lambda+\eta^{\prime})}

for some
D_{j}\in T_{n}^{\vee}(\mathbf{F})
.
We let
R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau,\overline{\beta}}
be the deformation ring that generalizes
R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\tau,\overline{\beta},\nabla}
in
[
LLHL19
]
, which parametrizes lifts of
(\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}},\overline{\beta})
of height bounded by
\eta^{\prime}
and satisfying the monodromy condition over the generic fiber, by replacing
\eta^{\prime}
with
\lambda+\eta^{\prime}
.
Then
R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau,\overline{\beta}}
is a versal ring to
Y^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau,\nabla_{\infty}}_{n}
at
\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}
.
As in Proposition 3.4.8 in
loc. cit.
, the universal partial Frobenius matrices has the form

\displaystyle A^{(j),\mathrm{univ},\nabla}=D_{j}^{\mathrm{univ}}(v+p)^{w_{j}(\lambda+\eta^{\prime})}U^{(j),\mathrm{univ},\nabla}

for
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
where
D^{\mathrm{univ}}_{j}\in T_{n}(R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau,\overline{\beta}})
lifting
D_{j}
and
w_{j}^{-1}U^{(j),\mathrm{univ},\nabla}w_{j}\in\overline{U}_{n}(R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau,\overline{\beta}})
. In addition, for any root
\alpha<0
,

\displaystyle\left(w_{j}^{-1}U^{(j),\mathrm{univ},\nabla}w_{j}\right)_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=v^{\delta_{w_{j}(\alpha)<0}}f_{\alpha}^{(j)}(v)

where
f_{\alpha}^{(j)}\in R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau,\overline{\beta}}[v]
is a polynomial of degree
<-\langle\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\alpha^{\vee}\rangle
and is zero modulo the maximal ideal of
R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau,\overline{\beta}}
. Moreover, as in the proof of Proposition 3.4.12 in
loc. cit.
(this is where we need
(2h_{\lambda+\eta}+1)
-genericity of
(s,\mu)
),
f_{\alpha}^{(j)}\bmod\varpi
is determined by its top degree coefficient. In particular, this implies that
R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau,\overline{\beta}}
is a quotient of a power series ring over
{\mathcal{O}}
in
\frac{n(n+1)f}{2}
variables.

It follows from the diagram (3.16) in
[
LLHL19
]
that there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime}}\llbracket x_{1},\dots,x_{nf}\rrbracket\simeq R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau,\overline{\beta}}\llbracket y_{1},\dots,y_{n^{2}}\rrbracket.

The former is of dimension
\frac{n(n+1)f}{2}+n^{2}+1
where the latter is quotient of a power series ring over
{\mathcal{O}}
in
\frac{n(n+1)f}{2}+n^{2}
. This shows that
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime}}
is formally smooth over
{\mathcal{O}}
of relative dimension
\frac{n(n-1)f}{2}+n^{2}
.
We have
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime}}\simeq R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta^{\prime}}
as the universal partial Frobenius matrices
A^{(j),\mathrm{univ},\nabla}
has elementary divisor exactly
(v+p)^{\lambda+\eta^{\prime}}
.

Finally, we prove that any
\rho:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathcal{O}})
of type
(\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau)
lifting
\overline{\rho}
is potentially diagonalizable. Let
\rho^{\prime}:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathcal{O}})
be a lift of
\overline{\rho}
of type
(\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau)
whose associated Breuil–Kisin module is contained in the locus of
\spec R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta^{\prime},\tau,\overline{\beta}}
given by the condition
U^{(j^{\prime}),\mathrm{univ},\nabla}=1
. Let
K^{\prime}/K
be an unramified extension of degree
n!
. Then
\rho^{\prime}|_{G_{K^{\prime}}}
is a sum of characters. Since
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta^{\prime}}
is a domain, this shows that
\rho
is potentially diagonalizable.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.1.4
.

We can repeat the proof of Theorem
5.1.5
using Proposition
4.4.2
instead of the diagram (3.16) in
[
LLHL19
]
. The symplecticity of
\overline{\rho}
implies the symplecticity of
(\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}},\overline{\beta})
(i.e.
D_{j}\in T^{\vee}(\mathbf{F})
instead of
T_{4}^{\vee}(\mathbf{F})
). The appropriate symplectic deformation ring
R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta,\tau,\overline{\beta}}
of
(\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}},\overline{\beta})
can be obtained from the
\mathrm{GL}_{4}
case by imposing that
A^{(j),\mathrm{univ},\nabla}
is valued in
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
, i.e.
D_{j}^{\mathrm{univ}}
valued in
T^{\vee}
and
w_{j}^{-1}U^{(j),\mathrm{univ},\nabla}w_{j}
valued in
\overline{U}
. Then
R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta,\tau,\overline{\beta}}
is a quotient of power series ring over
{\mathcal{O}}
with
7f
variables. On the other hand,
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
has dimension
4f+11
. Then we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}\llbracket x_{1},\dots,x_{3f}\rrbracket\simeq R_{\overline{{\mathfrak{M}}}}^{\leq\lambda+\eta,\tau,\overline{\beta}}\llbracket y_{1},\dots,y_{11}\rrbracket

which proves the claim. The potential diagonalizability can be proven similarly using Example
4.1.12
.
∎

We also record a lifting result for certain non-tame
\overline{\rho}
.

Lemma 5.1.6
.

Let
\kappa\in X^{*}_{1}(\underline{T})
be 0-deep. Let
\overline{\rho}:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
be of maximally nonsplit of niveau 1 and weight
\sigma=F(\kappa)
. Let
a
be an integer and let
k
be the unique integer such that
a-2k=:b\in\{3,4\}
. Let
(\lambda_{a}+\eta,\tau_{a})
be a type such that

\displaystyle\lambda_{a}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}k(1,1;2)&\text{if $b=3$}\\
(1,0;0)+k(1,1;2)&\text{if $b=4$}\end{array}\right.

and
\tau_{a}=\tau(1,\kappa-\phi^{-1}(\lambda_{a}))
.
Then there is a potentially crystalline lift
\rho:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}})
of
\overline{\rho}
with Hodge type
\lambda_{a}+\eta
and tame inertial type
\tau_{a}
and of the form

\displaystyle\rho={\begin{pmatrix}\chi_{1}&*&*&*\\
0&\chi_{2}&*&*\\
0&0&\chi_{3}&*\\
0&0&0&\chi_{4}\end{pmatrix}}

where
\oplus_{i=1}^{4}\chi_{i}=\epsilon_{p}^{\lambda_{a}+\eta}\prod_{{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}}{\omega}_{K,\sigma_{j}}^{\phi(\kappa_{j})-\lambda_{a,j}}
.

Proof.

It is clear that
\chi_{i}
lifts
\overline{\chi}_{i}
. Since
\kappa
is 0-deep, for
i<j
,
\overline{\chi}_{i}\overline{\chi}_{j}^{-1}\neq\overline{\epsilon}_{p}
. Thus,
\chi_{i}\chi_{j}^{-1}\neq\epsilon_{p}
. Then the existence of the lift
\rho
follows from the vanishing of cohomology
H^{2}(G_{K},\chi_{i}\chi_{j}^{-1})=0
. It is potentially crystalline by
[
EG23
, Lemma 6.3.1]
.
∎

5.1.7.
Local deformations:
l\neq p

We record deformation problems for Ihara avoidance argument. Let
l
be a prime and
l\neq p
. Let
F/\mathbf{Q}_{l}
be a finite extension with the ring of integers
{\mathcal{O}}_{F}
, a uniformizer
\varpi_{F}
, and the residue field
k_{F}
of size
q_{F}
. We assume that
q_{F}\equiv 1\bmod p
. Let
\overline{\rho}:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
be a trivial representation and
\psi:G_{F}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
be a continuous character trivial modulo
\varpi
.

Let
\zeta=(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})
be a pair of continuous characters
\zeta_{i}:{\mathcal{O}}_{F}^{\times}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
that are trivial modulo
\varpi
. We let
D_{\overline{\rho}}^{\zeta}
be the functor taking
A\in\widehat{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
to the set of
A
-valued lifts
\rho:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(A)
such that for any
\sigma\in I_{F}
, the characteristic polynomial of
\rho(\sigma)
is

\displaystyle(X-\zeta_{1}(\Art_{F}^{-1}(\sigma)))(X-\zeta_{2}(\Art_{F}^{-1}(\sigma)))(X-\zeta_{2}(\Art_{F}^{-1}(\sigma)))^{-1}(X-\zeta_{1}(\Art_{F}^{-1}(\sigma)))^{-1}.

Then
D_{\overline{\rho}}^{\zeta}
is a local deformation problem. We let
R^{\zeta}_{\overline{\rho}}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
be an object representing
D_{\overline{\rho}}^{\zeta}
. We record the following results on
R^{\zeta}_{\overline{\rho}}
.

Proposition 5.1.8

(Proposition 7.4.7 and 7.4.8 in
[
BCGP21
]
)
.

(1)

Suppose that
\zeta=1
is the pair of trivial characters. Then
\spec R^{1}_{\overline{\rho}}
is equidimensional of dimension 11 and every generic point has characteristic zero. Moreover, every generic point of
\spec R^{1}_{\overline{\rho}}/\varpi
is the specialization of a unique generic point of
\spec R^{1}_{\overline{\rho}}
.

(2)

Suppose that
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}\neq 1
and
\zeta_{1}\neq\zeta_{2}^{\pm 1}
. Then
\spec R^{\zeta}_{\overline{\rho}}
is irreducible of dimension 11, and its generic point has characteristic zero.

5.2.
Congruent patching functors

Recall the finite étale
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-algebra
{\mathcal{O}}_{p}
. It can be written as a finite product of finite étale local
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-algebras
\prod_{v\in S_{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{v}
. We write
F_{p}={\mathcal{O}}_{p}[1/p]\simeq\prod_{v\in S_{p}}F_{v}
. Let
{\mathcal{J}}=\Hom_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}({\mathcal{O}}_{p},{\mathcal{O}})
.

Let
\overline{\rho}:G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow\prescript{L}{}{\underline{G}}(\mathbf{F})
be a tame
L
-parameter. We consider
{\psi_{p}}:G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow\prescript{L}{}{\underline{\mathbf{G}_{m}}({\mathcal{O}})}
lifting
\simc(\overline{\rho})
. Note that
{\psi_{p}}
is equivalent to a collection
\left\{\psi_{v}:G_{F_{v}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}\right\}_{v\in S_{p}}
. For the applications in §
6
, we only consider
\psi_{p}
satisfying:

•

{\psi_{p}}
is potentially crystalline with tame descent data

•

there exists an integer
w
such that
\HT_{\sigma}(\psi_{v})=\left\{w\right\}
for all
v\in S_{p}
and
\sigma\in\Hom_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}(F_{v},E)
; and

•

\psi_{v}\epsilon_{p}^{-w}
has finite image for all
v\in S_{p}
.

Note that the first and second conditions imply

\displaystyle\psi_{p}\epsilon_{p}^{-w}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}=[\overline{\rho}\overline{\epsilon_{p}}^{-w}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}],

and the third condition implies
