\psi_{p}\epsilon_{p}^{-w}=[\overline{\rho}\overline{\epsilon_{p}}^{-w}]
.

Definition 5.2.1
.

(1)

We define
\Psi(\overline{\rho})
to be a set of
\psi_{p}:G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow\prescript{L}{}{\underline{\mathbf{G}_{m}}({\mathcal{O}})}
lifting
\simc(\overline{\rho})
and satisfying the above conditions. If
\psi_{p}\in\Psi(\overline{\rho})
, we say
\psi_{p}
and
\overline{\rho}
are
compatible
.

(2)

We say a type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
is
compatible with
\psi_{p}
if
\simc(\lambda_{j}+\eta_{j})=w
for
{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}
and

\displaystyle\simc(\tau)\epsilon_{p}^{w}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}=\psi_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}.

Remark 5.2.2
.

It follows from the above discussion that given a tame
L
-parameter
\overline{\rho}
and a type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
, there exists at most one
{\psi_{p}}\in\Phi(\overline{\rho})
that is compatible with
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
.

Let
\psi_{p}\in\Psi(\overline{\rho})
. We define

\displaystyle R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\psi_{p}}:=\widehat{\otimes}_{v\in S_{p},{\mathcal{O}}}R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\square,\psi_{v}},\ \ R_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}:=R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\psi_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}R^{p}

where

R^{p}
is a complete Noetherian equidimensional flat
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra. For a type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
compatible with
\psi_{p}
, we define

\displaystyle R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,\psi_{p}}:=\widehat{\otimes}_{v\in S_{p},{\mathcal{O}}}R_{\overline{\rho}_{v}}^{\lambda_{v}+\eta_{v},\tau_{v},\psi_{v}},\ \ R_{\infty}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,\psi_{p}}:=R_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}\otimes_{R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\psi_{p}}}R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,\psi_{p}}.

Let
\mathrm{Mod}(R_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}})
be the category of finitely generated modules over
R_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}
and
\Rep_{\mathcal{O}}^{\psi_{p}}(\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{p}))
be the category of topological
{\mathcal{O}}[\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})]
-modules, which are finitely generated over
{\mathcal{O}}
, with fixed central character given by
\otimes_{v\in S_{p}}(\psi_{v}\epsilon_{p}^{3}|_{I_{F_{v}}})\circ(\Art_{F_{v}}|_{{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}}})
.

Definition 5.2.3
.

A
fixed similitude patching functor
for
\overline{\rho}
is a triple
({\psi_{p}},R_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}},M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}})
where
\psi_{p}\in\Psi(\overline{\rho})
,
R_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}
is as above, and

\displaystyle M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}:\Rep^{\psi_{p}}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{p}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}(R_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}})

is an exact covariant functor satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

for a type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
compatible with
\psi_{p}
and a
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})
-stable
{\mathcal{O}}
-lattice
\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau)
in
\sigma(\lambda,\tau)
,
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))
is a maximal Cohen–Macaulay module over
R_{\infty}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}
if it is nonzero; and

(2)

for all
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
,
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}(\sigma)
is a maximal Cohen–Macaulay module over
R_{\infty}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}/\varpi
if it is nonzero.

Furthermore, we say that

(1)

M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}
is
minimal
if
R^{p}
is formally smooth over
{\mathcal{O}}
and
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))[p^{-1}]
is locally free of rank at most one over
R_{\infty}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}[p^{-1}]
; and

(2)

M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}
is
potentially diagonalizable
if
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))
is nonzero for all
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
such that
\overline{\rho}_{v}
has potentially diagonalizable lift of type
(\lambda_{v}+\eta_{v},\tau_{v})
for each
v\in S_{p}
.

For the following definition, we let
R_{\infty}
be a complete local Noetherian
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra with a surjection
R_{\infty}\rightarrow R_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}
for all
{\psi_{p}}\in\Phi(\overline{\rho})
. In the application,
R_{\infty}
will be a completed tensor product of local deformation rings and
R_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}
will be a quotient of
R_{\infty}
with fixed similitude character.

Definition 5.2.4
.

(1)

We say a set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}=\left\{({\psi_{p}},R^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty},M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}})\mid\text{$({\psi_{p}},R^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty},M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}})$ is a fixed similitude patching functor for $\overline{\rho}$}\right\}

is (or its elements are)
congruent
if the restrictions of
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}
to
\varpi
-torsion objects

\displaystyle M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}/\varpi:\Rep_{\mathbf{F}}^{\psi_{p}}(\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{p}))\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}(R_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}/\varpi)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}(R_{\infty}/\varpi)

for all
({\psi_{p}},R^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty},M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}})\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}
are equal.

(2)

We say that
{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}
is
minimal
(resp.
potentially diagonalizable
) if all
({\psi_{p}},R^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty},M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}})\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}
are minimal (resp. potentially diagonalizable).

(3)

A
congruent family of fixed similitude patching functors
for
\overline{\rho}
is a congruent set of fixed similitude patching functors
{\mathcal{M}}^{\Phi}_{\infty}
such that the map

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}^{\Phi}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\Phi(\overline{\rho})

\displaystyle({\psi_{p}},R^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty},M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}})

\displaystyle\mapsto{\psi_{p}}

is a bijection. In other words,
{\mathcal{M}}^{\Phi}_{\infty}
consists of exactly one fixed similitude patching functor for each
{\psi_{p}}\in\Phi(\overline{\rho})
.

5.3.
Algebraic automorphic forms

In this subsection, we recall the global setup in
[
EL
, §4]
. Let
F
be a totally real field.
Suppose that
[F:\mathbf{Q}]
is even. We also assume that
p
is unramified in
F
. Let
{\mathcal{G}}
be the
F
-group
\mathrm{GU}_{2}(D)
where
D
is a quaternion algebra over
F
ramified at all infinite places and split at all finite places. Such a
D
exists because
[F:\mathbf{Q}]
is even. Then
{\mathcal{G}}
is an inner form of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
. The center
Z_{\mathcal{G}}
is isomorphic to
\mathbf{G}_{m}
, and
{\mathcal{G}}
is compact modulo center at infinity. Choose a maximal order
{\mathcal{O}}_{D}
of
D
. It defines an
{\mathcal{O}}_{F}
-structure on
{\mathcal{G}}
. For each finite place
v
, we fix an isomorphism
{\mathcal{O}}_{D,v}\simeq M_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}})
which induces an isomorphism
\iota_{v}:{\mathcal{G}}_{/{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4/{\mathcal{O}}_{F_{v}}}
.

Let
\chi:\mathbb{A}_{F}/F^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\times}
be a Hecke character. We write
\chi_{p,\iota}=\iota^{-1}\circ\chi
. Let
U=U_{p}U^{\infty,p}\leq{\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})\times{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty,p}_{F})
be a compact open subgroup. For a finite place
v
of
F
, we write
{\mathrm{Iw}}(v)
(resp.
{\mathrm{Iw}}_{1}(v)
) for the (resp. pro-
p
) Iwahori subgroup of
{\mathcal{G}}(F_{v})\simeq\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(F_{v})
. Let
W
be an
{\mathcal{O}}
-module with a continuous action of
U_{p}
. We define
S_{\chi}(U,W)
to be the
{\mathcal{O}}
-module of functions
f:{\mathcal{G}}(F^{\times}){\backslash}{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})\rightarrow W
such that

\displaystyle f(zgu)=\chi_{p,\iota}(z)u_{p}^{-1}f(g)\ \ \ \forall g\in{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty}_{F}),u\in U,z\in Z_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty}_{F}).

Let
U\leq{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty})
be a compact open subgroup. We assume that
U
is
sufficiently small
in the sense that it contains no element of order
p
. For our local applications, we will fix a finite place
v_{0}
such that
q_{v_{0}}\not\equiv 1\bmod p
and has residue characteristic
>5
and assume that
U_{v_{0}}={\mathrm{Iw}}_{1}(v_{0})
. In this case, the choice of
U_{v_{0}}
ensures that
U
is sufficiently small. Let
P_{U}
be the finite set of finite places of
F
at which
U
is ramified. Let
S_{p}
be the set of places of
F
dividing
p
. For a finite set
P
, we define the
universal Hecke algebra

\mathbb{T}^{P,\mathrm{univ}}
to be the polynomial ring over
{\mathcal{O}}
generated by
S_{v},T_{v,1},T_{v,2}
for each
v\notin P
and
v=v_{0}
if
v_{0}\in P
. When
P
contains
S_{p}\cup P_{U}
, there is a natural
\mathbb{T}^{P,\mathrm{univ}}
-action on
S_{\chi}(U,W)
where
S_{v},T_{v,1},T_{v,2}
act through the double coset operators

\displaystyle\small{\left[U_{v}{\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{v}&&&\\
&\varpi_{v}&&\\
&&\varpi_{v}&\\
&&&\varpi_{v}\end{pmatrix}}U_{v}\right],\ \ \left[U_{v}{\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{v}&&&\\
&\varpi_{v}&&\\
&&1&\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}}U_{v}\right],\ \ \left[U_{v}{\begin{pmatrix}\varpi_{v}^{2}&&&\\
&\varpi_{v}&&\\
&&\varpi_{v}&\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}}U_{v}\right]}

respectively. We denote the image of
\mathbb{T}^{P,\mathrm{univ}}
in
\End_{{\mathcal{O}}}(S_{\chi}(U,W))
by
\mathbb{T}^{P}_{\chi}(U,W)
.

Let
\psi:=\chi_{p,\iota}\epsilon_{p}^{-3}
. Let
\overline{r}:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
be an absolutely irreducible continuous representation and
\simc(\overline{r})={\psi}\bmod\varpi
. When we have a fixed place
v_{0}
, we assume that
\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v_{0}}}}
is a sum of unramified characters with no two eigenvalues of
\Frob_{v_{0}}
have ratio
q_{v_{0}}
. In applications, we can always choose such
v_{0}
(
[
BCGP21
, §7.7]
). We write

\displaystyle\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v_{0}}}}\simeq\mathrm{ur}_{c_{1}}\mathrm{ur}_{c_{2}}\mathrm{ur}_{c_{0}}\oplus\mathrm{ur}_{c_{1}}\mathrm{ur}_{c_{0}}\oplus\mathrm{ur}_{c_{2}}\mathrm{ur}_{c_{0}}\oplus\mathrm{ur}_{c_{0}}

for
c_{1},c_{2},c_{0}\in\mathbf{F}^{\times}
. We denote by
P_{\overline{r}}
the set of finite places either dividing
p
or at which
\overline{r}
is ramified. For any finite set
P\supset P_{\overline{r}}
, we define a maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}\leq\mathbb{T}^{P,\mathrm{univ}}
with residue field
\mathbf{F}
by demanding that

(1)

for each
v\notin P
,

\displaystyle S_{v}\bmod{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}=\overline{\psi}(\Frob_{v})

and the characteristic polynomial of
\overline{r}(\Frob_{v})
in
\mathbf{F}[X]
is given by

X^{4}-T_{v,1}X^{3}+(q_{v}T_{v,2}+(q_{v}^{3}+q_{v})S_{v})X^{2}-q_{v}^{3}T_{v,1}S_{v}X+q_{v}^{6}S_{v}^{2}\bmod{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P},

(2)

for
v=v_{0}
(if
v_{0}\in P
),

\displaystyle S_{v}\bmod{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}=c_{0},\ T_{v,1}\bmod{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}=q_{v_{0}}c_{1},\ \ T_{v,2}\bmod{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}=q_{v_{0}}^{3}c_{2}

(cf.
[
BCGP21
, §2.4.7]
; note
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}
is well-defined by symplecticity of
\overline{r}
).

Definition 5.3.1
.

(1)

We say that a pair
(\overline{r},\chi)
as above is
automorphic of weight
\mu\in(X^{*}(T)^{+})^{S_{p}}
and level
U
if there is a finite set of finite places
P
containing
P_{U}\cup P_{\overline{r}}
such that
S_{\chi}(U,V(\mu)^{\vee})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}}\neq 0
.

(2)

Let
\sigma
be a Serre weight of
G_{0}(\mathbf{F}_{p})
. We say that
\overline{r}
is
modular of weight
\sigma
(and level
U
), or equivalently,
\sigma
is a
modular weight
of
\overline{r}
(at level
U
) if
S_{\chi}(U,\sigma^{\vee})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}}\neq 0
for some
\chi
. Note that
S_{\chi}(U,\sigma^{\vee})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}}
does not depend on the choice of
\chi
as long as
\chi_{p,\iota}\epsilon_{p}^{-3}\bmod\varpi=\simc(\overline{r})
.

(3)

We let
W(\overline{r})
be the set of modular Serre weights of
\overline{r}
.

We remark that
(\overline{r},\chi)
is automorphic for some
\chi
if and only if
\overline{r}
is a mod
p
reduction of the Galois representation attached to a regular algebraic cuspidal automorphic representation of
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
(equivalently, of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
); see
[
EL
, Remark 4.2.4]
.

Let
(\overline{r},\chi)
be automorphic of weight
\mu
and level
U
and
P
be as above. Suppose that there is a subset
R\subset P
disjoint from
S_{p}
such that for
v\in R
,
U_{v}={\mathrm{Iw}}_{1}(v)
,
q_{v}\equiv 1\bmod p
, and
\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is trivial. For each
v\in R
, we choose a pair of characters
\zeta_{v}=(\zeta_{v,1},\zeta_{v,2})
such that

(1)

for
i=1,2
,
\zeta_{v,i}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}_{v}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
is a continuous character trivial modulo
\varpi
;

(2)

either
\zeta_{v,1}=\zeta_{v,2}=1
, or
\zeta_{v,1},\zeta_{v,2}\neq 1
and
\zeta_{v,1}\neq\zeta_{v,2}^{\pm 1}
.

Let
T_{\mathrm{der}}:=\ker(T\xrightarrow{\simc}\mathbf{G}_{m})
. The projection to the first two diagonal entries induces an isomorphism
T_{\mathrm{der}}\simeq\mathbf{G}_{m}^{2}
. We write
\zeta_{R}
for the induced character

\displaystyle\zeta_{R}=\prod_{v\in R}[\overline{\zeta}_{v}]:\prod_{v\in R}T_{\mathrm{der}}(k_{v})\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}.

We let
\mathbb{T}^{P}_{\chi}(U,V(\mu))_{\zeta_{R}}
to be the image of
\mathbb{T}^{{P},\mathrm{univ}}
in
\End_{{\mathcal{O}}}(S_{\chi}(U,V(\mu))_{\zeta_{R}})
where the subscript
\zeta_{R}
denotes taking the
\zeta_{R}
-coinvariant for the action of
\Pi_{v\in R}T_{\mathrm{der}}(k_{v})
.

Proposition 5.3.2
.

Keep the above notations and assumptions. We further assume that
\overline{r}:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
is absolutely irreducible. Then there exists a unique continuous representation

\displaystyle r^{P}_{\chi,\mu}(U):G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{T}^{P}_{\chi}(U,V(\mu))_{\zeta_{R},{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}})

such that

(1)

r^{P}_{\chi,\mu}(U)
lifts
\overline{r}
;

(2)

\simc(r^{P\cup Q}_{\chi,\mu}(U(Q)))=\psi
;

(3)

if
v\notin P\cup Q
, then
r^{P}_{\chi,\mu}(U)
is unramified at
v
and the characteristic polynomial of
r^{P}_{\chi,\mu}(U)(\Frob_{v})
in
\mathbb{T}^{P}_{\chi}(U,V(\mu))_{\zeta_{R},{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}}[X]
is equal to

\displaystyle X^{4}-T_{v,1}X^{3}+(q_{v}T_{v,2}+(q_{v}^{3}+q_{v})\chi_{v}(\varpi_{v}))X^{2}-q_{v}^{3}T_{v,1}\chi_{v}(\varpi_{v})X+q_{v}^{6}\chi_{v}(\varpi_{v})^{2},

(4)

if
v\in R
, then the
\mathbb{T}^{P}_{\chi}(U,V(\mu))_{\zeta_{R},{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}}
-point of
R_{\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}}^{\square}
induced by
r^{P}_{\chi,\mu}(U)|_{G_{F_{v}}}
factors through
R_{\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}}^{\zeta_{v}}
;

(5)

if
v_{0}\in P
and
v=v_{0}
, then

\displaystyle r^{P}_{\chi,\mu}(U)|_{G_{F_{v}}}\simeq\mathrm{ur}_{T_{v,1}}\mathrm{ur}_{T_{v,2}}\mathrm{ur}_{S_{v}}\oplus\mathrm{ur}_{T_{v,1}}\mathrm{ur}_{S_{v}}\oplus\mathrm{ur}_{T_{v,2}}\mathrm{ur}_{S_{v}}\oplus\mathrm{ur}_{S_{v}};

(6)

and for every
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra homomorphism
f:\mathbb{T}^{P}_{\chi}(U,V(\mu))_{\zeta_{R},{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}}\rightarrow E^{\prime}
where
E^{\prime}
is a finite extension of
E
, the representation
f\circ r^{P}_{\chi,\mu}(U)|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is de Rham of Hodge–Tate weights
\mu_{v}+\eta_{v}
for all
v\in S_{p}
.

Proof.

This is proven in
[
EL
, Proposition 4.2.6]
except item (4) and (5). By the local-global compatibility in
[
Mok14
, Theorem 3.5]
, item (4) follows from Proposition 2.4.28
[
BCGP21
]
and item (5) follows from Proposition 2.4.3, 2.4.4, and 7.4.2 in
loc. cit.
.
∎

5.4.
Patching argument

In this subsection, we construct a congruent family of similitude patching functors in the global case (i.e.
{\mathcal{O}}_{p}={\mathcal{O}}_{F}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}}\mathbf{Z}_{p})
and in the local case (i.e.
{\mathcal{O}}_{p}={\mathcal{O}}_{K}
).

Definition 5.4.1
.

(1)

We say that a continuous representation
\overline{r}:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
is
odd
if for each infinite place
v
and corresponding choice of complex conjugation
c_{v}\in G_{F}
,
\simc(\overline{r})(c_{v})=-1
.

(2)

We say that a continuous representation
\overline{r}:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})

satisfies Taylor–Wiles conditions
if
\overline{r}
is absolutely irreducible, odd, and vast and tidy (in the sense of
[
BCGP21
, §7.5]
).

Theorem 5.4.2
.

(1)

Let
\overline{r}:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
be a continuous representation. Let
\chi
be a Hecke character and
\psi:=\chi\epsilon_{p}^{-3}
. Suppose that
\overline{r}
satisfies Taylor–Wiles conditions and
(\overline{r},\chi)
is automorphic (of some weight
\mu^{\prime}
and level
U^{\prime}
).
Then there exists a congruent family of fixed similitude patching functors for
\overline{r}_{p}
.

(2)

Let
\overline{\rho}:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
be a
16
-generic continuous representation. If
\overline{\rho}
is either tame or maximally nonsplit of niveau 1 and weight
\sigma
, there exists a congruent family of potentially diagonaliazable fixed similitude patching functors for
\overline{\rho}
. In the former case, it can be taken to be minimal.

Remark 5.4.3
.

(1)

Let
\sigma
be a Serre weight of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/p)
. Let
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}
be a fixed similitude patching functor in Theorem
5.4.2
(1). It will be clear from the construction of
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}
that

\displaystyle(M^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty}(\sigma)/{\mathfrak{m}}_{\infty})^{\vee}\simeq S_{\psi}(U,\sigma^{\vee})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}}.

In other words,
\sigma\in W(\overline{r})
if and only if
M^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty}(\sigma)\neq 0
. Similarly, for a type
(\lambda,\tau)
compatible with
{\psi_{p}}
, we have

\displaystyle(M^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))/{\mathfrak{a}}_{\infty})^{\vee}=S_{\psi}(U,\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau)^{\vee})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{P}}.

In particular, if
M^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))\neq 0
, then
\overline{r}_{p}
has a potentially crystalline lift of type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
.

(2)

Let
\sigma
be a Serre weight of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(k)
and
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}
be a fixed similitude patching functor in Theorem
5.4.2
(2). It will be clear from the construction and Theorem
5.4.4
that if
M^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))\neq 0
, then
\sigma\in W^{?}(\overline{\rho}|_{I_{K}})
. Also, for a type
(\lambda,\tau)
compatible with
{\psi_{p}}
, if
M^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))\neq 0
, then
\overline{\rho}
has a potentially crystalline lift of type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
by Proposition
5.3.2
.

Granting item (1) in Theorem
5.4.2
, we prove the following weight elimination result. It will be used to prove item (2) in Theorem
5.4.2
.

Theorem 5.4.4
.

Let
\overline{r}:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
,
\chi
, and
\psi
be as in Theorem
5.4.2
(1). Let
\sigma\in W(\overline{r})
. We suppose that either
(\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}})^{\mathrm{ss}}
is 22-generic for each
v|p
or
\sigma
is
7
-deep and
(\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}})^{\mathrm{ss}}
is 16-generic for each
v|p
. Then
\sigma\in W^{?}(\overline{r}^{\mathrm{ss}}_{p})
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.4.2
(1), there exists a congruent family of fixed similitude patching functors
\{M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}\}
for
\overline{r}_{p}
. Let
\sigma=F(\lambda)
be a modular Serre weight for
\overline{r}
. By the exactness of patching functors and Remark
5.4.3
,
\overline{r}_{p}
has a potentially crystalline lift of type
(\eta,\tau)
for any 1-generic tame inertial type
\tau
such that
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
.

Suppose that
\lambda
is
12
-deep. For each
s\in\underline{W}
, let
\tau_{s}=\tau(s,\widetilde{w}_{h}\cdot\lambda+\eta)
be a
9
-generic tame inertial
L
-parameter. Since
F(\lambda)\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau_{s}))
, for the unique
{\psi_{p}}\in\Phi(\overline{r}_{p})
compatible with
(\eta,\tau_{s})
, we have

\displaystyle M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\tau_{s}))\neq 0.

Since
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\tau_{s}))
is supported on
\spec R_{\infty}^{\eta,\tau_{s},{\psi_{p}}}
,
\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
admits a potentially crystalline lift with Hodge type
\eta_{v}
and tame inertial type
(\tau_{s})_{v}
for
v|p
.
By Proposition
5.1.3
, there exists a lowest alcove presentation of
\overline{r}_{v}^{\mathrm{ss}}
(which does not depend on
s
by Remark
2.5.13
) such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{r}_{v}^{\mathrm{ss}},\tau_{s})\in{\mathrm{Adm}}(\eta)
. By Lemma
2.5.12
,
F(\lambda)\in W^{?}(\overline{r}_{p}^{\mathrm{ss}})
.

Suppose that
\lambda
is not
12
-deep but
7
-deep. Then the tame type
\tau_{e}=\tau(e,\widetilde{w}_{h}\cdot\lambda+\eta)
is 4-generic but not 12-generic by
[
LLHL19
, Proposition 2.2.16]
and its proof. Also,
F(\lambda)\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau_{e}))
. Then
\std(\overline{r}_{p})
does not admit a potentially crystalline lift of type
{\mathcal{T}}(\eta,\tau_{e})
by Proposition 3.3.2 in
loc. cit.
. This contradicts the first paragraph of this proof.

Finally, suppose that
\lambda
is not 7-deep and
(\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}})^{\mathrm{ss}}
is 22-generic for each
v|p
. For
v|p
,
\std(\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}})
does not have a potentially crystalline lift of type
(\eta^{\prime},\std(\tau(e,\lambda)))
by
[
Enn19
, Theorem 8]
as explained in the proof of
[
LLHL19
, Corollary 4.2.4]
. Thus,
\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
does not have a potentially crystalline lift of type
(\eta,\tau(e,\lambda))
. This contradicts the assumption on
F(\lambda)
.
∎

Granting item (2) in Theorem
5.4.2
, we prove
Lemma
4.4.4
and
4.5.3
(thus finishing the proofs of Theorem
4.4.3
and
4.5.4
). We first prove the following partial converse of Proposition
5.1.3
.

Proposition 5.4.5
.

Suppose that a tame
\overline{\rho}:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
is 16-generic. Let
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
be a type with a
(h_{\lambda}+1)
-generic lowest alcove presentation of
\tau
. If
\overline{\rho}
has a
\lambda
-compatible lowest alcove presentation such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)^{*}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda+\eta)
, then
R^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}_{\overline{\rho}}\neq 0
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.4.2
(2), there exists a congruent family of potentially diagonalizable minimal fixed similitude patching functors
\{M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}\}
for
\overline{\rho}
. Choose a 16-generic lowest alcove presentation of
\overline{\rho}

\lambda
-compatible with
\tau
. For each
s\in\underline{W}
, there exists a tame inertial type
\tau_{s}
with 13-generic lowest alcove presentation compatible with
\overline{\rho}
and
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau_{s})=s^{-1}(\eta)
. By Lemma
2.5.11
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau_{s}))\cap W^{?}(\overline{\rho}|_{I_{K}})=\{F_{\overline{\rho}}(s)\}.

Note that any element in
\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau_{s}))
is 10-deep by Proposition
2.5.6
.

On the other hand,
\overline{\rho}
admits a potentially diagonalizable lift of type
(\eta,\tau_{s})
by Theorem
5.1.4
. Then for
{\psi_{p}}\in\Phi(\overline{\rho})
compatible with
(\eta,\tau_{s})
,
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\tau_{s}))\neq 0
. By Theorem
5.4.4
,
\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau_{s}))\cap W(\overline{r})
is nonempty and contained in
W^{?}(\overline{\rho}|_{I_{K}})
. Therefore,
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}(F_{\overline{\rho}}(s))\neq 0
and
\{F_{\overline{\rho}}(s)\}_{s\in\underline{W}}=W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{\rho})
. For
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
as in the statement,
\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))\cap W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{\rho})\neq\emptyset
by
[
LLHLMa
, Proposition 2.6.6]
. Thus
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\overline{\sigma}^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))\neq 0
and
R^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}_{\overline{\rho}}\neq 0
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.4.4
.

Suppose that
\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)
. There exists a tame
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}(\mathbf{F})
with a 16-generic lowest alcove presentation such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)^{*}=\widetilde{z}
. By Proposition
5.4.5
,
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{X}}^{\lambda,\tau}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}(\mathbf{F})
. Furthermore,
\overline{\rho}
is in
{\mathcal{X}}^{\lambda,\tau}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}(\widetilde{z})
because
\varepsilon_{\infty}(\overline{\rho})
is equal to the image of
\widetilde{z}
in
\Phi\text{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{K}^{{\text{{\'{e}t}}},{\mathrm{Sym}}}
under
\iota^{\prime}_{\widetilde{z}}
. Conversely, if
{\mathcal{X}}^{\lambda,\tau}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}(\widetilde{z})\neq\emptyset
, then
Y^{\leq\lambda,\tau}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}(\widetilde{z})\neq\emptyset
. This implies
\widetilde{z}\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda)
by Corollary
4.2.17
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.5.3
.

Let
(\widetilde{w},\omega)
be a lowest alcove presentation of
\sigma

\lambda
-compatible with
\tau
. We also set
\kappa=\pi^{-1}(\widetilde{w})\cdot(\omega-\eta)
so that
\sigma=F(\kappa)
. Then
\sigma
is 16-deep, which implies
\omega
is 13-deep. Let
\overline{\rho}:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
be maximally nonsplit of niveau 1 and weight
\sigma
. Let
\widetilde{w}=t_{\eta_{w}}w
. Note that
\eta_{w}
is dominant and 3-small. Then a direct computation shows that
\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}}|_{I_{K}}
admits a 10-generic lowest alcove presentation
(\pi^{-1}(w)^{-1}w,\omega+\pi^{-1}(w)^{-1}(\eta_{w}))
. Similarly, a tame inertial
L
-parameter
\tau(e,\kappa)
admits a 10-generic lowest alcove presentation
(\pi^{-1}(w)^{-1}w,\omega+\pi^{-1}(w)^{-1}(\eta_{w}-\eta))
. By Lemma
5.1.6
,
\overline{\rho}
admits a potentially diagonalizable lift of type
(\eta,\tau(e,\kappa))
. Since
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}},\tau(e,\kappa))=t_{\eta}
, we have
W^{?}(\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}})\cap\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau(e,\kappa)))=\{\sigma\}
by Proposition
2.5.11
. Note that any element in
\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau(e,\kappa)))
is
7
-deep by Proposition
2.5.6
.

By Theorem
5.4.2
(2),
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau(e,\kappa))\neq 0
for the unique
{\psi_{p}}\in\Phi(\overline{\rho})
compatible with
(\eta,\tau(e,\kappa))
, and by Theorem
5.4.4
,
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau(e,\kappa)))/\varpi=M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma)
. Then for any type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
such that
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
and the unique
\psi_{p}^{\prime}\in\Phi(\overline{\rho})
compatible with
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
,
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}^{\prime}}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))\neq 0
. Since it is supported on
R_{\infty}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,\psi_{p}^{\prime}}
,
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda,\tau}(\mathbf{F})
. Since such
\overline{\rho}
are dense in
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}
, this implies that
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda,\tau}
.
∎

5.4.6.
Construction of fixed similitude patching functors

We first provide a general construction of a fixed similitude patching functor by extending the construction in
[
EL
, §4.4]
, which is based on
[
CEG+16
]
and
[
BCGP21
]
. Then we explain how the construction applies to particular cases.

Let
F
and
\overline{r}
be as in Theorem
5.4.2
(1). Given
\psi_{p}\in\Psi(\overline{r}_{p})
, there exists a continuous character
\psi:G_{F}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
lifting
\simc(\overline{r})
and
\psi|_{G_{F_{v}}}=\psi_{v}
for all
v\in S_{p}
(
[
EL
, Lemma 4.4.3]
). Let
\chi
be a Hecke character such that
\chi_{p,\iota}=\psi\epsilon_{p}^{3}
. We assume that
(\overline{r},\chi)
is automorphic of weight
\mu
and sufficiently small level
U
.

Let
S_{p}
be the set of places of
F
dividing
p
and
S
be a finite set of finite places containing
S_{p}
. We define

\displaystyle q=h^{1}(F_{S}/F,\mathrm{ad}(\overline{r})(1)),\ \ \ g=2q-4[F:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]+\absolutevalue{S}-1.

For
T\subset S
, we define
{\mathcal{T}}_{T}:={\mathcal{O}}\llbracket y_{1},\dots,y_{11\absolutevalue{T}-1}\rrbracket
. We also define
S_{\infty}:={\mathcal{T}}_{S}\llbracket\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{2q}\rrbracket
.
Viewing
S_{\infty}
as an augmented
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra, we let
{\mathfrak{a}}_{\infty}
denote the augmentation ideal of
S_{\infty}
.

Suppose that
S
contains
S_{\overline{r}}\cup S_{U}
. Let
R
be a subset of
S
disjoint from
S_{p}
. We assume that for each
v\in R
,
U_{v}={\mathrm{Iw}}_{1}(v)
,
q_{v}\equiv 1\bmod p
, and
\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is trivial. The presence of
R
is necessary for the “Ihara avoidance” argument and is not necessary for applications to the Breuil–Mézard conjecture or the weight part of Serre’s conjecture.
For each
v\in R
, we choose a pair of characters
\zeta_{v}=(\zeta_{v,1},\zeta_{v,2})
as in §
5.3
which induce a character
\zeta_{R}:\prod_{v\in R}T_{\mathrm{der}}(k_{v})\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\times}
.

For each
v\in S
, let
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}\subset{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\square,\psi|_{G_{F_{v}}}}
be a local deformation problem represented by
R_{v}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
. If
v\in R
, we take
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}={\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\zeta_{v}}
. We consider a global deformation problem

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}=(S,\{{\mathcal{D}}_{v}\}_{v\in S},\psi).

If
T
is a subset of
S
, we write
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{T}
for the functor of
T
-framed deformations of type
{\mathcal{S}}
. Since
\overline{r}
is absolutely irreducible,
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{S}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{T}
) is represented by
R_{\mathcal{S}}
(resp.
R^{T}_{\mathcal{S}}
) in
\widehat{{\mathcal{C}}}_{\mathcal{O}}
. The choice of a universal lift
r_{{\mathcal{S}}}:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(R_{\mathcal{S}})
gives an isomorphism
R_{\mathcal{S}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{\mathcal{T}}_{T}\simeq R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}
. Let
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T,\mathrm{loc}}:=\widehat{\otimes}_{v\in T}R_{v}
. Then there exists a natural map
R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T,\mathrm{loc}}\rightarrow R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{T}
.

Given a Taylor–Wiles datum
(Q,(\overline{\alpha}_{v,1},\dots,\overline{\alpha}_{v,4})_{v\in Q})
(
[
EL
, Definition 4.4.6]
), we define the augmented deformation problem

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{Q}=(S\cup Q,\{{\mathcal{D}}_{v}\}_{v\in S}\cup\{{\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\square,\psi|_{G_{F_{v}}}}\}_{v\in Q},\psi).

For each
v\in Q
, let
\Delta_{v}=k_{v}^{\times}(p)^{2}
where
k_{v}^{\times}(p)
is the maximal
p
-power quotient of
k_{v}^{\times}
. Set
\Delta_{Q}=\prod_{v\in Q}\Delta_{v}
. Let
{\mathfrak{a}}_{Q}
denote the augmentation ideal of
{\mathcal{O}}[\Delta_{Q}]
. Then there is a canonical local ring homomorphism
{\mathcal{O}}[\Delta_{Q}]\rightarrow R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{Q}}^{S}
such that
R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{Q}}^{S}/{\mathfrak{a}}_{Q}\simeq R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{S}
.
Since
\overline{r}
is odd and vast,
[
BCGP21
, Corollary 7.6.3]
shows that for each
n\geq 1
, there exists a Taylor–Wiles datum
Q_{n}
disjoint from
S
such that

•

\absolutevalue{Q_n}=q
;

•

q_{v}\equiv 1\bmod p^{n}
for each
v\in Q_{n}
;

•

there exists a local
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra surjection

\displaystyle\varphi_{n}^{{\psi_{p}}}:R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{S,\mathrm{loc}}\llbracket x_{1},\dots,x_{g}\rrbracket\twoheadrightarrow R_{S_{Q_{n}}}^{S}

with
g:=2q-4[F:\mathbf{Q}]+\absolutevalue{S}-1
.

We define open compact subgroups
U^{p}_{1}(Q_{n})
of
{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{A}^{\infty,p}_{F})
by setting
U^{p}_{1}(Q_{n})_{v}=U^{p}_{v}
if
v\notin Q\cup S
and
U^{p}_{1}(Q_{n})_{v}={\mathrm{Iw}}_{1}(v)
if
v\in Q
.
We define a
{\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})
-patching datum in the sense of
[
EL
, Definition 4.3.5]

\displaystyle\left(S_{\infty},R_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}},(R_{n}^{{\psi_{p}}},\varphi_{n}^{{\psi_{p}}},\{M_{r}^{{\psi_{p}}}(H)_{n}\}_{r\geq 1,H\leq_{\mathrm{c.o.}}{\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})},\alpha_{n}^{{\psi_{p}}})_{n\geq 1},\{M_{r}^{{\psi_{p}}}(H)_{0}\}_{r\geq 1,H\leq_{\mathrm{c.o.}}{\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})}\right)

by setting

•

R_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}:=R_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{S,\mathrm{loc}}\llbracket X_{1},\dots,X_{g}\rrbracket
;

•

R_{n}^{\psi_{p}}:=R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{n}}}^{S}
with
S_{\infty}
-algebra structure induced by
S_{\infty}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}[\Delta_{Q_{n}}]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{\mathcal{T}}_{S}\rightarrow R^{S}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{n}}}
;

•

M_{r}^{\psi_{p}}(H)_{0}:=[S_{\chi}(H\cdot U^{p},{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{r})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{S},\zeta_{R}}]^{\vee}
where the subscript
\zeta_{R}
denotes that we take the
\zeta_{R}
-coinvariants for the action of
\prod_{v\in R}T_{\mathrm{der}}(k(v))
; and

•

for
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle M_{r}^{\psi_{p}}(H)_{n}

\displaystyle:=[S_{\chi}(H\cdot U^{p}_{1}(Q_{n}),{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi^{r})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\chi}^{S\cup Q_{n}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{Q_{n}},\zeta_{R}}]^{\vee}\otimes_{R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{Q_{n}}}}R_{n}^{\psi_{p}}

\displaystyle\alpha_{n}^{\psi_{p}}

\displaystyle:M_{r}^{\psi_{p}}(H)_{n}/{\mathfrak{a}}_{\infty}\simeq M_{r}^{\psi_{p}}(H)_{0}

where
{\mathfrak{m}}_{Q_{n}}
is the kernel of
\otimes_{v\in Q_{n}}{\mathcal{O}}[T(F_{v})/T({\mathcal{O}}_{v})_{1}]\rightarrow\mathbf{F}
sending

\displaystyle T({\mathcal{O}}_{v})/T({\mathcal{O}}_{v})_{1}\mapsto 1,\beta_{0}(\varpi_{v})\mapsto\chi_{v}(\varpi_{v}),\beta_{1}(\varpi_{v})\mapsto\overline{\alpha}_{v,1},\textrm{and }\beta_{2}(\varpi_{v})\mapsto\overline{\alpha}_{v,1}\overline{\alpha}_{v,2},

and the isomorphism
\alpha_{n}^{\psi_{p}}
follows from
[
BCGP21
, §2.4.29]
.

Fix a nonprincipal ultrafilter
{\mathcal{F}}\subset 2^{\mathbf{N}}
. By
[
EL
, Lemma 4.3.9]
,

\displaystyle M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}:=\varprojlim_{r,H}{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathcal{F}}(\{M_{r}^{{\psi_{p}}}(H)_{n}\otimes_{S_{\infty}}S_{\infty}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{S_{\infty}}^{r}\}_{n\geq 1})

is a finitely generated projective
S_{\infty}\llbracket\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})\rrbracket
-module with compatible
S_{\infty}[\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(F_{p})]
-action and the action of
S_{\infty}
factors through the map
S_{\infty}\rightarrow R_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}
induced by
S_{\infty}\rightarrow R_{n}^{\psi_{p}}
. We also let
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}
denote the covariant functor

\displaystyle\Rep^{\psi_{p}}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{p}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}(R_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}})

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle\mapsto\Hom_{{\mathcal{O}}[\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})]}(M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}},V^{\vee})^{\vee}.

Proposition 5.4.7
.

The triple
({\psi_{p}},R_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}},M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}})
is a fixed similitude patching functor for
\overline{r}_{p}
.

Proof.

Since
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}
is projective over
{\mathcal{O}}\llbracket\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})\rrbracket
,
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}
is an exact functor. Let
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
be a type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
compatible with
{\psi_{p}}
and
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
.
It follows from
[
BG19
, Theorem A]
and
[
BCGP21
, Proposition 7.4.7, 7.4.8]
that
\dim S_{\infty}=\dim R^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty}
. Then the maximal Cohen–Macaulayness of
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))
over
R^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty}
follows from the usual commutative algebra argument (e.g.
[
CEG+16
, Lemma 4.18]
). The maximal Cohen–Macaulayness of
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma)
over
R^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty}/\varpi
follows similarly.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.4.2
.

We first prove item (1). Let
\psi_{p}\in\Psi(\overline{r}_{p})
.
We can assume that the level
U^{\prime}
is sufficiently small by shrinking it if necessary. We apply the above construction for given
\overline{r}
,
\psi_{p}
,
S=S_{\overline{r}}\cup S_{U}
,
R=\emptyset
, and
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}={\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\square,\psi|_{G_{F_{v}}}}
for each
v\in S
. We can and do choose Taylor–Wiles datum
Q_{n}
for each
n\geq 1
and the nonprincipal ultrafilter
{\mathcal{F}}
independent of
{\psi_{p}}
. Then all objects in the
{\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})
-patching datum reduced modulo
\varpi
in the construction in §
5.4.6
are independent of
{\psi_{p}}
. Thus the set
\{M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}\}_{{\psi_{p}}\in\Phi(\overline{r}_{p})}
is a congruent family of fixed similitude patching functors for
\overline{r}_{p}
by
[
EL
, Lemma 4.3.4]
.

Suppose that we are in the setup of item (2). When
\overline{\rho}
is tame, we call it the tame case, and when
\overline{\rho}
is maximally nonsplit of niveau 1 and weight
\sigma
, we call it the maximally nonsplit case. In both cases, we apply
[
EL
, Lemma 4.4.4]
and the existence of potentially diagonalizable lifts (Theorem
4.1.14
) to obtain a totally real field
F
, a continuous representation
\overline{r}:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
, and a Hecke character
\chi
such that

•

F_{v}\simeq K
for all
v|p
;

•

\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}\simeq\overline{\rho}
;

•

\overline{r}
is unramified at all finite places not dividing
p
;

•

\overline{r}
satisfies Taylor–Wiles conditions; and

•

(\overline{r},\chi)
is potentially diagonalizably automorphic of level unramified outside
p
.

Since
\overline{r}
is vast and tidy, there exists a place
v_{0}\notin S_{p}
such that
q_{v_{0}}\not\equiv 1\bmod p
, no two eigenvalues of
\overline{r}(\Frob_{v_{0}})
has ratio
q_{v_{0}}
, and the residue characteristic of
v_{0}
is
>5
(
[
BCGP21
, §7.7]
). We take
U^{p}\leq{\mathcal{G}}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\infty,p})
by setting
U_{v}={\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{O}}_{v})
for
v\neq v_{0}
and
U_{v_{0}}={\mathrm{Iw}}_{1}(v_{0})
.

Fix a place
w|p
of
F
. Let
\psi_{w}\in\Psi(\overline{\rho})
. Then there exists a unique
{\psi_{p}}\in\Psi(\overline{r}_{p})
such that
\psi_{v}\simeq\psi_{w}
for each
v\in S_{p}
as a character of
G_{F_{v}}\simeq G_{K}
. We apply the construction in §
5.4.6
by taking
S=S_{p}\cup\{v_{0}\}
,
R=\emptyset
, and
{\mathcal{D}}_{v}={\mathcal{D}}_{v}^{\square,\psi_{v}}
for each
v\in S
. Again by choosing Taylor–Wiles datum and the nonprincipal ultrafilter independent of
{\psi_{p}}
,
\{M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}\}_{\Psi(\overline{\rho})}
is a congruent set of fixed similitude patching functors for
\overline{r}_{p}
.

Let
a
be an integer such that
\HT_{j}(\psi_{w})=a
for all
j\in{\mathcal{J}}
. There exists a unique integer
k
such that
a=2k+b
where
b\in\{3,4\}
. For each
a
, we can and do choose a type
(\lambda_{a},\tau_{a})
such that

\displaystyle\lambda_{a}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}k(1,1;2)&\text{if $b=3$}\\
(1,0;0)+k(1,1;2)&\text{if $b=4$}\end{array}\right.

and
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}},\tau_{a})=t_{\lambda_{a}+\eta}
(using Lemma
5.1.6
in the maximally nonsplit case). We take the ring
R^{p}
in §
5.2
to be

\displaystyle(\widehat{\otimes}_{v\in S_{p}{\backslash}\{w\}}R_{\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}}^{\lambda_{a}+\eta,\tau_{a},\psi_{v}})\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}R_{v_{0}}\llbracket x_{1},\dots,x_{g}\rrbracket.

Note that in the tame case,
R^{p}
is formally smooth over
{\mathcal{O}}
by Theorem
5.1.4
and
[
BCGP21
, Proposition 7.4.2]
. Set
R^{\psi_{w}}_{\infty}=R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\square,\psi_{w}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}R^{p}
.
We define a functor

\displaystyle M_{\infty}^{\psi_{w}}:\Rep_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{K}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}(R^{\psi_{w}}_{\infty})

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle\mapsto\Hom_{{\mathcal{O}}[\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})]}(M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}},((\otimes_{v\in S_{p}{\backslash}\{w\},{\mathcal{O}}}\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda_{a},\tau_{a}))\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}V)^{\vee})^{\vee}

and claim that
\{M_{\infty}^{\psi_{w}}\}_{\psi_{w}\in\Phi(\overline{\rho})}
is a congruent family of potentially diagonalizable minimal fixed similitude patching functors for
\overline{\rho}
.

For a type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
(resp. a Serre weight
\sigma
), the maximal Cohen–Macaulay property of
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{w}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))
(resp.
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{w}}(\sigma)
) follows from Proposition
5.4.7
.

By Lemma
2.5.11
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda_{a},\tau_{a}))\cap W^{?}(\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}}|_{I_{K}})=\{F_{\overline{\rho}}(e)\}.

For
V\in\Rep^{\psi_{w}}_{\mathbf{F}}(\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{K}))
, Theorem
5.4.4
implies

\displaystyle M_{\infty}^{\psi_{w}}(V)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{{\mathcal{O}}[\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})]}(M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}},(\otimes_{v\in S_{p}{\backslash}\{w\}}\overline{\sigma}(\lambda_{0},\tau_{0}))^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}V^{\vee})

\displaystyle=\Hom_{{\mathcal{O}}[\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})]}(M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}/\varpi,(\otimes_{v\in S_{p}{\backslash}\{w\}}F_{\overline{\rho}}(e))^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathbf{F}}V^{\vee}).

Since
\{M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}\}_{\Psi(\overline{\rho})}
is a congruent set of fixed similitude patching functors, this shows that

\left\{M_{\infty}^{\psi_{w}}\right\}_{\psi_{w}\in\Psi(\overline{\rho})}
is a congruent family of fixed similitude patching functors for
\overline{\rho}
. In the tame case,
R_{\infty}^{\lambda_{a}+\eta,\tau_{a},\psi_{w}}[1/p]
is regular and thus
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{w}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))[1/p]
is locally free over its support. Moreover, it has rank one; this can be checked at finite level, which follows from the multiplicity one assertion in Theorem
2.4.1
, the choice of
v_{0}
and Hecke operators at
v_{0}
, and
[
BCGP21
, Proposition 2.4.3, 2.4.4]
. This proves that
\left\{M_{\infty}^{\psi_{w}}\right\}_{\psi_{w}\in\Psi(\overline{\rho})}
is minimal in the tame case.

Finally, we show that
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{w}}
is potentially diagonalizable. Note that, for each
v\in S_{p}{\backslash}\{w\}
, there exists a potentially diagonalizable lift of
\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
of type
(\lambda_{a}+\eta,\tau_{a})
. If
\rho
is a potentially diagonalizable lift of
\overline{\rho}
of type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
compatible with
\psi_{w}
, we can apply
[
PT21
, Theorem 3.4]
to find a potentially diagonalizable lift
r
of
\overline{r}
with
\simc(r)=\psi
. By
[
EL
, Lemma 4.4.4]
,
r
is automorphic, which implies that
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{w}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))
is nonzero.
∎

5.4.8.
Ihara avoidance patching functors

We construct congruent patching functors for “Ihara avoidance” argument. We apply this to the modularity lifting result in §
6.3
.

Let
\overline{r}:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
be a continuous representation and
\chi
be a Hecke character. We assume that

(1)

\overline{r}
satisfies Taylor–Wiles conditions;

(2)

\overline{r}
is unramified at all finite places not dividing
p
; and

(3)

(\overline{r},\chi)
is automorphic.

We apply the construction in §
5.4.6
to two different setups: we let
S
to be a finite set containing
S_{p}\cup\{v_{0}\}
and take
R=S{\backslash}(S_{p}\cup\{v_{0}\})
. For each
v\in R
, we take
\zeta_{v}=(\zeta_{v,1},\zeta_{v,2})
to be either a pair of trivial characters for all
v\in R
, which we call the trivial case, or a pair of continuous characters such that
\zeta_{v,1},\zeta_{v,2}\neq 1

\zeta_{v,1}\neq\zeta_{v,2}^{\pm 1}
for all
v\in R
, which we call the non-trivial case. In the trivial case (resp. the non-trivial case), we obtain the following data

(1)

a global deformation problem
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
(resp.
{\mathcal{S}}_{\zeta}
) and a ring
R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}
(resp.
R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\zeta}}
) representing it;

(2)

a fixed similitude patching functor
({\psi_{p}},R^{1,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty},M^{1,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty})
(resp.
({\psi_{p}},R^{\zeta,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty},M^{\zeta,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty})
) for
\overline{r}_{p}
;

(3)

a
{\mathcal{O}}[{\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})]
-module

\displaystyle M^{1,{\psi_{p}}}:=\varprojlim_{H\leq{\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})}(S_{\chi}(H\cdot U^{p}(R),{\mathcal{O}})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\psi}^{S},1})^{\vee}\ \ \ \textrm{(resp.~}M^{\zeta,{\psi_{p}}}:=\varprojlim_{H\leq{\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})}(S_{\chi}(H\cdot U^{p}(R),{\mathcal{O}})_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{r},\psi}^{S},\zeta_{R}})^{\vee}\textrm{)}.

Proposition 5.4.9
.

We continue using the notation above.

(1)

The fixed similitude patching functors
({\psi_{p}},R^{1,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty},M^{1,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty})
and
({\psi_{p}},R^{\zeta,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty},M^{\zeta,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty})
are congruent.

(2)

The following diagrams of
S_{\infty}
-algebras commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\infty}^{1,{\psi_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{S_{\infty}}(M_{\infty}^{1,{\psi_{p}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{\infty}^{\zeta,{\psi_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{S_{\infty}}(M_{\infty}^{\zeta,{\psi_{p}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{{\mathcal{O}}}(M^{1,{\psi_{p}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\zeta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\End_{{\mathcal{O}}}(M^{\zeta,{\psi_{p}}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bmod{\mathfrak{a}}_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bmod{\mathfrak{a}}_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bmod{\mathfrak{a}}_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bmod{\mathfrak{a}}_{\infty}}

where the top rows modulo
{\mathfrak{a}}_{\infty}
are isomorphic to the corresponding bottom rows.

(3)

The isomorphism
M_{\infty}^{1,{\psi_{p}}}/\varpi\simeq M_{\infty}^{\zeta,{\psi_{p}}}/\varpi
reduced modulo
{\mathfrak{a}}_{\infty}
is the natural isomorphism
M^{1,{\psi_{p}}}/\varpi\simeq M^{\zeta,{\psi_{p}}}/\varpi
.

Proof.

Since all objects in the
{\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{O}}_{p})
-patching data in the trivial and non-trivial cases are congruent modulo
\varpi
, item (1) follows immediately. Item (2) and (3) follow from the construction.
∎

6.
Applications

6.1.
The Breuil–Mézard conjecture

We first state the
geometric
and
versal
Breuil–Mezard conjectures for
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
following
[
LLHLMa
, §8.1]
.

Recall that the algebraic stack
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}
is equidimensional of dimension
4f
and its irreducible components are labeled by Serre weights of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(k)
. We write
\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}]
for the free abelian group generated by irreducible components of
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}
. We call elements in
\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}]

cycles
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}\in\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}]
for a Serre weight
\sigma
an
irreducible cycle
. A cycle is
effective
if it is a sum of irreducible cycles with non-negative coefficients.

For a type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
, we define a cycle
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau}:=\sum_{\sigma}\mu_{\sigma}({\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}){\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}\in\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}]
where
\mu_{\sigma}({\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau})
is the multiplicity of
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}
as an irreducible component of
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
in the sense of
[
Sta18
,
Tag 0DR4
]
.

Conjecture 6.1.1

(Geometric Breuil–Mezard conjecture for
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}
be a set of types. For each
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}({\mathcal{S}})):=\cup_{(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
,
there exists an effective cycle
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}\in\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}]
such that for all
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau}=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))}[\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau):\sigma]{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}.

Let
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}(\mathbf{F})
. We fix a versal ring
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{ver}}
for
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}
at
\overline{\rho}
. For example, we can take
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{ver}}=R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\square}
the framed deformation ring. For a type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
, we define

\displaystyle\spf R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{ver},\lambda+\eta,\tau}

\displaystyle:=\spf R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{ver}}\times_{{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}}{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}

\displaystyle\spf R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}

\displaystyle:=\spf R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{ver}}\times_{{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}}{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}.

Note that the versal map
\spf R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}
is effective and arises from a map
i_{\overline{\rho}}:\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}
. The map
i_{\overline{\rho}}
induces a surjective map from the set of irreducible components of
\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}
to the set of irreducible components of
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}
containing
\overline{\rho}
(
[
Sta18
,
Tag 0DRB
]
). Define
\mathbf{Z}[\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}]
as the free abelian group generated by irreducible components of
\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}
. We interpret
\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}]
and
\mathbf{Z}[\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}]
as spaces of functions on sets of irreducible components. Then we have a pullback

\displaystyle i^{*}_{\overline{\rho}}:\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}]\rightarrow\mathbf{Z}[\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}].

We define
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau}(\overline{\rho}):=i^{*}_{\overline{\rho}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau})
. The cycle
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau}(\overline{\rho})
is equal to the cycle corresponding to
\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{ver},\lambda+\eta,\tau}/\varpi
(
[
Sta18
,
Tag 0DRD
]
).

Conjecture 6.1.2

(Versal Breuil–Mezard conjecture for
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}
be a set of types. For each
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}({\mathcal{S}}))
, there exists an effective cycle
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho})\in\mathbf{Z}[\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}]
such that for all
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau}(\overline{\rho})=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))}[\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau):\sigma]{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho}).

Let
\Lambda\subset X_{*}(\underline{T}^{\vee})
be a finite set of dominant cocharacters containing 0. We define
{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
as the set of types
(\lambda^{\prime}+\eta,\tau)
where
\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda
and
\tau
is
P_{\lambda+\eta,e}P_{22+h_{\lambda+\eta}}
-generic for some
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
The following is the main result of this subsection.

Theorem 6.1.3
.

Let
\overline{\rho}
be a 22-generic semisimple
L
-homomorphism. For each Serre weight
\sigma
, there exists an effective cycle
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho})\in\mathbf{Z}[\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}]
such that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau}(\overline{\rho})=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))}[\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau):\sigma]{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho}).

for all
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
.

Before we prove the Theorem, we discuss about cycles in the fixed similitude deformation ring. Suppose that
C
is an irreducible component in
\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}/\varpi
for a type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
and
{\psi_{p}}\in\Phi(\overline{\rho})
. By Lemma
5.1.2
, unramified twists of
C
give an irreducible component
\widetilde{C}\subset\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}/\varpi
. Its image in
\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\square}/\varpi
is independent of the choice of
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
and
{\psi_{p}}
.

Let
\mathbf{Z}[R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{p\text{-}crys}}]
be a free abelian group generated by
4[K:\mathbf{Q}_{p}]+10
-dimensional cycles in
\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\square}/\varpi
supported in the union of
\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}/\varpi
for all choices of a dominant character
\lambda\in X^{*}(\underline{T})
, a (possibly trivial) tame inertial
L
-parameters
\tau
, and the unique character
{\psi_{p}}\in\Psi(\overline{\rho})
compatible with
(\lambda,\tau)
. By the previous paragraph, there is a natural injective group homomorphism

(6.1.4)

\displaystyle\widetilde{(\cdot)}:\mathbf{Z}[R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{p\text{-}crys}}]\hookrightarrow\mathbf{Z}[\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}]

such that
\widetilde{Z}(R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}/\varpi)={\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau}(\overline{\rho})
for all
\lambda
,
\tau
, and
{\psi_{p}}
as above.

Let
R
be a Noetherian
\mathbf{F}
-algebra. Given a
R
-module
M
, we let
Z(M)
denote the cycle

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathcal{C}}}\mu_{\mathcal{C}}(M){\mathcal{C}}\in\mathbf{Z}[\spec R]

where
{\mathcal{C}}
ranges over irreducible components of
\spec R
, and
\mu_{\mathcal{C}}(M)
denotes the length of the module
M_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{\mathcal{C}}}
over
R_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{\mathcal{C}}}
for the prime ideal
{{\mathfrak{p}}_{\mathcal{C}}}
corresponding to
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Proof of Theorem
6.1.3
.

By Theorem
5.4.2
, there is a congruent family of minimal fixed similitude patching functors
\{M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}\}_{\psi_{p}\in\Psi(\overline{\rho})}
for
\overline{\rho}
.
For a Serre weight
\sigma
, we define
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho}):=\widetilde{Z}(M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma))
for some choice of
{\psi_{p}}\in\Phi(\overline{\rho})
. Note that this is independent of the choice of
{\psi_{p}}
.
For any
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
,
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
is domain by Theorem
4.4.3
. Let
\psi_{p}\in\Phi(\overline{\rho})
be the unique character compatible with
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
. By
[
EG14
, Lemma 2.2.7, 2.2.10]
and the definition of (
6.1.4
), we have
\widetilde{Z}(M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau)))=Z_{\lambda,\tau}(\overline{\rho})
and

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}(M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau)))=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))}[\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau):\sigma]\widetilde{Z}(M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma)).

We explain how one can interpolate Theorem
6.1.3
to prove a version of geometric Breuil–Mezard conjecture. We follow the axiomatic argument of
[
LLHLMa
, §8.3]
.
Let
{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}}
be the set of
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}}}(\mathbf{F})
such that
\overline{\rho}|_{I_{K}}
is 22-generic tame inertial type for
K
over
\mathbf{F}
. Note that Theorem
6.1.3
holds for
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}}
and
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
. We define a set
\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}
to be a union of
{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
and a set of types of the form
(\eta,\tau)
where
\tau
is 6-generic. We also define a set
\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{elim}}
to be the union of
\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}
and the set of types
(\eta,\tau)
.

Definition 6.1.5
.

Let
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}}
. We say that a Serre weight
\sigma
is
(\overline{\rho},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{elim}}
)-irrelevant
if there exists a type
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{elim}}
such that
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
and
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}=0
.

Lemma 6.1.6
.

Any
\sigma\notin W^{?}(\overline{\rho}|_{I_{K}})
is
(\overline{\rho},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{elim}}
)-irrelevant.

Proof.

Note that any Serre weight in
W^{?}(\overline{\rho}|_{I_{K}})
is 19-deep by Proposition
2.5.8
. If
\sigma\simeq F(\lambda)
and
\lambda
is not 12-deep, the third and the last paragraphs of the proof of Theorem
5.4.4
shows that
\sigma
is
(\overline{\rho},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{elim}}
)-irrelevant. Suppose that
\sigma\simeq F(\lambda)
is 12-deep and
not

(\overline{\rho},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{elim}}
)-irrelevant, i.e.
R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}\neq 0
for
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{elim}}
such that
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
. For each
s\in\underline{W}
, let
\tau_{s}=\tau(s,\widetilde{w}_{h}\cdot\lambda+\eta)
be a
9
-generic tame inertial
L
-parameter. Since
F(\lambda)\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau_{s}))
, we have
R_{\infty}^{\eta,\tau_{s}}\neq 0
. Then Proposition
5.1.3
and Lemma
2.5.12
imply that
F(\lambda)\in W^{?}(\overline{r}_{p}^{\mathrm{ss}})
(as explained in the first paragraph of the proof of Theorem
5.4.4
).
∎

Lemma 6.1.7
.

(1)

Suppose
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
for some
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
. Then there exists
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}}
such that
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}
.

(2)

Let
\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda})]
be the
\mathbf{Z}
-span of all irreducible components in
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
for some
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
.
Then the map

\displaystyle i_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}}^{*}:=\prod_{\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}}}i_{\overline{\rho}}^{*}|_{\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda})]}:\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda})]\rightarrow\prod_{\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}}}\mathbf{Z}[\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}]

is injective. Moreover,
Z\in\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda})]
is effective if and only if
i^{*}_{\overline{\rho}}(Z)
is effective for all
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}}
.

Proof.

(1)

By Theorem
4.5.2
,
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
for some
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
implies that
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
. By Proposition
4.5.6
, it suffices to find
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}}
such that
\sigma\in W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{\rho}|_{I_{K}})
. This can be proven as
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 8.4.9]
.

(2)

This easily follows from (1) (see Lemma 8.2.2 in
loc. cit.
).

∎

Lemma 6.1.8
.

For any Serre weight
\sigma
, there exists integers
n_{\lambda,\tau}^{\sigma}
such that

\displaystyle[\sigma]-\sum_{(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}n_{\lambda,\tau}^{\sigma}[\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau)]

is supported only at
({\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{elim}}
)-irrelevant Serre weights. In other words,
\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}
is
({\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda,\mathrm{elim}}
)-Breuil–Mézard system in the sense of
[
LLHLMa
, Definition 8.3.3]
.

Proof.

This can be proven as
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 8.4.4]
using Lemma
6.1.6
.
∎

Lemma
6.1.7
shows that for each
\sigma
as above, if there exists
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}\in\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda})]
such that
i^{*}_{\overline{\rho}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma})={\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
for all
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}}
, then Conjecture
6.1.1
for
{\mathcal{S}}={\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
follows from Theorem
6.1.3
.
Due to Lemma
6.1.8
, one may hope to define
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}
by

\displaystyle\sum_{(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}n_{\lambda,\tau}^{\sigma}{\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau}.

However, the condition
i_{\overline{\rho}}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma})={\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
may not hold in this case. This is because Theorem
6.1.3
does not apply to
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}{\backslash}{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
. This motivates the following definitions.

Definition 6.1.9

(cf. Definition 8.3.3 in
[
LLHLMa
]
)
.

Let
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}))
be a Serre weight.

(1)

We say that
\sigma

\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}
-covers

\sigma^{\prime}
if for
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}
such that
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
,
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
lies in
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
.

(2)

We say that
\sigma
is
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda})
-generic
if for all Serre weights
\sigma^{\prime}
such that
\sigma

\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}
-covers
\sigma^{\prime}
,
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
does not lie in
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}
for any
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}{\backslash}{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
.

Lemma 6.1.10
.

Suppose
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
are 9-deep Serre weights, and
\sigma

\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}
-covers
\sigma^{\prime}
. Then
\sigma^{\prime}\uparrow\sigma
.

Proof.

Let
\tau
be a 6-generic tame inertial
L
-parameter such that
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau)
.
Since
\sigma

\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}
-covers
\sigma^{\prime}
,
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},\mathbf{F}}^{\eta,\tau}
. By Theorem
4.5.2
, we have
\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
.
In particular,

\sigma
covers
\sigma^{\prime}
in the sense of
[
LLHLMa
, Definition 2.3.10]
, i.e.

\displaystyle{\sigma^{\prime}}\in\bigcap_{{\small\begin{array}[]{cc}\text{$\tau$ $6$-generic}\\
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))\end{array}}}\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau)).

Then our claim follows from the equivalence between item (1) and (4) in Proposition 2.3.12 in
loc. cit.
using 9-deepness of
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
. (Note that in our setup, if
\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{W}^{+}
and
\widetilde{w}_{1}\uparrow\widetilde{w}
for some
\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{W}^{+}_{1}
, then
\widetilde{w}_{1}\in\widetilde{W}_{1}^{+}
. Thus
L(\pi^{-1}(\widetilde{w}_{1})\cdot(\omega-\eta))|_{\mathrm{G}}
in item (4) in
loc. cit.
is equal to
F(\pi^{-1}(\widetilde{w}_{1})\cdot(\omega-\eta))=F_{(\widetilde{w}_{1},\omega)}
and
F_{(\widetilde{w}_{1},\omega)}\uparrow F_{(\widetilde{w},\omega)}
.)
∎

Let
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda}\subset{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
be the subset consisting of
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)
such that all
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
are
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda})
-generic.

For
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}))
, we define
\tr_{\sigma,\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}
an idempotent endomorphism of
\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}]
which maps
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
to itself if
\sigma

\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}
-covers
\sigma^{\prime}
and to 0 otherwise. The following Lemma shows that
\tr_{\sigma,\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}
eliminates the contribution of types that Theorem
6.1.3
does not apply to.

Lemma 6.1.11
.

If
\sigma
is
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda})
-generic, we have
\tr_{\sigma,\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau})=0
for any
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}{\backslash}{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}
.

Proof.

This follows from the definition of
\tr_{\sigma,\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}
.
∎

For
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}}
and
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda})
-generic
\sigma
, there is an unique idempotent endomorphism
\tr_{\sigma,\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}(\overline{\rho})
of
\mathbf{Z}[\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}]
satisfying the following condition (see
[
Sta18
,
Tag 0DRB
,
Tag 0DRD
]
)

(6.1.12)

\displaystyle i^{*}_{\overline{\rho}}\circ\tr_{\sigma,\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}=\tr_{\sigma,\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}(\overline{\rho})\circ i^{*}_{\overline{\rho}}.

Then we have the following equality (see,
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 8.3.7]
)

(6.1.13)

\displaystyle\tr_{\sigma,\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}(\overline{\rho})({\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho}))={\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho}).

Theorem 6.1.14
.

For each
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda})
-generic
\sigma
in
\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}({\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda}))
, there exists a unique effective cycle
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}
in
\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}]
with the support contained in
\left\{{\mathcal{C}}_{\kappa}|\kappa\uparrow\sigma\right\}
and for each
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}}
,
i^{*}_{\overline{\rho}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma})={\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho})
. Moreover, for
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau}=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))}[\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau):\sigma]{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}.

In particular, Conjecture
6.1.1
holds for
{\mathcal{S}}={\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda}
.

Proof.

We define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}:=\tr_{\sigma,\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}\left(\sum_{(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}n^{\sigma}_{\lambda,\tau}{\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau}\right).

For each
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}}
, choose a fixed similitude patching functor
(\psi_{\overline{\rho}},R_{\infty}^{\psi_{\overline{\rho}}},M_{\infty}^{\psi_{\overline{\rho}}})
. We have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{Z}(M_{\infty}^{\psi_{\overline{\rho}}}(\sigma))

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}n_{\lambda,\tau}^{\sigma}\widetilde{Z}(M_{\infty}^{\psi_{\overline{\rho}}}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau)))

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}n_{\lambda,\tau}^{\sigma}\tr_{\sigma,\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}(\overline{\rho})(\widetilde{Z}(M_{\infty}^{\psi_{\overline{\rho}}}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))))

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}}n_{\lambda,\tau}^{\sigma}\tr_{\sigma,\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}(\overline{\rho})(\widetilde{Z}(M_{\infty}^{\psi_{\overline{\rho}}}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))))

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda}}n_{\lambda,\tau}^{\sigma}\tr_{\sigma,\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}}(\overline{\rho})({\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau}(\overline{\rho}))

\displaystyle=i^{*}_{\overline{\rho}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma})

where the first equality is by definition, second equality follows from Lemma
6.1.8
and
6.1.6
, the third equality follows from (
6.1.13
), the fourth equality follows from Lemma
6.1.11
, the fifth equality follows from the proof of Theorem
6.1.3
, and the final equality follows from the definition of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}
and (
6.1.12
). Now the uniquiness and effectivity of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}
, as well as the claimed equality, follow from Lemma
6.1.7
.
∎

Remark 6.1.15
.

If there is a Breuil–Mezard system
\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}^{\prime}
containing
\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}
such that
\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda}
is a
({\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{ss}},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}^{\prime})
-Breuil–Mezard system and Conjecture
6.1.1
holds for
\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}^{\prime}
, then the cycles constructed in Theorem
6.1.14
conincide with those in Conjecture
6.1.1
for
\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}^{\prime}
(cf.
[
LLHLMa
, Theorem 8.3.5(4)]
).

Although the set
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda}
is not characterized by genericity conditions, there is a smaller subset that is characterized by genericity conditions. Given a polynomial
f\in\mathbf{Z}[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
and
\omega\in X^{*}(T)\simeq\mathbf{Z}^{3}
, define

\displaystyle f^{\omega}(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}):=\prod_{\nu=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2};\nu_{3})\in{\mathrm{Conv}}(\omega)}f(X_{1}-\nu_{1},X_{2}-\nu_{2},X_{3}-\nu_{3})

We also define

\displaystyle P_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda,e}=\prod_{\lambda\in\Lambda}(P_{\lambda+\eta,e}\prod_{{j\in{\mathcal{J}}}}\widetilde{P}_{\eta,e}^{(\lambda+\eta-w_{0}(\eta))_{j}}).

The following Lemma is a straightforward generalization of
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 8.4.11, 8.5.1]
.

Lemma 6.1.16
.

(1)

If
\tau
is a tame inertial type for
K
with lowest alcove presentation
(s,\mu-\eta)
with
P_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda,e}
-generic
\mu
, then
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda}
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda
.

(2)

If
\overline{\rho}
is a tame inertial type for
K
over
\mathbf{F}
with a lowest alcove presentation
(s,\mu-\eta)
with
P_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda,e}
-generic
\mu
, then for any tame inertial
\tau
type for
K
with a lowest alcove presentation such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)\in{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda+\eta)
, then
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda}
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda
.

Corollary 6.1.17
.

For each Serre weight
\sigma
, there exists an effective cycle
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}
in
\mathbf{Z}[{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}]
with the support contained in
\left\{{\mathcal{C}}_{\kappa}|\kappa\uparrow\sigma\right\}
such that for any
\lambda\in\Lambda
and a tame inertial type
\tau
for
K
with a lowest alcove presentation
(s,\mu_{\eta})
with
P_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda,e}
-generic
\mu
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}_{\lambda,\tau}=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))}[\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau):\sigma]{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.1.16
, for any
\lambda
and
\tau
as above, all
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
is
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda})
-generic. Then the claim follows from Theorem
6.1.14
.
∎

Finally, we can use Corollary
6.1.17
to prove versal Breuil–Mézard conjectures for (not necessarily tame)
\overline{\rho}
with polynomial genericity.

Corollary 6.1.18
.

Let
\overline{\rho}:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
be a continuous representation. Suppose that
\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}}|_{I_{K}}
has a lowest alcove presentation
(s,\mu-\eta)
with
P_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda,e}P_{22}
-generic
\mu
. Then for each Serre weight
\sigma
, there exists a cycle
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho})\in\mathbf{Z}[\spec R_{\overline{\rho}}^{\mathrm{alg}}]
such that for any
\lambda\in\Lambda
and any tame inertial type
\tau
for
K
,

\displaystyle Z(R^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}_{\overline{\rho}}/\varpi)=\sum_{\sigma}[\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau):\sigma]{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho}).

Proof.

If
\sigma
is
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda})
-generic, we define
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho}):=i^{*}_{\overline{\rho}}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma})
with
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}
as in Theorem
6.1.14
. Otherwise, we define
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho})=0
. If
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\in{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda}
, then all
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
are
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda})
-generic, and the claim follows from Theorem
6.1.14
.

If
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\notin{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda}
, we have
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)\notin{\mathrm{Adm}}^{\vee}(\lambda+\eta)
by Lemma
6.1.16
. Then Proposition
5.1.3
shows that
R^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}_{\overline{\rho}}=0
. It remains to show that
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho})=0
for all
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
. If
\sigma
is not
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda})
-generic, this is automatic. Suppose that
\sigma
is
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda})
-generic and
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho})\neq 0
. Then the support of the cycle
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}
contains the point
\overline{\rho}\in{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},{\mathrm{red}}}(\mathbf{F})
. This implies that
\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}}
is in the support of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}
.
In particular,
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}(\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}})=\widetilde{Z}(M_{\infty}^{\psi_{\overline{\rho}}}(\sigma))\neq 0
. However, this implies that
R^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}_{\overline{\rho}^{\mathrm{ss}}}\neq 0
. Then
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho},\tau)\in{\mathrm{Adm}}(\lambda+\eta)
by Proposition
5.1.3
(2). By Lemma
6.1.16
(2), this contradicts our assumption
(\lambda+\eta,\tau)\notin{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathrm{ss}},\Lambda}
. This concludes the proof.
∎

6.2.
The weight part of a Serre’s conjecture

Let
F,\chi,\overline{r}
be as in §
5.3
. We further assume that
\overline{r}
is automorphic of some weight
\mu
and level
U
. Recall that
W(\overline{r})
is the set of modular Serre weights of
\overline{r}
. The following conjecture is due to Gee–Herzig–Savitt (
[
GHS18
]
).

Conjecture 6.2.1
.

If
\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is tame and sufficiently generic at
v|p
, then
W(\overline{r})=W^{?}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})
.

Following
[
LLHLMa
, §9.1]
, we formulate a version of this conjecture without the tameness hypothesis.

We say that a Serre weight
\sigma
is
generic
if
\sigma
is
({\mathcal{S}}_{\{0\}},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\{0\}})
-generic. Note that if
\sigma
is generic, then it is
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda})
-generic for any finite set of dominant cocharacters
\Lambda\subset X_{*}(\underline{T}^{\vee})
(cf.
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 8.4.8]
). We let
W_{\mathrm{gen}}(\overline{r})
be the subset of generic Serre weights in
W(\overline{r})
.

Definition 6.2.2
.

Let
\overline{\rho}:G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}\rightarrow\prescript{L}{}{\underline{G}}(\mathbf{F})
be an
L
-parameter.
We define
W^{\mathrm{BM}}_{\mathrm{gen}}(\overline{\rho})
to be a set of
\sigma=\otimes_{v\in S_{p}}\sigma_{v}
where
\sigma_{v}
is a generic Serre weight such that
\overline{\rho}_{v}
is contained in the support of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma_{v}}
defined in Theorem
6.1.14
.

Using this recipe, we can formulate a generalization of Conjecture
6.2.1
for
\overline{r}
not necessarily tame at places above
p
.

Conjecture 6.2.3
.

The set
W_{\mathrm{gen}}(\overline{r})
is equal to
W^{\mathrm{BM}}_{\mathrm{gen}}(\overline{r}_{p})
.

We also define
W_{g}(\overline{r}_{p})=\{\sigma=\otimes_{v\in S_{p}}\sigma_{v}\mid\sigma_{v}\in W_{g}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{G_{F_{v}}})\}
, a set of Serre weights of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/p)
, where
W_{g}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{G_{F_{v}}})
is the set defined in Proposition
4.5.6
. Note that any
\sigma\in W_{g}(\overline{r}_{p})
is 3-deep.

Let
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},F_{p}}:=\prod_{v\in S_{p},{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},F_{v}}
. By Theorem
4.1.10
, there is a bijection between irreducible components in the underlying reduced substack
{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},F_{p},{\mathrm{red}}}
and isomorphism classes of Serre weights of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/p)
. If
\sigma=\otimes_{v\in S_{p}}\sigma_{v}
is a Serre weight of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{F}/p)
, we denote its corresponding irreducible component by
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}:=\prod_{v\in S_{p},{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma_{v}}
.

Lemma 6.2.4
.

Let
\sigma\in W_{g}(\overline{r}_{p})
. Let
R_{\overline{r}_{p}}:=\widehat{\otimes}_{v\in S_{p}}R_{\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}}^{\square}
with a versal morphism

\displaystyle\iota_{\overline{r}_{p}}:R_{\overline{r}_{p}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},F_{p}}.

If
\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}}
is
22
-generic, then
\iota^{*}_{\overline{r}_{p}}({\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma})
is an irreducible cycle corresponding to an irreducible subscheme
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}(\overline{r}_{p})\subset\spec R_{\overline{r}_{p}}/\varpi
. Moreover, if
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}\subset\prod_{v\in S_{p},{\mathcal{O}}}{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathrm{Sym}},F_{v},\mathbf{F}}^{\lambda_{v}+\eta_{v},\tau_{v}}
, then
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}(\overline{r}_{p})
is contained in
\spec R_{\overline{r}_{p}}^{\lambda+\eta,\tau}/\varpi
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.5.6
,
W_{g}(\overline{r}_{p})\subset W^{?}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})
. By Lemma
2.5.14
and
[
LLHLMa
, Remark 2.1.8]
, there is a tame inertial
L
-parameter
\tau
with a lowest alcove representation such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{\rho}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}},\tau)\in{\mathrm{Adm}}(\eta)
and
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
. Since
\overline{\rho}
is 22-generic, such
\tau
is 19-generic. Then the first claim follows from Theorem
4.5.4
and Proposition
3.7.3
. The second claim follows from the definition of
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}(\overline{r}_{p})
.
∎

The following is the main result of this subsection.

Theorem 6.2.5
.

There exists a polynomial
P(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3})\in\mathbf{Z}[X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}]
independent of
p
such that if
\overline{r}:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{F})
is automorphic, satisfies Taylor–Wiles conditions, and for each
v|p
,
\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is tame with a lowest alcove presentation
(s_{v},\mu_{v}-\eta)
with
P
-generic
\mu_{v}
, then the Conjecture
6.2.1
and
6.2.3
hold for
\overline{r}
.

Proof.

We claim that there exists a polynomial
Q(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3})
such that if
\mu_{v}
is
Q
-generic, then
W^{?}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})=W_{g}(\overline{r}_{p})
. It follows from Proposition
4.5.6
(1) that
W_{g}(\overline{r}_{p})\subset W(\overline{r})
. Let

\displaystyle Q(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}):=\prod_{\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{W}_{1}^{+}/X^{0}(T)}\prod_{\widetilde{w}_{2}\uparrow\widetilde{w},\widetilde{w}_{2}\in\widetilde{W}^{+}}\prod_{w\in W}P_{\widetilde{w}}(X+w\widetilde{w}_{2}^{-1}(0)).

with the polynomial
P_{\widetilde{w}}
in Proposition
4.5.6
. Let
\sigma\in W^{?}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})
with a lowest alcove presentation
(\widetilde{w},\omega)
compatible with
(s,\mu-\eta)
. If
\mu_{v}
is
Q
-generic, then
\omega_{v}
is
P_{\widetilde{w}}
(this can be checked directly using Proposition
2.5.8
). Thus, Proposition
4.5.6
(2) implies that
W^{?}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})=W_{g}(\overline{r}_{p})
.

We take
P
to be the product
P_{22}P_{2\eta,e}P_{\eta,e}^{\eta_{0}}Q
and assume that
\mu_{v}
is
P
-generic.

Step 1

We first show that
W(\overline{r})\cap W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})
is nonempty.
Let
\tau
be the tame inertial
L
-parameter with a lowest alcove presentation
\eta
-compatible with that of
\overline{r}_{p}
and
\widetilde{w}(\tau)=\widetilde{w}(\overline{r}_{p})t_{-\eta-w_{0}\eta}
(note that this condition and
P_{2\eta,e}P_{22}
-genericity of
\mu_{v}
imply that
\tau
is
P_{2\eta,e}P_{22}
-generic). By
[
LLHLMa
, Lemma 2.6.7]
,
W^{?}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})\subset\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\eta,\tau))
. For the unique
\psi_{p}\in\Psi(\overline{r}_{p})
compatible with
(2\eta,\tau)
and a fixed similitude patching functor
M^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty}
for
\overline{r}_{p}
(which exists by Theorem
5.4.2
), the automorphy of
\overline{r}
implies that
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\eta,\tau))\neq 0
. By Theorem
4.4.3
and
P_{2\eta}
-genericity of
\tau
,
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\eta,\tau))
has full support on
\spec R_{\overline{r}_{p}}^{2\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}
. By Lemma
6.2.4
, we have
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}(\overline{r}_{p})\subset\spec R_{\overline{r}_{p}}^{2\eta,\tau}/\varpi
for any
\sigma\in W^{?}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})
. Then there exists
\sigma^{\prime}\in W^{?}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})
such that
\widetilde{Z}(M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma^{\prime}))
is supported on
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}(\overline{r}_{p})
. Since the support of
\widetilde{Z}(M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma^{\prime}))={\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(\overline{\rho})
is contained in
\{{\mathcal{C}}_{\kappa}|\kappa\uparrow\sigma^{\prime}\}
, this in particular implies that
\sigma\uparrow\sigma^{\prime}
.
Take
\sigma\simeq F(\lambda)\in W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})
such that
\lambda
is in the highest
p
-restricted alcove. Then
\sigma\uparrow\sigma^{\prime}
implies that
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}
, and
\sigma
is also contained in
W(\overline{r})
.

Step 2

We show that
W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})\subset W(\overline{r})
. Let
\sigma\in W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})
. We can write
\sigma=F_{\overline{\rho}}(w^{-1})
for some
w\in\underline{W}
. We take
\tau
to be
19
-generic tame inertial type such that
\widetilde{w}(\overline{r}_{p},\tau)=t_{w(\eta)}
. By Lemma
2.5.11
,
\sigma\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
.

There exists a unique
{\psi_{p}}\in\Psi(\overline{r}_{p})
compatible with
(\eta,\tau)
and a fixed similitude patching functor
M_{\infty}^{\psi_{p}}
for
\overline{r}_{p}
