. By the previous step, we can choose
\sigma\in W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})\cap W(\overline{r})
. Then we have

M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\tau_{0}))\neq 0
. Thus, there exists a lift
r_{0}
of
\overline{r}
such that is potentially crystalline of type
(\eta_{v},\tau_{0,v})
at
v|p
. By Theorem
5.1.4
, this shows that
\overline{r}
is potentially diagonalizably automorphic. For an arbitrary
\sigma\in W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})
, we can apply Theorem
5.1.4
,
[
Boo19
, Theorem 1.1]
, and
[
PT21
, Theorem 3.4]
to find a lift
r
of
\overline{r}
that is potentially diagonalizable of type
(\eta_{v},\tau_{v})
at
v|p
. By
[
EL
, Lemma 4.4.4]
,
r
is automorphic and
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\tau))\neq 0
. By reducing modulo
p
,
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma)\neq 0
and thus
\sigma\in W(\overline{r})
.

Step 3

We show that
W(\overline{r})=W^{?}(\overline{r}_{p}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})
.
Let
\sigma\in W^{?}(\overline{r}|_{I_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}})
and
\tau
be the type satisfying conditions in Lemma
2.5.14
. Since
\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))\cap W^{?}(\overline{r}|_{I_{K}})
is nonempty if and only if
\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))\cap W_{\mathrm{obv}}(\overline{r}|_{I_{K}})
is nonempty, the previous step implies that
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\tau))\neq 0
and it has full support on
\spec R_{\overline{r}_{p}}^{\eta,\tau}
, by Theorem
4.4.3
and the
P^{\eta_{0}}_{\eta,e}
-genericity of
\mu
. Since
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}(\overline{r}_{p})\subset\spec R_{\overline{r}_{p}}^{\eta,\tau}
by Lemma
6.2.4
, there exists
\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathrm{JH}(\overline{\sigma}(\tau))
such that
M^{\psi_{p}}_{\infty}(\sigma^{\prime})
is supported on
{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}(\overline{r}_{p})
. As we argued at the end of Step 1, we have
\sigma\uparrow\sigma^{\prime}
, which implies
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}
by Lemma
2.5.14
and thus
\sigma\in W(\overline{r})
. Thus the Conjecture
6.2.1
holds for
\overline{r}
.

Step 4

It remains to prove that
W_{\mathrm{gen}}(\overline{r})=W^{\mathrm{BM}}_{\mathrm{gen}}(\overline{r}_{p})
. Let
\sigma
be a generic Serre weight. Then
\sigma
is
({\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda},\widehat{{\mathcal{S}}}_{\Lambda})
-generic. Let
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}
be a fixed similitude patching functor for
\overline{r}_{p}
constructed in Theorem
5.4.2
(1). Then
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma)\neq 0
if and only if
\sigma\in W(\overline{r})
. On the other hand,
\sigma\in W^{\mathrm{BM}}_{\mathrm{gen}}(\overline{r}_{p})
is equivalent to
\overline{r}_{p}\in\iota_{\overline{r}_{p}}^{*}({\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma})
, which is equivalent to
M_{\infty}^{{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma)\neq 0
by the construction of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{\sigma}
. Thus the Conjecture
6.2.3
holds for
\overline{r}
.
∎

6.3.
Modularity lifting in generic tamely potentially crystalline case

We prove the following modularity lifting result.

Theorem 6.3.1
.

Let
r:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(E)
be a continuous representation satisfying following conditions:

(1)

r
is unramified at all but finitely many places;

(2)

r|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is potentially crystalline of type
(\lambda_{v}+\eta_{v},\tau_{v})
with a lowest alcove presentation
(s_{v},\mu_{v}-\eta_{v})
with
P_{\lambda+\eta,e}
-generic
\mu_{v}
;

(3)

\overline{r}
satisfies Taylor–Wiles hypothesis;

(4)

\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is tame at
v|p
; and

(5)

\overline{r}\simeq\overline{r}_{\pi,p,\iota}
for some cuspidal automorphic representation
\pi
of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
of weight
\lambda
and central character
\chi
such that
\sigma(\tau_{v})
is a
K
-type for
\pi
at
v|p
.

Then
r\simeq r_{\widetilde{\pi},p,\iota}
for some cuspidal automorphic representation
\widetilde{\pi}
of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
of weight
\lambda
central character
\chi
such that
\sigma(\tau_{v})
is a
K
-type for
\widetilde{\pi}
at
v|p
.

The Theorem follows from the following Lemma and base change argument.

Lemma 6.3.2
.

Let
r:G_{F}\rightarrow\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(E)
be a continuous representation satisfying following conditions:

(1)

r
is unramified at all but finitely many places;

(2)

if
r
is ramified at a place
v\nmid p
, then
\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is trivial,
r|_{G_{F_{v}}}
has only unipotent ramification, and
q_{w}=1\bmod p
;

(3)

r|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is potentially crystalline of type
(\lambda_{v}+\eta_{v},\tau_{v})
with a lowest alcove presentation
(s_{v},\mu_{v}-\eta_{v})
with
P_{\lambda+\eta,e}
-generic
\mu_{v}
;

(4)

\overline{r}
satisfies Taylor–Wiles hypothesis;

(5)

\overline{r}|_{G_{F_{v}}}
is tame at
v|p
; and

(6)

\overline{r}\simeq\overline{r}_{\pi,p,\iota}
for some cuspidal automorphic representation
\pi
of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
of weight
\lambda
central character
\chi
such that
\sigma(\tau_{v})
is a
K
-type for
\pi
at
v|p
and for all finite places
v
of
F
,
(\pi_{v})^{{\mathrm{Iw}}(v)}\neq 0
.

Then
r\simeq r_{\widetilde{\pi},p,\iota}
for some cuspidal automorphic representation
\widetilde{\pi}
of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
of weight
\lambda
central character
\chi
such that
\sigma(\tau_{v})
is a
K
-type for
\widetilde{\pi}
at
v|p
.

Proof.

We apply Proposition
5.4.9
to
F
,
\chi
,
S=S_{r}\cup\{v_{0}\}
. By the assumptions on
r
, it gives an
E
-point of
\spec R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}
. On the other hand,
r_{\pi,p,\iota}
gives an
E
-point of
\spec R_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\zeta}}
. Thus the module
M^{\chi,{\psi_{p}}}
is nonzero. By Theorem
4.4.3
,
P_{\lambda+\eta,e}
-genericity of
\mu
,
[
BCGP21
, Proposition 7.4.2]
, and Proposition
5.1.8
(2),
M_{\infty}^{\zeta,{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))
has full support over
R^{\zeta,\lambda+\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty}
. By the congruence between
M_{\infty}^{1,{\psi_{p}}}
and
M_{\infty}^{\zeta,{\psi_{p}}}
,
M_{\infty}^{1,{\psi_{p}}}(\overline{\sigma}(\lambda,\tau))
has full support over
R^{1,\lambda+\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty}/\varpi
. Then by Proposition
5.1.8
(1),
M_{\infty}^{1,{\psi_{p}}}(\sigma^{\circ}(\lambda,\tau))
has full support over
R^{1,\lambda+\eta,\tau,{\psi_{p}}}_{\infty}
. This proves the claim.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.3.1
.

There exists a solvable extension of totally real fields
F^{\prime}/F
satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

F^{\prime}/F
is linearly disjoint from
F^{\ker\overline{r}}
;

(2)

any place
v|p
of
F
splits completely in
F^{\prime}
;

(3)

if
r|_{G_{F^{\prime}}}
is ramified at a place
w
of
F^{\prime}
lying over a place
v\nmid p
of
F
, then
\overline{r}|_{G_{F^{\prime}_{w}}}
is trivial,
r|_{G_{F^{\prime}_{w}}}
has only unipotent ramification, and
q_{w}=1\bmod p
;

(4)

there is a cuspidal automorphic representation
{\pi^{\prime}}
of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A}_{F^{\prime}})
of weight
\lambda^{\prime}=(\lambda_{w})
, where
\lambda_{w}=\lambda_{v}
for a place
v
of
F
and
w|v
, which is a base change of
\pi
, and for all finite places
w
of
F^{\prime}
,
(\pi^{\prime}_{w})^{{\mathrm{Iw}}(w)}\neq 0
(here we use
[
Mok14
, Proposition 4.13]
).

Then by
[
BCGP21
, Lemma 8.3.2]
(which easily generalizes to our setup), it suffices to show that there exists a cuspidal automorphic representation
\widetilde{\pi}^{\prime}
of
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbb{A}_{F^{\prime}})
of weight
\lambda^{\prime}
such that for a place
w|p
of
F^{\prime}
above a place
v
of
F
,
\sigma(\tau_{v})
is a
K
-type for
\pi
at
w
, and
r|_{G_{F^{\prime}}}\simeq r_{\widetilde{\pi}^{\prime},p,\iota}
. This follows from Lemma
6.3.2
.
∎

Appendix A
Torus fixed points of certain affine Springer fibers

In this section, we explain how the main result of
[
BA
]
generalizes to the group
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
.
Let
\mathrm{Fl}_{\mathbf{C}}
be the affine flag variety over
\mathbf{C}
whose
\mathbf{C}
-points are given by
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{C}(\!(v)\!))/\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{C}}
, where
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{C}}\subset\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{C}\llbracket v\rrbracket)
is the Iwahori subgroup. Let
{\mathbf{a}}\in\Lie\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{C})
be a regular semisimple element. We consider the affine Springer fiber
\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}}}
associated to the element
v{\mathbf{a}}\in\Lie\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{C}(\!(v)\!))
. The
\mathbf{C}
-points of
\mathrm{Fl}_{\mathbf{C}}
correspond to
Iwahori subalgebras
inside the loop algebra
\Lie\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{C}(\!(v)\!))
, i.e.
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{C}(\!(v)\!))
-conjugates of the Lie algebra of the Iwahori subgroup. Under this correspondence, the
\mathbf{C}
-points of
\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}}}
are characterized as Iwahori subalgebras containing the element
vs
. If
g\in\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{C}(\!(v)\!))
, then its image in
\mathrm{Fl}_{\mathbf{C}}(\mathbf{C})
is in
\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}}}
if and only if
v{\mathbf{a}}\in gL^{+}{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{C}}g^{-1}
, or equivalently,
g^{-1}{\mathbf{a}}g\in\frac{1}{v}L^{+}{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathbf{C}}
.

Recall that
\mathrm{Fl}_{\mathbf{C}}
admits
T
-action induced by the left multiplication on
L\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
. The
T
-fixed points of
\mathrm{Fl}_{\mathbf{C}}
are given by the image of the map
\widetilde{W}\rightarrow\mathrm{Fl}_{\mathbf{C}}(\mathbf{C})
sending
wt_{\lambda}
to
\phi(w)v^{-\phi(\lambda)}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}
. Note that this is a composition of the map
(-)^{*}:\widetilde{W}\rightarrow\widetilde{W}^{\vee}
, taking inverse on
\widetilde{W}^{\vee}
, and the natural embedding of
\widetilde{W}^{\vee}
into
\mathrm{Fl}_{\mathbf{C}}(\mathbf{C})
. For simplicity, we write the image of
\widetilde{w}
under this map by
\widetilde{w}^{-*}
. We want to further understand which of these
T
-fixed points are contained in
\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}}}
. Let
\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{W}
. We define

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}}}(\widetilde{w}):=\mathrm{GSp}_{4}(\mathbf{C}\llbracket v\rrbracket)\widetilde{w}^{-*}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}\cap\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}}}.

By
[
BA
, Proposition 2.1]
, if
\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{W}_{1}^{+}
, then
\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}}}(\widetilde{w})
is irreducible of dimension 4. Let
\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{w}}
be the closure of
\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}}}(\widetilde{w})
inside
\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}}}
.

Remark A.1
.

Suppose that
\widetilde{w}\in\widetilde{W}
is equal to
\widetilde{w}^{\prime}\delta
for some
\widetilde{w}^{\prime}\in W_{a}
and
\delta\in\Omega
. Since
\delta^{-*}
normalizes
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}
, we have
\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{w}}=\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{w}^{\prime}}\delta
.

Remark A.2
.

Note that we use the right quotient by Iwahori to define the affine flag variety
\mathrm{Fl}_{\mathbf{C}}
, as opposed to the left quotient in the body of this article. These conventions can be compared by taking inverse at the level of
L\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
. This also explains the difference between the embedding of the affine Weyl group
\widetilde{W}
into the loop group
L\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
in this appendix and the body of this article (which are differed by taking inverse).

Theorem A.3
.

The set of
T
-fixed points of
\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{w}}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{w}}^{T}=\{\widetilde{z}^{-*}\mid\widetilde{z}\in\widetilde{W},\widetilde{z}\leq w_{0}\widetilde{w}\}.

Proof.

We follow the proof of
[
BA
, Theorem 3.1]
with minor modifications.

By Remark
A.1
, it suffices to consider
\widetilde{w}\in W_{a}
. Let
w
be an element of
W^{\vee}
. By noting that
w\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{w}}=\mathrm{Fl}_{vw({\mathbf{a}}),\widetilde{w}}
,
\widetilde{z}^{-*}\in\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{w}}
if and only if
w\widetilde{z}^{-*}\in\mathrm{Fl}_{vw({\mathbf{a}}),\widetilde{w}}
. Thus, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\{\widetilde{z}^{-*}\mid\widetilde{z}\in\widetilde{W}^{+,\vee},\widetilde{z}\leq\widetilde{w}\}\subset\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{w}}^{T}.

Let
\widetilde{w}^{\prime}:={\mathcal{T}}(\widetilde{w})
. Recall that there exists a 6-dimensional affine subvariety
M^{\widetilde{w}^{\prime}}
inside
L^{+}\overline{U}_{4}
defined in
[
BA
, Appendix B.2]
. We define
M^{\widetilde{w}}:=M^{\widetilde{w}^{\prime}}\cap L^{+}\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
. By Lemma B.2 in
loc. cit.
, we have

\displaystyle M^{\widetilde{w}}\widetilde{w}^{-*}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}\subset\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{w}}.

Note that
M^{\widetilde{w}}\widetilde{w}^{-*}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}
is contained in the lower triangular Iwahori orbit, which is locally closed and affine by
[
GKM06
, Theorem 0.2]
. Since both are 4-dimensional affine spaces, they have to be the same. Moreover, since
\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{w}}
is also 4-dimensional,
M^{\widetilde{w}}\widetilde{w}^{-*}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}
is open (and dense) in
\mathrm{Fl}_{v{\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{w}}
.

For
\widetilde{z}\in\widetilde{W}
, there exists a neighborhood
U_{\widetilde{z}}=\widetilde{z}^{-*}L^{-}U{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}\subset\mathrm{Fl}_{\mathbf{C}}
such that
\widetilde{z}^{-*}
is in
\mathrm{Fl}_{vs,\widetilde{w}}
if and only if
U_{\widetilde{z}}\cap M^{\widetilde{w}}\widetilde{w}^{*}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}
is non-empty (see Lemma 3.1 in
loc. cit.
).

It remains to prove that the intersection
M^{\widetilde{w}}\widetilde{w}^{-*}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}\cap U_{\widetilde{z}}
is nonempty for
\widetilde{z}\in\widetilde{W}
such that
\widetilde{z}\leq\widetilde{w}
. Indeed, we prove a slightly stronger statement. Let
\widetilde{w}_{0}\in W_{a}
be the element corresponding to the highest restricted alcove. We show that

(A.4)

\displaystyle M^{\widetilde{w}_{0}}\widetilde{w}^{-*}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathbf{C}}\cap U_{\widetilde{z}}\neq\emptyset.

As explained in Appendix B.3 of
loc. cit.
(also, see Lemma B.1), this follows from a non-vanishing of certain matrix determinant. To explain this, we introduce some notation.

We view
\mathrm{Fl}_{\mathbf{C}}
as a subset of affine flags in a 4-dimensional
\mathbf{C}(\!(v)\!)
-vector space. Let
e_{1},\dots,e_{4}
be the standard basis of the
\mathbf{C}(\!(v)\!)
-vector space. For vectors
v_{1},\dots,v_{4}
, we let
\langle v_{1},\dots,v_{4}\rangle
be the
\mathbf{C}\llbracket v\rrbracket
-span of
v_{1},\dots,v_{4}
and
\langle v_{1},\dots,v_{4}\rangle_{v^{-1}}
be the
\mathbf{C}[1/v]
-span of
v_{1},\dots,v_{4}
. Then (
A.4
) holds if and only if

(A.5)

\displaystyle M^{\widetilde{w}_{0}}\widetilde{w}^{-*}\langle e_{1},\dots,e_{l},ve_{l+1},\dots,ve_{4}\rangle\cap\widetilde{z}^{-*}\langle v^{-1}e_{1},\dots,v^{-1}e_{l},e_{l+1},\dots,e_{4}\rangle_{v^{-1}}=0

for all
0\leq l\leq 3
. In our case, we can check these conditions directly.

We have

\displaystyle M^{\widetilde{w}_{0}}={\begin{pmatrix}1&&&\\
vc_{12}A_{21}&1&&\\
c_{13}A_{31}+vc_{123}A_{21}A_{32}&c_{23}A_{32}&1&\\
v(c_{14}A_{41}+c_{124}A_{21}A_{42}+c_{134}A_{31}A_{43})+v^{2}c_{1234}A_{21}A_{32}A_{43}&c_{24}A_{42}+vc_{234}A_{32}A_{43}&vc_{34}A_{43}&1\end{pmatrix}}

where the condition on
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
implies

\displaystyle c_{34}A_{43}

\displaystyle=-c_{12}A_{21}

\displaystyle c_{24}A_{42}

\displaystyle=c_{13}A_{31}

\displaystyle c_{123}A_{21}A_{32}

\displaystyle=c_{234}A_{32}A_{43}.

We can write down the matrix representation of
M^{\widetilde{w}_{0}}
with respect to
v^{k}e_{j}
for all
k\in\mathbf{Z}
,
1\leq j\leq 4
. However, to check (
A.5
), we only need to consider finitely many
v^{k}e_{j}
’s. Let
\omega_{l}=(1,\dots,1,0,\dots,0)
be the cocharacter of
\mathrm{GL}_{4}
whose first
l
entries are 1 and the remaining entries are 0. Note that
\widetilde{w}\langle e_{1},\dots,e_{l},ve_{l+1},\dots,ve_{4}\rangle
contains
v^{1-\widetilde{w}(\omega_{l})_{4}}\langle e_{1},\dots,e_{4}\rangle
which is stabilized by
M^{\widetilde{w}_{0}}
. Similarly,
\widetilde{z}^{-*}\langle v^{-1}e_{1},\dots,v^{-1}e_{l},e_{l+1},\dots,e_{4}\rangle_{v^{-1}}
contains
v^{-\widetilde{z}^{-*}(\omega_{l})_{1}}\langle e_{1},\dots,e_{4}\rangle_{v^{-1}}
. Thus, we can only consider
v^{k}e_{j}
’s whose image under
M^{\widetilde{w}_{0}}
is not contained in the union of
v^{1-\widetilde{w}(\omega_{l})_{4}}\langle e_{1},\dots,e_{4}\rangle
and
v^{-\widetilde{z}^{-*}(\omega_{l})_{1}}\langle e_{1},\dots,e_{4}\rangle_{v^{-1}}
, and there are only finitely many of them.

Now we compute a block matrix whose
ij
-th block, for
1\leq i\leq(1-\widetilde{w}(\omega_{l})_{4})-(-\widetilde{z}^{-*}(\omega_{l})_{1})-1
and
1\leq j\leq(1-\widetilde{w}(\omega_{l})_{4})-(1-\widetilde{w}(\omega_{l})_{1})
, is a matrix representation of
M^{\widetilde{w}_{0}}
with respect to the linearly independent vectors

\displaystyle v^{(1-\widetilde{w}(\omega_{l})_{1})+j-1}e_{1},\dots,v^{(1-\widetilde{w}(\omega_{l})_{1})+j-1}e_{r_{lij}}\ \ \text{in the domain}

\displaystyle v^{(-\widetilde{z}^{-*}(\omega_{l})_{1})+i}e_{1},\dots,v^{(-\widetilde{z}^{-*}(\omega_{l})_{1})+i}e_{s_{lij}}\ \ \text{in the codomain,}

where
r_{lij}
is the maximal integer such that
v^{(1-\widetilde{w}(\omega_{l})_{1})+j-1}e_{r_{lij}}\in\widetilde{w}\langle e_{1},\dots,e_{l},ve_{l+1},\dots,ve_{4}\rangle
and
s_{lij}
is the maximal integer such that
v^{(-\widetilde{z}^{-*}(\omega_{l})_{1})+i}e_{s_{lij}}\notin\widetilde{z}^{-*}\langle v^{-1}e_{1},\dots,v^{-1}e_{l},e_{l+1},\dots,e_{4}\rangle_{v^{-1}}
. Then (
A.5
) holds if and only if this block matrix has a trivial kernel after specializing all
A_{mn}
’s to some complex numbers satisfying the required relations.

For simplicity, we check this for
\widetilde{w}=\widetilde{w}_{0}
and
\widetilde{z}=e
, and the remaining cases will be a straightforward computation. When
l=0
, the block matrix is given by

\displaystyle\left(\begin{tabular}[]{c| c c c}0&1&0&0\\
$c_{12}A_{21}$&0&1&0\\
$c_{123}A_{21}A_{32}$&$c_{13}A_{31}$&$c_{23}A_{32}$&1\\
$c_{14}A_{41}+c_{124}A_{21}A_{42}+c_{134}A_{31}A_{43}$&0&$c_{24}A_{42}$&0\end{tabular}\right).

(Note that in this case and the cases
l=1,2
below, the first row of blocks is empty.) One can easily see that the determinant has a non-zero term
c_{14}A_{41}
(which is not affected by the condition imposed by
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
).

When
l=1
, the block matrix is given by

\displaystyle\left(\begin{tabular}[]{c|cc}0&1&\\
$c_{12}A_{21}$&0&1\\
$c_{123}A_{21}A_{32}$&$c_{13}A_{31}$&$c_{23}A_{32}$\end{tabular}\right)

whose determinant obvious does not vanish everywhere.

When
l=2
, the block matrix is given by

\displaystyle\left(\begin{tabular}[]{c|cc|ccc}0&1&0&&&\\
$c_{12}A_{21}$&0&1&&&\\
\hline\cr 0&0&0&1&0&0\\
0&$c_{12}A_{21}$&0&0&1&0\\
0&$c_{123}A_{21}A_{32}$&0&$c_{13}A_{31}$&$c_{23}A_{32}$&1\\
$c_{1234}A_{21}A_{32}A_{43}$&$c_{14}A_{41}+c_{124}A_{21}A_{42}+c_{134}A_{31}A_{43}$&$c_{234}A_{32}A_{43}$&0&$c_{24}A_{42}$&0\end{tabular}\right).

In this case, one can observe that the determinant has a non-zero monomial
c_{14}A_{41}
, which is not affected by the condition imposed by
\mathrm{GSp}_{4}
. Thus the determinant does not vanish everywhere.

Finally, when
l=3
, the block matrix is given by

\displaystyle\left(\begin{tabular}[]{cc|ccc}1&\lx@intercol\hfil 0\hfil\lx@intercol &&&\\
\hline\cr 0&0&1&&\\
$c_{12}A_{21}$&0&0&1&\\
$c_{123}A_{21}A_{32}$&0&$c_{13}A_{31}$&$c_{23}A_{32}$&1\\
$c_{14}A_{41}+c_{124}A_{21}A_{42}+c_{134}A_{31}A_{43}$&$c_{234}A_{32}A_{43}$&0&$c_{24}A_{42}$&0\end{tabular}\right)

and its determinant is equal to
-c_{234}A_{32}A_{43}
which does not vanish everywhere.
∎
